[日本語](ja/design.md)

# ondina design doc

> **ondina** — A harbor pilot. It shows the channel, but does not take the helm.
> Origin: A harbor pilot boards a ship entering unfamiliar waters and shows the channel, while the captain retains the decision and responsibility for steering. This is exactly the relationship between this tool and a reviewer randomly assigned to unfamiliar code. The spelling is Italian (from the harbor pilots Undine in ARIA).

Created: 2026-09-13 / Version: 0.1 (for starting development)
Scope: A review aid that helps reviewers understand large PRs and reach a state where they can Approve with confidence
Attachment: `ondina-prototype.html` (a working UI prototype; the source of truth for the screen specifications in this document)

---

## 0. Goals and non-goals

### Goal
Help the reviewer (myself) understand a PR and Approve with confidence.

Background:
- PRs exceeding 1000 lines arrive frequently. They are too much to follow by eye, cause mental fatigue, and take time away from my own implementation work
- Reviewers are randomly assigned and must start by understanding the task's requirements
- Reviewers need to check whether the requirements are met, as well as review the code in the PR itself
- AI coding increases both PR size and volume. Existing AI review tools only produce “review comments (answers)” and do not increase the reviewer's understanding
- PR descriptions are often pasted straight from AI output and cannot be trusted

### Non-goals (what this tool does not do)
- Review itself (judge correctness or point out bugs). The reviewer makes those judgments
- Enforce review conditions or rules. Do not gate Approve (these differ by project)
- Force authors to split PRs. Do not assume author cooperation
- Suggest code fixes

---

## 1. Design principles

1. **Subtract instead of adding**
   Review comments and summaries add “AI's understanding” on top of a diff. This tool removes “parts you do not need to read” from the diff and arranges the remainder in reading order. The reviewer reads the code itself, rather than AI prose. The amount to read drops from 1000 lines to 200; the reviewer builds the understanding.

2. **Limit AI judgments to those whose verification cost is far lower than their generation cost**
   - “This hunk mechanically follows the rename of X” → Can be checked in 5 seconds. OK
   - “The test corresponding to spec item 3 is testFoo” → Can be checked in 30 seconds. OK
   - “There is a race condition here” → Verification requires understanding the whole system. Do not produce this

3. **Programs handle facts; the LLM handles meaning. The LLM selects from candidates produced by the program instead of generating freely**
   Three stages: deterministic preprocessing → pass JSON to the LLM for one labeling call → the program validates and renders. Since 2026-10-03, after the program builds the reading order (courses), a second LLM call writes prose (the entry explanation and course guidance; 5.3). Neither call writes counts (unattributed hunks, the progress meter, and so on).

4. **Distinguish facts from inferences visually**
   Follow-ons confirmed as structurally identical by the AST are uniform (「一様」); LLM judgments are probably uniform (「たぶん一様」). Files that match no layer setting have a dotted inferred (「推定」) chip. LLM prose (the entry explanation and course guidance) has a dotted border and an AI (「AI」) chip; prose assembled from facts has a solid border and an automatic (「自動」) chip (2026-10-03. Decided by comparing examples. The chip says AI, rather than inferred). Blurring this distinction undermines trust.

5. **Show without judging**
   Always draw calls that skip layers as red dashed lines, but do not describe them as good or bad. Mark discrepancies between the author's description and the diff only as no diff (「差分なし」).

6. **Share viewing state across all views**
   The entry (「入口」), by purpose (「目的ごと」), course (「コース」), and diff list (「差分一覧」) views project the same data model. Pressing viewed (「見た」) in a course changes the percentage in by purpose and the file dot in the diff list at the same time.

7. **Match the look of the usual IDE**
   Catppuccin (4 flavors + accent), Inter + JetBrains Mono, IDEA-style semantic highlighting, and jumps to IDEA. Reviewing depends on sight and feel.

8. **Derive follow-on descriptions mechanically from the core**
   A follow-on group's pattern (for example, `getDiscount() → discount`) is derived from the removed and added symbols in the core hunk, rather than written as prose by a person or LLM. This structurally prevents discrepancies between the core diff and its follow-on description.

9. **Reproducibility**
   Produce the same map for the same PR and commit. Make a few LLM calls per PR. On reruns after a push, pass the previous How mapping as input and reassign only changed hunks (so How 2 does not turn into How 3 during a review).

---

## 2. Terminology

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Semantic hunk (hereafter hunk) | A diff at the level of a changed symbol (function, method, class, property, or top-level declaration). Git hunks are arbitrary units split by context lines, so tree-sitter splits them again |
| Core | A change that implements intent, such as domain logic, a new declaration, or a signature change |
| Follow-on | A structurally identical diff mechanically caused by a core change (such as a caller rename or adding a field to a DTO) |
| Follow-on group | A bundle of follow-ons arising from the same core. Display one representative + N remaining members + deviations. The UI calls the representative a sample (「サンプル」) and a deviation differs (「差異あり」) (2026-10-13. `docs/wording.md`) |
| Deviation | A hunk expected to belong to a follow-on group but whose shape differs from the pattern. A place a person should read. The UI says differs; a close deviation is a slight difference (「わずかな差異」) (2026-10-13) |
| Uniform / probably uniform | The source of the judgment that a follow-on group is structurally identical. An AST comparison confirms uniform; an LLM judgment is probably uniform |
| Unattributed | A hunk belonging to no How. A diff to examine when considering asking the author or splitting it into another PR. Always red |
| Entanglement | A hunk belonging to two or more Hows. A place where intents are mixed |
| How | A unit of means for realizing the PR's intent (Why). The level between the PR description and the code. Arranged in dependency order |
| Course (formerly tour) | Guidance for one How. Follow stops (places with diffs, from entry point to persistence; unchanged symbols have not been stops since 2026-10-11) in call order, visit each stop on one screen, then summarize (3.3. Renamed from tour on 2026-09-30. Until 2026-10-11, each stop had two screens: guidance → actual code) |
| Baseline | The state before the change. What randomly assigned reviewers lack most. Show unchanged counterparts through unchanged relationships (「差分なしの関係」) and a drawer from the right to fill this gap (3.3. Until 2026-10-11, unchanged symbols were stops) |
| Spec item (requirement (「要求」) in the UI) | “What should hold in this PR,” extracted from the ticket. As many as the ticket states (up to 10. 10.8. The definition was 3–5 until 2026-10-12, but fixture tickets had 6–8) |
| Claim | Each sentence in the author's PR description. Checked for support in the diff |

---

## 3. Screen specifications (UX)

Reference resolution: 1440×810 or higher (16:9). The prototype `ondina-prototype.html` is the source of truth. This document describes intent and behavior.

### 3.0 Shared shell

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ #PR [解析し直す] タイトル branch → base  [完了メーター…]  PR 一覧 P · 全体図 · 目的ごと · 差分一覧 D · 締め  ⌘K [解析中 #415 · 順番待ち 2] ⚙ │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ （知らせがあるときだけ）新しいコミットが 2 件あります（a41c09e → 3f9c2e1）    [解析し直す] │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                                                    │
│        画面（PR 一覧 / 入口 / 目的ごと / コース / 差分一覧 / 締め のいずれか。全幅）   │
│                                                                                    │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

The left rail (which listed screens as A–D) was removed on 2026-09-30. Navigation and settings moved to the top bar (decided by comparing examples. `packages/web/src/components/TopBar.tsx`).

- **Progress meter (「完了メーター」)** (the only permanent element): `帰属 n/N` `閲覧 n/N` `絡み k` `コース t/T` `仕様 s/S`. N is the hunk count (each member of a follow-on group counts individually). Green at 100%; otherwise the normal color, with incomplete attribution and spec coverage highlighted in red
- **Approve on GitHub (「GitHub で承認」)**: In the wrap-up (「締め」) (3.9). Always available (only opens the PR page in a new tab). No gating. The meter provides information, not conditions. Moved from the top bar to the wrap-up on 2026-10-03 (decided by comparing examples. Unviewed diffs enter the reviewer's sight at least once before approval)
- **Screen destinations**: Buttons in the top bar. PR list (「PR 一覧」) (3.11. Key `P`. Available even without an analysis. 2026-10-22), overview (「全体図」) (also shown at entry. 2026-10-28. Same as `Esc` during a course), return to course n (「コース n に戻る」) (only when visiting by purpose or the diff list while still in a course), by purpose (3.2), diff list (3.4. Key `D`), wrap-up (3.9. Left of `⌘K`. ⌘K and the gear are special buttons and stay at the far right). By purpose and wrap-up have no keys (accessible through `⌘K`). Startup opens the entry's explain tab (3.1. The order is explain first, then the map. 2026-11-04). If the URL names a screen, open that screen (“Screen URLs” below). With no analyses, open the PR list (3.11). Below 1360px, hide branch names and keep button names on one line. Below 1180px, use two rows and move the progress meter to the lower row. In the PR list, align navigation to the right in the same position as other screens; since the PR list uses one row, do not give the bar a two-row height even below 1180px (navigation positions differed between the PR list and other screens. 2026-10-28)
- **Screen URLs** (2026-11-04): Each screen has its own URL path. Reloading returns to the same screen, and the browser's back and forward work. Previously, startup read only `?pr=<key>`, and a reload went back to the entry (the PR's latest version). `packages/web/src/route.ts`
  - An analysis screen is `/pr/<number>/<first 7 characters of head>/<screen>`. A change without a PR is `/local/<first 7 characters of head>/<screen>`. The PR list is `/prs`
  - Screens: `explain` (entry's explain tab), `map` (map tab), `hows` (by purpose; `hows/<How number>` filters), `course/<course number>` (before-reading guide), `course/<number>/<n>` (stop n, counted from 1), `course/<number>/summary` (summary), `diff?file=<path>` (diff list with the selected file), `closing` (wrap-up). Outside the courses is number 0; it has no before-reading guide, so `course/0` opens the first unreviewed stop
  - `/` opens the latest analysis, and `/pr/<number>` and `/local` open that key's latest version, on the explain tab. After opening, the URL is rewritten to the screen's URL. An unmatched sha opens the PR's latest version. A missing course, stop, purpose, or file opens the explain tab. The `/?pr=<key>` URLs used until 2026-11-04 are read as `/pr/<key>`. The CLI prints `/pr/<number>` or `/local`
  - History: add an entry when the analysis, version, screen kind, or course changes. Moves within one screen (stop, file, filter) rewrite the current entry. Showing a screen at startup or on back / forward adds nothing. `App.tsx` rewrites the URL; the reducer's `route.go` moves the screen
- **Running jobs (「動いている処理」)** (2026-10-22): When serve's analysis queue (5.5) has queued or running work, show a spinner and analyzing #415 · queued 2 (「解析中 #415 · 順番待ち 2」) to the left of the gear. If AI review (3.11) is running, also show AI reviews running n (「AI レビュー中 n」) (added the same day; the heading changed from running analyses (「動いている解析」)). Press to open the PR list (3.11). Hide when nothing is running. In the PR list, hide the open analysis's title, branch, meter, and the row under the bar (the PR list is not a view of the open analysis). `JobsChip` in `TopBar.tsx`
- **Screen width** (2026-10-13): Shrinks with the window. Previously, the whole screen had a minimum width of 1180px, so a narrower window clipped the right side and made top-bar buttons inaccessible. Keep the appearance at 1180px and above. Rules below 1180px are grouped in one place at the end of `styles.css`: two-row top bar; columns shrink with `clamp` (three by purpose columns in 3.2, the diff list's file list in 3.4, and the course's left guidance in 3.3); column headings keep their names and move descriptions and buttons to the next line; the ask the guide (「案内人に聞く」) drawer overlays the screen (3.10). The lower bound is 700px (fits half of a 1440px screen; narrower widths make the top bar three rows). Scroll horizontally below that
- **Settings (「設定」)**: The top-bar gear opens a centered modal (fairly wide; vertically stack each item's heading + field + one-line explanation). Tabs: general (「全般」) (theme (follow system (「システムに合わせる」) / light (「ライト」) / dark (「ダーク」), with separate light and dark flavors; 3.5), worktree location, LLM command (3.8)) and shortcut keys (「ショートカットキー」) (18.2. 2026-10-20). Theme and shortcut keys change immediately; other settings take effect on the next `analyze`. Opens and fully works without an analysis (2026-10-11). Settings live in one place across PRs (`.ondina/ui.json`. 3.8). `components/SettingsModal.tsx`
- **New-commit notice (one row under the bar)** (2026-10-13): When commits are added to a PR, show one row below the top bar so the user can rerun analysis from the UI. Previously, the only option was to rerun `analyze` in the CLI (some environments do not allow copy and paste). Hide when there is nothing to report. Placement, notification, and switching behavior were decided by comparing working examples (notify when found; notify and wait; one row under the bar. Rejected starting analysis without a press and switching automatically when finished). `components/ReanalyzeStrip.tsx`; `stripOf` in `reanalyze.ts` determines the content
  - When to check: Ask serve whether a newer head than the current version exists when opening an analysis, returning to the screen, and every 2 minutes while open (`GET /api/latest`. 5.5). Use gh for PRs; for `local`, read the head ref with git. Show nothing if retrieval fails
  - Found: 2 new commits are available (a41c09e → 3f9c2e1) (「新しいコミットが 2 件あります（a41c09e → 3f9c2e1）」) and [reanalyze (「解析し直す」)]. Count PR commits after the current version. Omit the count if rewritten history prevents counting. The button remains available while another PR is being analyzed; pressing it queues the analysis and shows analysis of PR #7 is queued (1 ahead) (「PR #7 の解析は順番待ちです（前に 1 件）」) (serve runs only one at a time, with the rest queued. 5.5. Until 2026-10-22, the button was unavailable until completion)
  - Analyzing: Spinner, analyzing 1:07 (「解析中 1:07」), latest progress (the start of each `analyze` stage and a line while waiting for the language server; 5.5), you can keep using the previous version (「前のバージョンはこのまま使えます」). While waiting, users can read the previous version, mark no issues (「問題なし」), and write memos
  - Ready: Analysis of new version 3f9c2e1 is ready. 3 diffs changed (「新しいバージョン 3f9c2e1 の解析ができました。変わった差分は 3 か所です」) and [switch (「切り替える」)]. Keep the previous version until pressed (do not replace code during reading). Show the same row for versions already analyzed through the CLI
  - Switched: Switched to new version 3f9c2e1. Reset 3 changed diffs to unviewed (also reset courses 3 and 6 to incomplete). 2 memos remain on the previous version (…) (「新しいバージョン 3f9c2e1 に切り替えました。変わった差分 3 か所を未確認に戻しました（コース 3・6 も未完了に戻しました）。前のバージョンに残したメモが 2 件あります（…）」) and [×]. Content comes from the new version's `ReviewState.carried` (chapter 4); × saves it with `dismissed` set
  - Failed: Could not analyze: reason (「解析できませんでした: 理由」), you can keep using the previous version (「前のバージョンのまま使えます」), and [retry (「もう一度」)]
  - **[Re-analyze (「解析し直す」)] in the top bar** (2026-11-04): re-analyzes the open version at any time, even without new commits. Use it after changing settings (LLM command, ticket source), or to rebuild an analysis made by an older ondina or one whose LLM step failed. It sits right of the version at the upper left and is hidden on the PR list. It is disabled while that PR is queued or being analyzed. It uses the same arguments as the strip (that version's arguments, 5.5), so if the PR has new commits it analyzes the new version and shows "ready". With the same head, it reloads the current analysis when done (keeping the screen and selected file) and shows 「b568cfd を解析し直して、表示を新しい解析に入れ替えました。」 with [×]
  - Opening another version of the same PR (switching or selecting at the upper left, and reloading the same version after re-analyzing it) preserves the current screen, selected file, and purpose filter (if present in the new version). In a course, go to its first unviewed stop. A different PR still opens at entry
  - **Carryover**: When opening a new version for the first time, serve builds state from the previous version (5.5). Preserve no issues only for hunks whose id and `lines` both match; reset changed diffs to unviewed (rejected clearing everything). Completed courses stay complete if all their diffs remain marked no issues in the new version. Memos and AI drafts are covered in chapter 12
- **`Esc`**: Close overlays in order (keymap → palette → settings → ask the guide drawer (3.10) → map (drawer or large) → peek → by purpose filter), then return to entry. In the PR list (3.11), do nothing after closing closable elements (including the PR-details drawer). By purpose, diff list, and wrap-up return to the course if entered from one (`Esc` after visiting the diff list from wrap-up also returns to the course, not wrap-up. Return to wrap-up through the top bar). At entry, deselect the course
- **Keyboard**: On the course screen, `Enter` / `→` advance (no issues · next (「問題なし · 次へ」) at stops), `←` goes back, `j` / `k` move to the next / previous stop (without marking no issues), `M` opens the map (`Shift+M` enlarges), `R` opens unchanged counterparts (`Shift+R` enlarges), `F` shows the full file, `B` collapses the right pilot-guidance column, and `Esc` closes drawer (map or unchanged counterparts) → peek → leaves the course (3.3). `Q` asks the guide on any screen (3.10)

### 3.1 Entry (overview) and explain tab

Goal: Avoid overwhelming users at entry. Briefly convey “this is the problem, and this PR solves it this way.” Show the PR's overview on one map and invite users in through the course sequence (demo approved on 2026-09-29. Implemented on 2026-09-30 in `packages/web/src/components/HomeView.tsx`).

Screen structure (top to bottom):
- Header: explain (「説明」) `1` / map (「地図」) `2` tabs (swapped on 2026-11-04; before that the map came first and opened at startup), title, count chips (diffs n · courses n · outside n (「差分 n · コース n · 外 n」)), a bar showing each course's proportion of diffs (count hunks by their first How; color = course), and a start button (selected course; otherwise the first incomplete course; if all complete, to wrap-up (「締めへ」) (3.9); if there are no courses and only outside-course diffs, open those. `Enter`)
- Notice that the LLM did not run (`Analysis.llm.status` is `skipped` / `failed`. If the first call is `ok` and the second call's `Analysis.llmText.status` is `skipped` / `failed`, show could not write guidance with the LLM. Reason: … (「LLM で案内文を書けませんでした。理由: …」). The second-call bar appears only at entry. 2026-10-03): One-row bar under the title, on both map and explain tabs. Use polite Japanese (could not separate by purpose with the LLM. Reason: … (「LLM で目的ごとに分けられませんでした。理由: …」)). The configure LLM command (「LLM コマンドを設定する」) button opens settings and explains rerunning with `--relabel` (decided 2026-09-30. `components/LlmNotice.tsx`. The same component also tops the by purpose screen)
- Map tab: **a request-flow map** (2026-11-05. Approved with the flow5 mockup. `components/FlowMap.tsx`; steps, boxes, layout and code come from `map/flowmap/`. Flows come from `Analysis.flows`, chapter 4). The map is about the request flow, not about boxes per course. At the top: the flow's name (with two or more flows, click to pick one; an AI title has a dotted underline; command `map.flow`) and the stage switch: **① Background** (the flow before the change = only the calls that existed before) / **② This PR** (every call; added = green, changed = yellow, same = gray, removed = red) / **③ After merge** (only the calls that exist after the change). Switch with the buttons or `n` (the same key as the explain tab's stages; on the map tab it cycles ① → ② → ③ → ①; command `map.stage`). ② shows call counts (added, changed, same, removed) above the map. Layers are horizontal bands from top to bottom (dashed borders, the band name at the top left; inferred layers say 「（推定）」). A box is a class (for a function, the class that holds it); its lines are the members this flow calls. Boxes are placed with elkjs layered (layers fixed by partition, orthogonal lines, 100px between boxes, 70px between layers, 9px between parallel lines). The layout is built once per flow, so boxes stay put when the stage changes. When it does not fit, the map scrolls instead of shrinking. Calls are solid lines; returns are dotted lines along the same path in reverse, offset by 9px. The gray dot at the top is the caller. Steps go "call → nested calls → return"; only the current step's line is thick, past lines are faint, future lines are hidden. Past calls get a numbered dot; the current step gets a label with the call expression (a return shows 「↩ return type」). A ball runs along the current step's line (calls from the caller, returns from the callee) and the box it reaches gets a frame. In ②, boxes carry 「新しい / 変わった / 差分なし」 and member colors (added members get 「＋」, removed ones a strikethrough). A class not called in the current stage is faint with 「この段では呼ばれません」; a class that is new shows 「まだありません」 in ①. Below the map, the playback bar: ▶ (plays from the step on screen; from the start when at the end), back / next (`←` / `→`), the step bar (status colors in ②, returns are short, click to jump), n / N, and the speed slider (0.25–3×, `mapSpeed` in `.ondina/ui.json`). Under it, the current step (caller → callee, the call expression, and the calling line: ① shows the line before the change, ③ the line after, ② the hunk's removed and added lines with the call highlighted when the line is in the diff; the entry shows the first 4 lines of its hunk; 「差分一覧で開く」 opens the caller's hunk) and a side panel (① the classes in this flow and the classes not called before the change / ② **changes outside the flow** = diffs that no flow's calls touch, one row per file, one row per follow-on group, tests as a count; click to open that place in the diff list. The diff is the unit of responsibility, so every diff can be reached from the map / ③ the difference from before = call counts). Switching stages stays on the same call in the same direction (or its parent call when the new stage does not have it). Moving to a step scrolls the map so the current box is visible. **Selecting a course narrows the map to that course** (the cards below, `home.select`): the stage becomes ②, only that course's calls and their returns are colored and the rest is gray. Next, back and play stop only at that course's steps and stop at its last step. The side panel lists 「コース n で変わる所」 (click to jump) and that course's changes outside the flow. When no flow has been picked, the first flow the course passes through is shown. `Esc` clears the course (staying on the current step). Stage, flow, step and playing live in `AppState.home.mapStage` / `flow` / `step` / `play` (not saved). `Enter` on the home screen still enters the selected course. Analyses without flows (old analyses, and PRs with no calls between changed functions) show the previous map (every changed place, with the selected course's route; `map/route.ts`) and a notice saying why. Below the stage switch, the stage's one or two sentences (a guess written by the second LLM call: dotted frame and an "AI" chip, a line break at each full stop. `CallFlow.stages`. Not shown at ② inside a course scope, which shows the course's change count instead). There is no per-step narration (the step pane shows only code). The course's pre-reading guidance also shows the flow map narrowed to that course (3.3)
- Explain tab: Contents of the table below. The left box has two stages: 1 background (「1 背景」) and 2 this PR (「2 この PR」) (2026-10-29. Comparing examples led to separating background into stage 1. The team reviews everyone's PRs without knowing the project background, so understanding that background is the largest bottleneck. `components/ExplainStages.tsx`). Headings are reader questions (what is this PR for? (「何のための PR？」) / what project is it part of? (「どんな案件の一部？」) / what existed before this PR? (「この PR の前からあるものは？」) / what do these terms mean? (「出てくる言葉は？」) / what changes when used? (「使うと何が変わる？」) / where did things change? (「どこを変えた？」)). Switch stages with the buttons above or key `n` (`home.stage`. Temporary entry state, not saved. Start from background whenever an analysis opens). Below background: 2 to this PR → (「2 この PR へ →」). Text comes from the second LLM call (5.3), so boxes have dotted borders and headings have AI chips (principle 4). The “why” copied from the ticket uses a solid quote border and original text of ticket SHOP-421 (「チケット SHOP-421 の原文」). If empty, show no explanation (「説明はありません」). Analyses in the pre-2026-10-29 format (problem, approach, untouched parts, before/after table) show this analysis uses the previous explanation format. Reanalyze to… (「この解析は前の形の説明です。解析し直すと…」) (those four fields were used from 2026-10-03 to 10-29. The demo's handwritten `OV` was the target prose form)
- Course sequence (always below): Cards (stop count, diff count, proportion marked no issues; green border when complete). Press to select (shows the route; press the same card again / `Esc` to deselect); enter (「入る」) on the selected card opens the course
- The entry tab and selected course are temporary screen state (`AppState.home`. Not saved)

| Element | Content | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 1 background: what is this PR for? | One-sentence gist (what this PR does) and a “why” sentence copied from the ticket (without a ticket, explain `--ticket`; with a ticket but no “why,” say so) | The gist is from the second LLM call (diffs and courses as input. **Do not use the PR description**. 10.4). Requirement extraction (10.8) copies the “why”; the program checks that it appears in the original |
| 1 background: what project is it part of? | Quote from the parent ticket (solid quote border with original text of parent ticket SHOP-400 (「親チケット SHOP-400 の原文」) chip; bold title links to Jira, followed by the purpose sentence copied from the parent's body) and a PRs in the same project (「同じ案件の PR」) row (2026-10-29). Each PR has a status chip (`マージ済み` / `レビュー中` / `Draft` / `クローズ`; Draft matches the PR list) and `#40 題` (GitHub link). This PR is bold with a `この PR` chip (solid accent; dotted lines denote AI, so do not use them). A PR in another repository is `mail-templates#12`. If none are found: no PRs in the same project found. Searched PR titles and bodies using 4 parent and child ticket numbers (「同じ案件の PR は見つかりませんでした。親と子のチケットの番号 4 個で、PR の題と本文を探しました。」). Reasons for numbers skipped because there were too many, or items not retrieved, each get one gray line. Omit the whole question if there is no parent or the ticket is not Jira | The program retrieves parent tickets, tickets under the same parent, and PRs in the same project as facts from Jira and GitHub (5.1. `packages/cli/src/project.ts`. 2026-10-29). Requirement extraction (10.8) copies the purpose sentence from the parent's body; the program checks it against the original |
| 1 background: what existed before this PR? | In 1–3 rows, things the diff uses but does not create, and things removed by this PR (2026-10-29). Each row: “code name (`DiscountPolicy.rateFor()`; for the second item with the same owner onward, just names: `Order.promo · getDiscount()`) / one sentence / factual chip (`この PR で変更なし`, or pink `この PR で削除` for removals. Changed from the example's ‘unchanged’ (「変えていない」) and ‘removed’ (「消える」) to match the noun-ending chip rule in `docs/wording.md`).” End with a row for layers in the diagram's boxes (layer names under the boxes in order of appearance, if two or more. `層 4 つ` / API · Application · Domain · Infrastructure. Layer names attached to the boxes in “this PR.” / `設定ファイルの分け方`, or dotted `分け方は推定` for inferred layers). Omit the whole question if empty | The program produces candidates (unchanged counterparts called by stops = out in `related` in 10.4, and declarations only in base = `core/src/hunks/removed.ts`. Also inspect primary-constructor `val` / `var`). The second LLM call selects candidates and writes one sentence. The program checks that names are candidates and whether they are removed (5.4, item 9). The UI builds the layer row from `Symbol.layer` / `layerProvenance` |
| 1 background: what do these terms mean? | Inferred meanings (「意味は推定」) chip in the heading. Up to 4 items in a two-column grid. “Term (bold) / code name / one sentence of meaning” (example: subtotal `subtotal` the sum of item amounts before discounts). Omit the whole question if empty | The second LLM call writes terms and meanings. The program checks that code names are identifiers in diff lines (5.4, item 9) |
| 2 this PR: what changes when used? | 1–2 sentences using concrete test values (example: subtotal 1000 with SPRING10 → total 900), with a chip citing the values: values from OrderTest.kt lines 15–17 (「値は OrderTest.kt 15〜17 行」) | The second LLM call. The program checks that cited lines appear in stop diffs |
| 2 this PR: where did things change? | Flow diagram. Box = 1–3 courses (title, layer chip (dotted if inferred), course circles (press for `home.select`), name of the central stop); before and after to the right; dotted tags for unchanged parts; values passed onward on arrows between boxes. Courses included in no box appear below as circles under courses outside the diagram (「図にないコース」) | The second LLM call (box sequence and prose). The program checks that courses exist and appear only once, and that names belong to stops in the box's courses (5.4, item 9) |
| Specification (「仕様」) | The ticket number (link), title, type, status, and body specified by `--ticket`, unchanged (2026-10-12). If retrieval fails, show why. Without `--ticket`, show the ticket selected by the LLM from the PR title, branch name, and body (inferred; a dotted border includes where it was found and unselected candidates. Say if none was found). Requirements extracted from the retrieved ticket (dotted border and AI), with corresponding course circles per row. A requirement with no match has a red no corresponding course (「対応するコースなし」) chip. Fold the original text below the requirements (2026-10-12) | Original ticket (Jira / command / file. 5.1. Does not pass through the LLM). For ticket selection, the program collects candidates and the LLM selects (10.7). Requirements and mappings: LLM (extract; select from Hows) + program (retain only existing Hows; count. 10.8) |
| Author's explanation (「作者の説明」) | Display the original “as claims.” Mark sentences unsupported by the diff, with a no diff flag and reason below | LLM (judgment). Do not modify the original |

Implementation note (2026-09-19. `packages/web/src/components/ExplainView.tsx`): The original is `Analysis.pr.body` (`analyze` saves the PR body or `--body`). For entries in `claims` with `backed: false`, find their `text` in the original by **exact match** and mark it (`packages/web/src/claims.ts`). Slight rewriting by the LLM prevents a match, so unmatched sentences appear separately as sentences whose location in the body could not be identified (「本文中に該当箇所を特定できなかった文」) (the UI makes the failed approximation visible). Render the body as Markdown (2026-10-10. `packages/web/src/authorBody.ts`). Match GitHub: support GFM (tables, task lists, strikethrough, autolinks) and filter raw HTML with the same rules as GitHub (rehype-sanitize defaults). Hide HTML comments (PR-template writing instructions). Apply markers to the rendered syntax tree (HAST). Get claim ranges from original offsets and compare them with source positions held by text nodes (claims are copied from the original, so include symbols such as `` ` ``). Wrap inline elements wholly inside a range (such as code) in one `<mark>`; do not wrap paragraphs, lists, or tables. Sentences found in the original but not displayable (such as in comments) also go to the unidentified-sentences box. `analyze` colors code blocks with the same Shiki role tokens as diffs and stores them in `Analysis.pr.bodyCode` (matched by language + content. `packages/core/src/highlight/body.ts`); the UI uses the same colors as diffs (no colors for old analyses without `bodyCode` or languages unsupported by Shiki). Render mermaid blocks as diagrams in the UI (load `mermaid` only when opening a body containing diagrams. `securityLevel: 'strict'`; default theme for light flavors, dark theme for dark flavors). If rendering fails, show the original and reason. Open links in new tabs; if an image cannot load (GitHub Enterprise Server images require login), use an image: alt text (「画像: 代替テキスト」) link. For old analyses without a saved body, list `claims` one sentence per line.

Implementation note (2026-10-12. Specification box. `ExplainView.tsx` and `packages/web/src/ticketBody.ts`): Render `Analysis.pr.ticket` (5.1). If retrieved, put a ticket (「チケット」) chip and ticket number (link to `url`, new tab) in the heading; start the body with title, type and status chips, and retrieved from Jira at 10/12 14:03 (「Jira から 10/12 14:03 に取得」) (retrieval may also be through command / file). Render by `format`: parse `html` (Jira's HTML-converted body) as HTML; render `markdown` (command / file) with the author's-explanation rules. Filter both with the same rules as the author's explanation (rehype-sanitize defaults) and resolve relative links and images to absolute URLs against `url`. If images cannot load (Jira attachments require login), use an image: alt text link. Keep `text` (when Jira returns no HTML) as plain text with line breaks. Solid border because the original does not pass through the LLM. If retrieval fails (`error`), show could not retrieve SHOP-421 (「SHOP-421 を取れませんでした」) and the reason in a red-bordered box. Without `ticket`, show ticket unspecified (「チケット未指定」) and `--ticket` usage. Requirements (`specs`) are covered in the next implementation note

Implementation note (2026-10-12. Tickets found in title, branch name, or body. `TicketGuess` / `TicketNotFound` in `ExplainView.tsx` and `packages/web/src/ticketPlace.ts`): Render when `Analysis.pr.ticketSearch` (5.1 / 10.7) exists. If the LLM selected one (`chosen`), add an inferred chip to the heading and a dotted box at the start of the body: inferred, probably this PR's ticket (「たぶん、この PR のチケット」), where found (title / branch name / body line N, and a quote of that line. Collected string has a purple background. Trim long lines to 30 characters before and 50 after, with …), unselected candidates (numbers and locations), and if incorrect, add --ticket to analyze to retrieve that ticket (「違うときは analyze に --ticket を付けると、そのチケットを取ります」). Below is the same as stage 1 (retrieved body / retrieval failure reason). Without a selection, show no ticket found (「チケットは見つかりませんでした」) (no candidates: no ticket numbers or URLs in the PR title, branch name, or body (「PR の題・ブランチ名・本文に、チケットの番号や URL がありませんでした」) / LLM answered none: strings resembling numbers or URLs were present, but the LLM judged them not to be this PR's ticket (「番号や URL の形の文字列はありましたが、LLM がこの PR のチケットではないと判断しました」)), or no ticket selected (「チケットを選んでいません」) (LLM unused or failed; include reason), found candidates, and `--ticket` guidance. Decision from examples (2026-10-12): select one if there are multiple candidates and list the others with locations (rejected retrieving all or selecting none)

Implementation note (2026-10-12. Requirements extracted from tickets. `Requirements` / `SpecNotice` in `ExplainView.tsx`): Render `Analysis.specs` and `Analysis.llmSpecs` (10.8). Decision from examples (2026-10-12): **course circles** on the right of each row (same colors and numbers as the cards below and map circles. `button.cb.cc<n>`). Press to select the course (same `home.select` as pressing a card; keep the explain tab. Select the card below and change the start button to enter course n (「コース n に入る」)). Hover: course n: title (press to select the course below) (「コース n: 題（押すと下のコースを選ぶ）」); ring the selected course's circle (`aria-pressed`). Rejected the previous textual “How 3” chip because it used another name for what the card immediately below calls “course 3.”
- At least one requirement (`llmSpecs.status` is `ok`): Dotted box with AI · requirements extracted from ticket · right = corresponding courses (selected by LLM; absent ones in red) (「AI · チケットから抜き出した要求 · 右 = 対応するコース（LLM が選び、ないものは赤）」), followed by numbered prose-and-circle rows. Unmatched rows have a red no corresponding course chip (a fact that the LLM selected no How, not a judgment; principle 1). Below: ▸ original ticket <title> (「▸ チケットの原文 <題>」) button (command `home.ticket`. `AppState.home.ticket`. Not saved; folds on analysis reload). Opens the same original as stage 1 (title, chips, body)
- Extraction failed (`failed`): Start with could not extract requirements (「要求を抜き出せませんでした」) and reason, showing original ticket as is (「チケットの原文をそのまま出しています」), and run analyze again to re-extract (「もう一度 analyze すると抜き出し直します」); keep the original open (no button). LLM answered zero: no requirements found in the ticket (「チケットに要求を見つけませんでした」), LLM answered 0 (「LLM が 0 件と答えました」), and explain that `--relabel` re-extracts (the answer is saved, so plain analyze gives the same result)
- No `llmSpecs` (no ticket, retrieval failed, first LLM call did not run, or old analysis): Same as stage 1 (original only)
- Same for tickets selected from title / body (`ticketSearch.chosen`). The dotted probably this PR's ticket box comes first, followed by requirements
- Count specification n/N (「仕様 n/N」) in the top bar (3.7) from `specs` (red when incomplete)

### 3.2 By purpose (How decomposition)

Goal: Insert one level before looking directly at code. PR explanation → Hows x, y, z that realize it → code.

Open with by purpose in the top bar (2026-09-30. Previously opened from B in the left rail).

Three columns: **How (dependency order)** | **Diffs (hunk list)** | **Preview**

How cards:
- Number, heading (short imperative), one-line “why,” `核 n` `追従 n` `テスト n` `見た p%`, progress bar
- Click to select. Unrelated hunks dim while selected (opacity 0.26). Click again to deselect
- Selecting a hunk highlights the How cards it belongs to (bidirectional)

Hunk-list rows:
- `path › symbol`, How chip (number), kind chip (core / follow-on / test / unattributed), viewing toggle
- Rows belonging to two or more Hows have an `絡み` chip
- Unattributed rows have a red background. Always show `未帰属 n` in the list header
- Follow-on-group row: `N ファイル › 追従 N件`; second line: `パターン · 一様（AST判定）|たぶん一様（LLM判定） · 差異あり n · わずかな差異 n` (close deviation = deviation whose distance in chapter 8 is at or below the threshold; places token-sequence comparison cannot settle); right edge: `見た k/N` (count, not toggle). Click or `Enter` expands to individual rows: representative (chip `サンプル`) → deviations (chip `差異あり`; red if close; omit word counts. 2026-10-13) → members (path order), each with its own viewing toggle. **Viewing the group row or representative does not mark members viewed** (check each individually. Decided 2026-09-19). Selecting the group row = selecting the representative
- Order: How id order (= dependency order), with each How's hunks ordered core → follow-on → test, and path order within each kind. Hunks belonging to two or more Hows appear once under their first How. Hunks belonging to none go last. `j` / `k` follow this order (while filtered, skip unrelated rows; a collapsed group contributes only its representative, an expanded group each member in turn). `Esc` clears the filter (implementation: `packages/web/src/hows.ts`, 2026-09-19)

Preview column:
- **The selected hunk's entire file** (2026-10-08. Decided by comparing examples; subdividing the file further made reading harder). Embed all of the file's hunks in the full head text (`Analysis.files`) and show lines outside hunks (`buildFileRows` in `diff/rows.ts`. Same counting as `fileRows` on the course screen). Insert each hunk's header (same `@@ symbol`, course circles, kind, and no issues as 3.4) immediately before it; a purple line marks the selected hunk's header and the left of its line numbers. Selecting a hunk (list row, `j` / `k`, or in-file header) scrolls its header to the top of the preview (hunks near the file end stop at the end). Selecting another hunk in the same file scrolls without reopening; smooth for up to two screenfuls, instant for longer distances or a different file. Full file in both split and unified modes. Fold lines far from diffs into the same strips as the diff list (3.4), with show all (「すべて表示」) `F` on the strip's left (2026-10-13). For old analyses without full text, show all file hunks and the full file is not in this analysis (rerun analyze to display it) (「ファイル全体は解析にありません（analyze を実行し直すと表示されます）」). Deleted files (no full head text) show hunks only (which are the whole file). Leave the diff list (3.4) and course (3.3) screens as they are
- **The preview column has variable width; the hunk list caps at 360px** (`296px minmax(0,360px) minmax(430px,1fr)`). Extra window width goes to the diff (1052px at 1920px). At minimum width 1180px, retain the previous 430px diff and 242px hunk list (2026-09-21. Previously the hunk list grew with `1fr` while the diff stayed at 430px). Below 1180px, all three columns shrink with the window and the diff gets the remainder (`clamp(190px,25vw,296px) clamp(200px,30vw,360px) minmax(0,1fr)`. 2026-10-13. Screen width in 3.0)
- Default to split view (2026-10-11. Previously unified was the default to work in narrow windows. Split halves are narrow, so wrap long lines; split view in 3.4). Toggle split/unified (`s` / `u`; stored separately from the diff list in `UiSettings.diffMode.b` (3.8; across PRs. Until 2026-10-11, stored in each PR's `ReviewState`))
- Header includes How chips, kind, entanglement explanation (`絡み · How 1 と 3 の両方が触る`), unattributed explanation (`どの How にも入らない差分です。作者に聞くか、別の PR に分けるかを考えるときに見ます。`), and group-hunk notes such as `同じ書き換え n 件のサンプルです。残りは展開して 1 件ずつ確認できます`
- `IDEA で開く` (3.6), `見た`

Facts the by purpose screen guarantees:
1. Every hunk belongs to some How (those that do not are shown as unattributed)
2. Hunks belonging to two or more Hows are shown as entanglement

### 3.3 Courses

**Requirement course (2026-11-09).** When `Analysis.chapters` exists, course entry opens a separate `RequirementCourseView` (`/req/start`, `/req/<zero-based chapter index>`, `/req/end`); older analyses keep the How course. Three columns show chapters, all files in `githubDiff` order, and Files changed with Viewed. File names appear first, with dimmed directories beneath and full paths in titles. Local analyses have no GitHub metadata, so they use the existing file order and allow explicitly selected hunk lines, as other local memo screens do. Definition jumps reuse the existing drawer. Current-chapter hunks open in file line order, other hunks fold; navigation follows `chapter.hunkIds`. Overlapping requirements share viewed marks and display every chapter chip. Entering a chapter focuses `chapter.hunkIds[0]` even when viewed, and scrolls its header into view. The current hunk has an outline and 「いまここ」. Written intent quotes its source, guesses use a dotted border and AI label, and missing intent shows nothing. Follow members use their representative's intent for question drafts, with a one-line guide. 「わかった」 and saved 「聞く」 add the hunk to `ReviewState.viewed`; undo and file unview go through the existing GitHub Viewed sync. Understanding or saving a question advances to the next unviewed hunk in reading order, returning to earlier skipped hunks if needed. Once all are viewed, focus stays and a short completion notice links to the next chapter (also →). Saving an opinion stays in place. Questions and opinions save reviewer drafts with `label`. Reviewers select GitHub-commentable lines in that hunk using the existing diff “+” and drag; no lines are chosen automatically, and saving stays disabled until selected. Enter marks the current hunk, j/k or ↓/↑ move hunks, →/← move chapters. The start page shows the project, ticket reason, what changes in this PR, and the requirement-to-diff correspondence; AI-written headlines and examples use dotted boxes and AI chips. It uses `background.project`, `summary.headline`, the ticket quotes in `background.why`, and `summary.example`; the program computes requirement counts and diff presence from `chapters`. The end page shows file-level Viewed and remaining counts computed from all hunks, counts and bodies of active reviewer questions and opinions, requirements with no hunks, and the closing link. Remaining-file and comment rows use `req.goHunk` to select the hunk in the first chapter that contains it; remaining files open their first unviewed hunk in file line order. Empty requirements show their text and no action buttons. The F key (`file.full` on this screen) or the banner button narrows the view to this chapter’s hunks as cards in `chapter.hunkIds` reading order, with code on the left and the guide on the right. Narrowing survives chapter moves, is not saved, and ends when navigating to a file through Files changed or ]/[. Switching uses a View Transition with the same name per hunk, keeps the banner and side columns static and the current hunk at the same height, and happens instantly with reduced motion enabled. The map sits on the start page ("Flow and requirements") and under the banner of each requirement chapter (2026-11-11; `RequirementMap` in `RequirementCourseView.tsx` uses a small form of the entry map `FlowMap`). It shows stage 2 (this PR) only, with no stage buttons, code pane or side pane. On a chapter's map, a box is dark when its symbol's `hunkId` is in the chapter's `hunkIds`; a line is dark when the hunk of its calling line is in the chapter or the called box is dark; the program decides this (`requirementMap.ts`), and the rest is faded. The play strip (play / pause, previous, next) stops only at dark lines and moves through `map.prev` / `map.next` (no keys); the arrow keys always move between chapters on this screen. Pressing a dark box jumps to its diff (`req.focus`). Under the map, "Outside the map: ..." lists the symbols of the chapter's diffs that are not on the flow. A chapter without a map shows one sentence for one of three reasons (no diff found for the requirement, the analysis has no flow, the chapter's diffs are not on the flow); the reason is never guessed. The start page map shows everything in stage 2 colors and puts a chip on each box for the requirements that change it (pressing it opens that chapter). "Pin the map" (`req.pin`, not saved) sticks the map above the center column. Approve remains available without a gate.


Goal: Guide one How as one course, beyond merely spreading out a map. Guidance begins on entry; each stop shows why it is here, what changed, and the code, followed by a summary after the round (2026-09-26. The clickable demo of 2026-09-29 is the screen source of truth; newer than `docs/prototype.html`). **Visit only diffs** (2026-10-11. A diff is the unit of responsibility. Even when AI makes reading efficient, the person who Approves is responsible for every diff. For unchanged related files, knowing the relationship is enough; do not treat them like changed files). Show the baseline lacking in random assignments (unchanged callers and callees) through an unchanged-relationships row and a drawer from the right, rather than stops (below).

Implementation: `packages/web/src/course.ts` (course and stop information; pure functions), `components/CourseView.tsx` (screen), `components/StopColumn.tsx` (right column of stop screen; 2026-10-20), `map/CourseMap.tsx` (map), `diff/fileRows.ts` / `FileView.tsx` (full file). 2026-09-30.

Courses:
- One How = one course. Stops = steps in `tours` (reading order built by the program following `call` edges. When one caller calls several targets, use call-line order; fixture course 5 is execute → find → Order → save (2026-10-22; previously layer order). Diffs only. `course.ts` drops unchanged steps left in analyses from before 2026-10-11). How id order. Hunks belonging to no How are **outside courses** (id 0; red outside circle) and use the same screen
- Circle color and number = course. Colors in course order: blue / teal / mauve / peach / pink / yellow / lavender, repeating from the eighth. Outside is red (2026-10-30. Previously indexed by number, leaving courses numbered 8 and above without colors. `courseColorVars` (sets `--c<番号>` on `.app`) and `courseBg` in `course.ts`)
- Stops passing through another course's diff say diff from course n (「コース n の差分」) (in the fixture, course 5's second stop = Order from course 3)
- Enter from the course sequence or start button at entry (3.1) (2026-09-30. Previously from “course” in the rail and its provisional sequence). Leaving a course (`Esc` / top-bar overview / to overview (「全体図へ」) in the summary) returns to entry. Completing the last course advances to wrap-up (3.9) (leaves the course)

Three screens. Always show the course bar above (circle, title, pre-reading guidance (「読む前の案内」), stop circles and short names (tests say test (「テスト」); diffs from other courses have their course circles). Press to go there. Right side: checks n / N · no issues n / M (「確かめること n / N · 問題なし n / M」). Count a check once every supporting place is marked no issues. Analyses without checks show only no issues. course list (「コースの一覧」) `Esc`):
0. **Pre-reading guidance** (`brief`. 2026-10-22. Review time was not getting shorter: users read what the code does, then followed the same thing in the implementation. Stop prose restated adjacent code, with no guidance before opening it. A “pilot guidance first, then follow the code to verify it” example compared “pre-reading guidance → visit in call order and check” (option 1) with “visit in check order” (option 2); chose option 1). First screen on course entry (entry's enter, `Enter`, summary's complete · next (「完了 · 次は」), or retry). Absent outside courses (go straight to a stop). Keep the left file tree, merge center and right into one area, top to bottom: title, **in the PR flow (「PR の流れの中で」)** (courses calling this course → this course → courses called by this course. `call` edges whose endpoint diffs belong to different courses. Automatic. Omit if unconnected. `courseFlow` in `brief.ts`), **in the call flow (「呼び出しの流れの中で」)** (2026-11-05. The same flow map as the home map tab (3.1), narrowed to this course: it starts at ② This PR, colors only this course's calls and their returns, and grays out the rest. `←` / `→` and playback stop only at this course's steps. Only flows that carry this course's changes are offered; with two or more, pick one at the top. Courses on no flow get no map. The course name is already in the heading, so the map does not repeat it. Stage, flow, step and playing state live in `AppState.briefMap`, separate from the home map (not saved; reset to the start of ② on every course entry; coming back from a stop keeps the step). `Enter` on this screen goes to the stops (`→` advances the map). `courseFlows` in `steps.ts`), **what changes (「変わること」)** (before / after in `Tour.change`. AI. Old analyses or analyses without the LLM show the course introduction), two columns of **reading order (「読む順」)** (stops top to bottom, edge type labels (types passed) between them, dotted line before stops without an edge. Each stop has numbers of checks supported there. Press to go to the stop. Automatic) and **what to check (「確かめること」)** (`Tour.checks`. Number, prose, supporting-place chips such as execute line 16 (「execute 16 行」). Press to highlight supporting places in the reading order: `course.check`. AI), **things to know (「知っておくこと」)** (another course's diff calls this course's stops: OrderController.apply in course 6 calls execute at line 18 and save at line 19 (「コース 6 の OrderController.apply が、18 行で execute、19 行で save を呼びます。」) and peek at apply line 18 (「apply の 18 行を覗く」) (peek while staying on this screen; highlight call lines. If calls are near each other, highlight through the last call line. `line` / `endLine` in `map.peek`). Number of reading places that are diffs from other courses. Corresponding requirements (`Spec.hows`. AI). `callerFacts` in `brief.ts`), go to place n (「n か所目へ」) `Enter` (first unviewed stop; does not mark no issues). Return using previous (「前へ」) / `←` at the first stop or pre-reading guidance in the top bar (`course.brief`). Screen: `BriefBody` in `CourseChecks.tsx`
1. **Stop** (`stop`. One screen per place. 2026-10-12. Previously two screens: guidance (`guide`) → view (「見る」) `Enter` → actual code (`code`). Since code was added to the guidance screen's right on 2026-10-11, reopening an actual-code screen that simply redisplayed the same code at full width felt awkward. Working examples compared “current / one screen per place / one screen per place with collapsible left / Enter collapses left”; chose “one screen per place with collapsible left”). **Three columns** (2026-10-20. Working examples compared “guidance above, questions below / guidance also in conversation” for the right column and “dim other courses / same intensity / close other folders” for the left tree; chose “guidance also in conversation” and “dim other courses.” Previously two columns: guidance and this course's file tree on the left, code on the right; questions in a drawer from the right): **tree of all diff files** on the left (below); in the center, header (i / N, name, layer, kind, course n, no issues, map `M`, callees (「呼んでいる相手」) `O`, open in IDEA (「IDEA で開く」)), unchanged relationships (below), and code; **pilot guidance and questions column** on the right (3.10). Right column top to bottom: header (pilot guidance (「水先案内」), i / N, collapse » (「たたむ »」) `B`), about (「何について」), conversation, no issues · next `Enter` (last stop: no issues · to summary (「問題なし · 総括へ」)), previous `←`, input. The first conversation message is pilot guidance: name, **this place (「この場所」)** (`steps[].role`. AI; otherwise one line assembled from facts. Old analyses show why here (「なぜここか」) (`steps[].guide`, faint, first) and what changed (「何を変えたか」) (`steps[].say`); use two boxes if sources differ (AI / automatic). Principle 2), fact chips (call information first), **what to check** (2026-10-22. Show every sentence; highlight those supported here with supporting-place chips (here (「ここ」) / viewed) and what to inspect here (`at[].say`). Press to strongly highlight only that sentence's supporting lines (`course.check`; clears on stop change). Other sentences are one faint row; press to go to an unviewed supporting place), things to know related to this stop (same as pre-reading guidance), for another course's diff: this diff belongs to course n. Its content is covered in course n (「この差分はコース n のものです。中身はコース n で見ます。」), next: X (「次は X」) (call-location line: from execute line 17 (「execute の 17 行から」), test, or file start. `nextReason` in `brief.ts`). Questions and answers follow below (repeated questions push guidance upward, so conversation's lower right has to top (「一番上へ」) and to bottom (「一番下へ」). 3.10). Show course introduction in pre-reading guidance. Only outside courses, show outside courses · start (「コース外 · 出発」) and introduction at the very top of the first stop's conversation. **Supporting code lines** (2026-10-22): Highlight backgrounds of check-supporting lines at that stop (head side; right column in split mode); at the first visible supporting line per sentence, show its number at the line number (if a sentence is selected, only that one is strong). Do not fold supporting lines (`marks` in `FileView`). At a 1440px window, left 300px and right 400px; shrink both in narrower windows (`clamp`). **Drag column boundaries to resize** (2026-10-20. Same for the diff list file column. 3.4). Save to `panes` in `.ondina/ui.json` (3.8) on release (not during dragging); double-click a boundary to restore defaults (remove from `panes`; both use `ui.pane`). Clamp saved widths too: minima left 240px / right 280px (measured on the actual UI so headings hold) and window-relative maxima left 30vw / right 35vw, keeping center code usable when narrowing the window. Hide the right boundary when the right column is collapsed. No keyboard resizing (`←` `→` move stops). Column differences: `PANES` table in `panes.ts`; boundaries: `components/PaneHandle.tsx`. Code **embeds diffs in the full file and folds everything except 3 lines around changed lines at the current place (central lines for unchanged stops) into strips** (same strips as diff list. 3.4. Since 2026-10-13, also fold unchanged lines inside the viewed hunk. Previously only outside-hunk lines folded to `⋯ 上の n 行`, pressing which showed the full file). Full file (「ファイル全体」) `F` shows all; press again for context only. Changing stops restores context only. Do not fold memo / AI-draft lines within the current hunk plus 3 context lines (`foldRows` in `diff/fileRows.ts`; range used to decide which stop counts memos, see memo indicators below); outside that range, show counts memos n (「メモ n」), drafts n (「下書き n」) on strips. Other hunks in the same file are faint. **If this course has two or more reading places in the same file** (2026-10-19. Showing each separately made one file look like several. Guide same-file reading places within the file display on the right), also leave other places' changed lines unfolded and faint (opacity 0.5); left of each place's first visible line number, show a numbered reading-order circle (✓ when done, dotted for tests, press for `course.jump`; widen the first column to 66px only then), and place n / m in this file (「このファイルの n / m か所目」) in the file bar. Move between places in the same file by smooth scrolling without reopening (`places` in `FileView`). Toggle **split (default) / unified** (2026-10-09. Split / unified (「左右 / 統合」) in the file bar or `s` / `u`. `UiSettings.diffMode.c` (3.8; across PRs); default split if absent). Split arrangement matches the diff list (pair consecutive removed and added lines row by row; hatch the unmatched side. Same unchanged line on both sides. Do not pair lines from different hunks. `splitRows` in `diff/fileRows.ts`). Split halves the width, so wrap long lines (same as 3.4 split view). Unified scrolls horizontally. Folding is independent of display mode. Show memo “+” here too (memos below). `Enter` marks no issues and advances to the next stop (once per place); `←` goes to the previous stop (pre-reading guidance at the first stop; does nothing at the first outside-course stop; does not clear no issues)
   - **Collapse right column** (`B` or collapse ». `course.fold`. Until 2026-10-19, collapsed the left guidance column): Right column becomes a 44px strip (“«” opens; stop circles navigate), widening code (animate width; no animation with reduced motion. Keep right-column content during animation, `inert` while collapsed). Pilot guidance and chips (and introduction at the first stop) move to the bar above code; previous and no issues · next move below code. Preserve collapse while moving stops / courses and after leaving the course (stay wide in PRs with long lines). Not saved (`AppState.courseFolded`; reopens expanded). Does not apply in summary. Do not collapse left tree
2. **Summary (「総括」)** (`summary`): No issues n / N, unviewed list (pilot-guidance box), checks (2026-10-22. Each sentence: all places viewed (「場所を全部見た」) / some places not yet viewed (「まだ見ていない場所がある」) plus supporting-place chips. Press to go to that stop), visited places (return). If course hunks touch follow-on bundles, one same rewrite n (「同じ書き換え n」) box per bundle (all in representative-id order if multiple. `courseRipples`): representative (sample), deviations (differs; shows diffs and can mark no issues), remaining (same shape as sample (「サンプルと同じ形」); check individually to mark viewed). Right: complete · next course n (「完了 · 次はコース n」) `Enter` (last course: complete · to wrap-up (「完了 · 締めへ」); outside courses: finished viewing (「見終えた」)), retry, to overview

- The map is an **overlay** on the stop screen (2026-10-11. Compared five working examples (replace right side / drawer from right / large in center / always small in corner / only while held). Default to drawer for checking when needed, and allow enlargement because large PRs also have large maps). `M` or map in the header opens a drawer from the right. Enlarge (「大きく」) `⇧M` in the map header or `Shift+M` fills the center (covering the top bar too); original size (「元の大きさ」) or another `Shift+M` restores the drawer. When closed, `Shift+M` opens large directly. `Esc` / `M` / outside / close (「閉じる」) closes. Do not remember size (always opens as drawer). Closes on stop change. Resizing does not rebuild the map (same elements). Headings: you are here (「今ここ」) and next: X (until 2026-10-11, opening from guidance showed first here / next here: name (「まずはここ / 次はここ 名前」), and the next stop's marker pulsed)
- **Guidance prose** (2026-10-03. Comparing examples settled three choices: LLM for all three / automatic prose where missing / dotted border and AI): The second LLM call (writes prose; 5.3) writes the course introduction (`Tour.intro`), one line for why here (`steps[].guide`; guidance-screen line until 2026-10-11), and one line for what changed (`steps[].say`; likewise actual-code-screen line). Dotted border and AI chip (principle 4. `components/Narr.tsx`). Where LLM prose is missing (second call did not run, omitted it, or old analysis), use a fact-assembled line (`stopInfo` in `course.ts`; caller · diff from course n · file start) with an automatic chip. Outside courses always uses automatic prose because it is absent from `tours`. In analyses since 2026-10-22, the LLM writes what changes (`Tour.change`), checks (`Tour.checks`), and this place (`steps[].role`) (before reading and stop right column). Old-analysis introduction (`Tour.intro`) appears before reading if what changes is absent. Count prose (summary's unviewed (「未確認」) and wrap-up's one line) and factual chips remain program-generated. No judgment words (principle 1)
- **Unchanged relationships** (2026-10-11. Decided by comparing three examples. `RelRow` in `CourseView.tsx`): One row above code on the stop screen. Counts of current stop's unchanged callers and callees; if at most 3, show names, otherwise per-layer count chips (still one row even with hundreds of counterparts). Pressing a name opens that counterpart, or a layer filters to that layer, in the unchanged-counterpart drawer (map section). Far right: show on right (「右に出す」) `R`. Hide if no counterparts. No viewed mark. **Only show counterparts related to the diff** (2026-10-12): Callees must be called from added lines (head `+` lines). Omit calls from unchanged lines and, for class-wide diffs, targets called by other methods in the same class (fixture course 5's execute loses `OrderRepository.find` and retains only `OrderRepository.save`). Determined when edges are built (“call location” in 9.6), so the map's unchanged: n places, drawer, `o` list, and second LLM call's `related` shrink too. Rerun analyze for old analyses
- Break prose at sentence-ending punctuation, one sentence per line. Facts are chips (kind, layer, `+n −m`, tests n, ? not retrieved, file-level, sample / differs among n follow-ons)
- **Memos (「メモ」)** (2026-10-06. Decided one question at a time with examples. `memo.ts`, `diff/FileView.tsx`, `components/MemoBox.tsx`): In stop-screen code, press “+” shown on line-number hover to **select lines before writing** (same as GitHub. Do not remove the effort of choosing memo lines during review. No `N` key automatically attaching to the first changed line). Hold “+” and drag up or down to select a range (same as GitHub; replaced Shift extension on 2026-10-15. Can span base and head. Dragging downward from removed to added lines or from unchanged / added to removed lines both work). During dragging, only color the range; open the writing box on release (opening during dragging shifts lower lines and the drag target. For the same reason, unfold the selected lines' 3-line context only on release). When a writing or editing box is open and another line's “+” is pressed, keep an empty space of the same height where the old box was until release (2026-11-02. Removing the box shifted lower lines up, so a different line came under the still mouse and the range grew past the pressed line. `MemoUi.hold`). In split view, use lines in the pointer's column (left unchanged lines are head-side lines, same as right); do not change the range over another file's lines, fold strips, or memo boxes (`memo.pick` → `memo.drag` → `memo.drop`; release anywhere in the window). Open the writing box **immediately below the last selected line** (`⌘⏎` save, `Esc` cancel (「やめる」)); saved memos stay below that line with edit (「直す」) and delete (「消す」) (sent memos read-only). Any number per line at a place. In split view, as in diff list split mode (3.4), “+” appears at left line numbers for removed lines and right line numbers for added / unchanged lines; memos and writing boxes sit under that side's column (2026-10-09). Line order (`order`) is the pre-fold file position, shared by split and unified, so mode changes preserve selected lines and drafts. Memos inside folded lines show as memos n on the strip. **Cannot write memos on lines absent from GitHub's diff** (unchanged caller / callee code shown on the right, or lines far from changes) (2026-10-22. Same as GitHub, which cannot attach line comments outside diffs. Previously sent these in the overall review body, but publishing with GitHub's “Submit review” removed the body. Chapter 12). Show dotted “?” (ask-only marker) instead of “+” at those line numbers. Pressing opens an ask (「聞く」) box without a memo field, with this line is absent from GitHub's diff, so memos are unavailable. You can ask the guide (「この行は GitHub の差分に出ないので、メモは書けません。案内人に聞くことはできます」) and an ask button. Ask the guide about selected lines (3.10) on any line (this is the only line-question method in unchanged caller / callee code shown on the right; chosen over “show no marker” in working examples). Also use ask if only one range endpoint is outside the diff. Hide make memo (「メモにする」) on answers too if the asked-about lines are outside the diff. Checked by `onGithubDiff` in `memo.ts` (chunks in chapter 12). Analyses without a PR (`local`) and old analyses without GitHub diff ranges allow writing on any line. Memos are `CommentDraft` (`source: 'reviewer'`), accepted when saved. Draft prose survives range extension or line reselection (held in reducer's `memo.draft`, because the writing box is recreated below the last selected line). One writing box at a time; opening another memo's edit discards the draft as cancel does. Moving stops, peeking, or changing screens clears selected lines. “+” is mouse-only (`tabIndex=-1` avoids tabbing through every line; keyboard line selection in 3.8). For both new and edited memos, reducer `draft` holds unfinished text (the diff list is virtualized, so offscreen boxes disappear. `memo.edit` loads current body. 2026-10-06)
- **Left file tree** (2026-10-19. Decided by comparing working examples. Previously a reading-order itinerary (dot, name, layer, kind, memos n), where a file such as `DiscountCommand.kt` and `domain.DiscountCommand` appeared on two rows, looking like multiple files): Since 2026-10-20, **all diff files** (all files in every course; previously only this course's) use the same tree as the diff list (3.4; compact folders, indent guides, icons), **one row per file**. Heading: files (「ファイル」), n files (「n ファイル」), expand all / collapse all (「すべて開く / すべて閉じる」); one row below: (course circle) files in this course n (「このコースのファイル n」). Keep the tree purely a file tree (no reading-place rows or numbers on the left). Right side of this course's file rows: total memos n and AI n (「AI n」) across that file's reading places, plus no-issues indicator (count of reading places marked no issues). Press to go to the file's first place not yet marked no issues (first place if all done). Other-course file rows and folders with no files from this course are faint (opacity 0.42, hover 0.8); right side matches diff list (course circles, outside, no-issues indicator). Press to **peek (「覗く」)** at the file (`course.peekFile`; “peek” in map section below). The peeked file row is not faint and has a blue-tinted background; when not peeking, brighten the current place's file row. Open/closed state: `AppState.treeShut.course` (separate from diff list; when the current file changes, open its ancestor folders. Expand all / collapse all uses `tree.openAll` / `tree.closeAll` with `tree: 'course'`). Top circle sequence and collapsed-strip circles remain per reading place
- **Memo indicators** (2026-10-06. Examples led to “both in summary and during the course.” `courseMemos` in `memo.ts`): Orange memo count at the upper-right shoulder of each top circle (`CourseBar` dot); memos n on the stop screen's left file row (total for the file's reading places; itinerary row until 2026-10-19); in summary, a memos n count tile (even at 0), memos n on visited-place rows, and a memos in this course (「このコースのメモ」) box (stop order, then top-to-bottom lines within a stop. Each: `ファイル名 行`, cannot send to GitHub (「GitHub に送れません」) (memo on a line outside GitHub's diff; 3.9), sent, body, open (「開く」)). Count a memo **once, at the first stop where its lines are visible** (first stop whose initially visible 3-line context includes the memo's final line; if none, first stop in the same file, whose fold strip shows memos n). Count only one's own memos (including sent ones; AI drafts have separate indicators below). Summary's open uses `comment.open` with that course, opening the stop (full file if outside the 3-line context). Memos written in the diff list count at the same place for the same lines
- **AI drafts below lines** (2026-10-13. Previously AI drafts appeared only in wrap-up, invisible in diff or course code. Chose “open box” from examples. `DraftBox` in `components/MemoBox.tsx`): Show externally supplied AI drafts (chapter 12) in boxes at the same position as personal memos (below the line; in split mode, removed lines below the left column, others below the right). On the same line: memo → draft → writing box. Three locations: stop screen, unchanged-counterpart drawer, and diff list (3.4). Dotted box like pilot guidance indicates AI-written content; heading: AI draft (「AI の下書き」), source chip, `ファイル名 n 行目`. Same buttons as wrap-up (`採用` / `編集` / `破棄`; while editing: accept this text (「この文で採用」) / revert (「戻す」); accepted: revert). Acceptance gives a solid green border and accepted (「採用」) chip. Sent drafts read-only. Discarded drafts fold to one row (discarded AI draft (「破棄した AI の下書き」), body opening, revert). Sending still happens from wrap-up. Drafts within folded lines (except discarded) appear as drafts n in strips. Reducer `draftText` (draft id → text; not saved) holds edits; below-line and wrap-up boxes read the same text (survives disappearing offscreen boxes in the virtualized diff list and screen changes. `comment.edit` loads current body, `comment.text` changes it, and accept / revert / discard clears it)
- **AI-draft indicators** (2026-10-13. Examples led to “count separately from memos.” `courseDrafts` / `isOpenDraft` in `memo.ts`): **Drafts neither accepted nor discarded** (`draft` and `edit`) appear as dotted counts on top circles' **upper-left shoulder**, AI n next to memos n on left file rows (total for the file's reading places) and summary's visited-place rows, and a summary count tile AI drafts not yet decided (「まだ決めていない AI の下書き」) (“—” when no drafts in the course). Counts disappear when all decisions are made. Summary has an AI drafts in this course (「このコースの AI の下書き」) box (all states; count = undecided / total. Each: `ファイル名 行`, source, accepted / discarded / sent, body, open). Placement matches memos (once at the first stop where lines are visible)
- **Courses do not stop at import-only diffs** (2026-11-07. When the setting `autoCheckImports` is on; 3.8): a stop whose diff has `Hunk.importsOnly` stays in the stop list (shown as checked), but next (`Enter` / `→` / 「問題なし · 次へ」), previous, `j` / `k` and the entry position of a course (the first unchecked stop) skip it. When no stop is left to go to, it acts like the last stop and goes to the summary (the button reads 「問題なし · 総括へ」 and the right column says 「最後の停留所」). Pressing a stop dot or a map box (`course.jump`) still opens that stop
- **No issues marks viewed, not approval** (principle 8). Press to set, not toggle. Stops are diffs only, so add hunks to `ReviewState.viewed` (before 2026-10-11, unchanged stops added `<コース id>:<Symbol.id>` to `ReviewState.course.done`; old state `done` is read-only). Do not mark unchanged code. Completed courses go in `ReviewState.course.completed` (exclude outside courses). Enter courses at the first unviewed stop
- Full files: `Analysis.files` (head side; diff files and files in `Symbol.path`; same tokenization and semantic-token overlay as hunks). For old analyses without `Analysis.files`, show only that hunk (`Symbol.excerpt` if unchanged) and the full file is not in this analysis (rerun analyze to display it) (principle 2)
- Test stops do not appear on the map (heading says tests are not on the map (「テストは地図に出ない」))

Map (SVG, layered layout with elkjs. Implemented 2026-09-25. `packages/web/src/map/`):
- Rows (bands) = layers (configured `layers` order, ending with no layer (「層なし」)). Put band names at the far left (right edge is hidden if the map is wider than its column)
- **Nodes = changed places only** (2026-10-11). Do not make boxes for unchanged symbols (callers and callees queried one level deep per changed symbol through the language server; 9.2)
- **Group unchanged counterparts per diff box and layer as unchanged: n places** (2026-10-11. `relationsOf` / `relGroups` in `graph.ts`). Second line: callers a · callees b (「呼び出し元 a · 呼び出し先 b」). If a counterpart shares the diff box's layer, place it immediately to the right (`MapNode.side`; short connecting line); otherwise use a dotted box in its layer band (`MapNode.rel`; id = `REL_PREFIX` + diff box id + layer) and edge (`MapEdge.rel`; counterpart → diff box if more callers; bidirectional if both. Counts appear in boxes, so omit type labels and `×n`. Red dashed if even one call skips layers). Omit counterparts reached only from tests. If the same function calls two diffs, count it for both. Course-filtered maps show only groups for in-scope boxes (purple border). Press to open the **unchanged-counterpart drawer** filtered to that layer (below). For layout, pass each box plus its right neighbor to elkjs as one box; attach edges to the midpoints of the top and bottom edges of the diff-box portion (ports; without them, edges attach above the right neighbor and appear connected to it). History: Interpreted “want to see impact” on 2026-09-26 as showing unchanged functions in the same boxes as diffs. On a real project, even two-file PR overviews exceeded 10,000px in width; the 10/10 single “callers n places” (「呼び出し元 n か所」) box (3+ with same layer, counterpart, and direction) still did not fit (different counterpart sets left groups of 1–2). Policy (2026-10-11): diffs are units of responsibility. Even when AI makes reading efficient, the person who Approves is responsible for every diff. For unchanged related files, knowing the relationship is enough; do not treat them like changed files. Chose “per layer” in examples (alternative: one box per diff box). Reproducing with 90 unchanged callers added to sample diffs reduced the entry map from 6321px (62 boxes) to 1764px (31 boxes)
- **Unchanged-counterpart drawer** (2026-10-11. Decided by comparing three examples. `components/RelDrawer.tsx`, `rel.ts`): Press an unchanged map box or `R` on a course stop screen (current stop's box; test stops have no map box, but tests are diffs too, so use the test function to show unchanged counterparts it calls). Drawer from the right (same shape and position as map drawer; never both open); enlarge `⇧R` or `Shift+R` fills the center. Heading: diff-box name's callers / callees (「差分の箱の名前 の呼び出し元 / 呼び出し先」); direction tabs and layer chips below (all layers (「すべての層」) and per-layer counts). Names on the left (map-band order, alphabetical within layers), selected code on the right (unchanged-symbol form of `FileView`: full file in unified mode, scrolled to and marked at declaration and lines calling the diff (`centers` in `Symbol.excerpt`). No split / unified or full-file toggle). `↑` `↓` (`j` `k`) select next / previous counterpart (stop at ends; do not change stops). `Esc` / outside / close / another `R` closes and returns to the diff being read. Closes on stop change, screen change, or opening another analysis. **No viewed marks** (not counted toward completion). State: `AppState.rel` (`host` / `dir` / `layer` / `sel` / `size`; not saved); commands: `rel.open` / `rel.toggle` / `rel.big` / `rel.close` / `rel.select`
- For symbols without retrieved call relationships (`edgesMeasured: false`), write ? not retrieved (「? 未取得」) on box line 2
- Node badge `テスト n` (number of tests targeting that symbol)
- **Hide test symbols and test edges** (decided 2026-09-24). The badge retains “covered by tests” (「テストで触られている」)
- **Collapse a follow-on group (bundle) into one box**. Box shows count and rewrite pattern. Leave deviations as independent boxes. Click opens the bundle in by purpose (opening within the map is undecided; 3.8)
- Edges = calls (solid) and references (dotted; only between changed symbols). **Combine same-direction edges into one** (strongest kind: call > schema > test > reference; count `×n`). Input/output types on edge labels (`request → command`, `command → Order`). Type-changing places = transformation points
- Map scope is course (How). **Keep the course's hunks and edge-connected neighbors (impact), compacting empty space while preserving overall order (left/right, top/bottom)**. Bundle members use bundle boxes. Keep edges only between remaining boxes. Purple border for course hunks; ring around current stop. Outside courses (id 0) seeds from hunks belonging to no How (`OUTSIDE_SCOPE` in `graph.ts`)
- **Visually distinguish stop boxes (steps in `tours`) from related boxes (not stops)** (decided 2026-09-26). Stops have numbered circles at upper left (same numbers as top circles).
