```ts
//   (removed lines for base, added lines for head), otherwise same. Present on one side only: added / removed. Order follows the after side; a removed call goes right after the call that preceded it before the change
// - Entry = a symbol with a diff and no caller with a diff, either before or after the change (symbols with no calls on either side do not become flows). Order as for behaviors
// - The callee is opened every time it is called (calling save twice opens it twice). Only calls of the parent's sides are opened. Depth 6, a cycle, or 1000 calls per tree stops opening with cut
// - The root's status comes from the shape of the entry's hunk lines (no + and no - = same, only + = added, only - = removed, otherwise changed). Courses are the Hows of the hunks the calling line falls in (the root uses the entry's hunk)
// - Call expressions are chosen as for behaviors, from the base file before the change and the head file after it
// The fixture has one flow from OrderController.apply: ② = execute (changed) → find (same), applyDiscount (added) → rateFor (added), save (added); save (added);
// OrderResponse.from (same). ① is the 3 calls that exist before the change (execute → find, from). ③ is the 7 calls after the change. Golden: expected/flows.json
interface CallFlow {
  id: string;                                // from 'f1'. Numbered in program order
  title: string; titleProvenance: Provenance; // llm = title from the second LLM call (dotted on screen). ast = no LLM, so the entry's short name
  root: FlowCall;                            // the entry call (from = null)
  stages?: { before?: string; change?: string; after?: string };  // one or two sentences per stage (a guess by the second LLM call. ① Background / ② This PR / ③ After merge). 5.4, item 11
}
interface FlowCall {
  key: string;                               // points at the same call across steps. "caller>callee#n" joined with / from the root. The root is ">entry#1"
  from: string | null; to: string;           // Symbol.id. The root's from is null
  status: 'same' | 'changed' | 'added' | 'removed';
  before?: FlowCallSite; after?: FlowCallSite;  // one side of the call (lines are in each side's file). ① follows only calls with before, ③ only calls with after
  courses: number[];                         // How ids of the hunks the calling line falls in (ascending)
  kids: FlowCall[];                          // calls inside the callee (calling order)
  cut?: true;                                // there is more, but it is not opened (the screen shows "… rest omitted")
}
interface FlowCallSite { line?: number; call?: BehaviorCall; returns?: string }  // the root is a declaration, so it has no line or call

interface AnalysisConfig {                   // 11章。変えたら analyze の打ち直しが要る
  project?: string; language?: string; sourceRoots: string[];
  layers: Array<{ name: string; match: string[] }>;
  allowedDependencies: Record<string, string[]>;
  normalize: Record<string, string[]>;
  lsp: Record<string, { command: string; args: string[]; readyTimeoutSec: number;
         importNotification?: { method: string; succeededField: string; failedField: string } }>;  // 9.1。args の {cacheDir} は cli が置換
  llm?: { command: string };                 // LLM コマンド（5.3）。UI の UiSettings.llmCommand が優先
}
interface UiSettings {                       // .ondina/ui.json。PR をまたぐ UI 設定（3.8）。画面の設定は全部ここ。解析が無くても読み書きできる
  worktreeRoot?: string; llmCommand?: string;
  reviewCommand?: string;                    // AI レビューのコマンド（3.11。2026-10-22）。PR ごとに sh -c で起動する。無ければ PR 一覧に「AI にレビューを頼む」を出さない
  ticket?: { source?: 'jira' | 'command'; jiraUrl?: string; email?: string; command?: string };  // チケットの取り方（5.1。2026-10-12）。source が無ければ jira
  theme?: ThemeSettings;                     // 3.5。無ければ DisplayConfig.theme
  diffMode?: { b?: 'sbs'|'uni'; c?: 'sbs'|'uni'; d?: 'sbs'|'uni' };  // b = 目的ごとの画面、c = コースの停留所の画面、d = 差分一覧。無い画面は左右
  keymap?: Record<string, string[]>;         // ショートカットキー（18.2。2026-10-20）。既定から変えた操作だけ（操作の名前 → キー。空の配列はキーなし）
  panes?: Partial<Record<'stopTree'|'stopGuide'|'diffTree', number>>;  // 列の幅（3.3。2026-10-20）。境目をドラッグした列だけ（列 → px の整数）。無い列は既定の幅
  mapSpeed?: number;  // playback speed of the home map's flow (3.1; 2026-11-04, the flow since 2026-11-05). 0.25–3; 1 when missing
  autoCheckImports?: boolean;  // mark import-only diffs (`Hunk.importsOnly`) as viewed on load, and do not stop at them in courses (3.3, 3.8; 2026-11-07). Off when missing
}
interface DisplayConfig {                    // 11章の表示だけの部分。serve が毎回読んで GET /api/config で UI に渡す（再解析不要）
  packagePrefix?: string;
  theme: ThemeSettings;                      // テーマの既定。UiSettings.theme が無いときに使う
  ide: { scheme: 'jetbrains'; product: string; project?: string };
}
interface ThemeSettings {                    // 3.5（2026-10-22）
  mode: 'system' | 'light' | 'dark';         // system = OS の外観（prefers-color-scheme）に合わせる
  light: string; dark: string;               // ライト・ダークのときのフレーバー（light は latte、dark は frappe / macchiato / mocha）
  accent: string;
}
```

Derived values (not saved): unattributed = `hunks.filter(h => h.hows.length === 0)`, entanglement = `hows.length > 1`, meters (3.7).

---

## 5. Architecture and pipeline

```
[入力]                    [Stage 1: 決定的]                      [Stage 2: LLM 1回]        [Stage 3: 検証・描画]
gh pr view / diff  ──▶  git: 変更ファイルの中身(base / head)
チケット本文       ──▶  tree-sitter: 意味的hunk + 安定ID   ──┐
ondina.config.yaml ──▶ 追従検出(AST正規化) + パターン導出   ├──▶ JSON ──▶ Claude ──▶ JSON ──▶ 検証 ──▶ Analysis.json ──▶ Web UI
                        レイヤー割当(glob)                     │      (structured output)          (存在しないID除外、
                        kotlin-lsp: semantic tokens,           │                                    未帰属/絡み計算、
                                    call hierarchy,            │                                    前回How ID維持)
                                    definition               ──┘
                        Shiki: 字句トークン + 意味トークン重ね
```

### 5.1 Input
- Get metadata with `gh pr view <n> --json number,title,baseRefName,headRefName,headRefOid`. Read base / head contents from git (do not create worktrees for analysis. 2026-10-23). Implemented in `packages/cli/src/{pr,git,analyze}.ts` (2026-09-16)
  - If the target repository is a shallow clone, first run `git fetch --unshallow origin` (CI's `actions/checkout` defaults to `fetch-depth: 1`; merge-base cannot be found without the branch point locally). Fetch head as `refs/pull/<n>/head` from `origin`. **baseSha is `merge-base(base ブランチ, head)`** (the same file set as GitHub's Files changed. Unrelated changes do not get mixed in even if base has advanced). TODO: the remote name is fixed to `origin`
  - List changed files with `git diff --name-status --no-renames baseSha headSha`, read their contents from git with `git cat-file --batch`, and create `ChangedFile { path, before, after }` (`readBlobs` in `git.ts`. Launched once each for base / head. Launching once per file took 2.4 seconds for 300 files; batching took 0.05 seconds. Measured 2026-10-23). Read files for unchanged symbols on the path from head in the same way. Files outside the repository in language-server answers (starting with `../`, or absolute) are not read and get no contents; their count and examples are reported as 「呼び出し関係に、リポジトリの外のファイルが N 件ありました」 (git aborts the whole cat-file if even one such path is passed. 2026-11-05). Treat renames as deletion + addition (consistent with “renames get a different ID” in 5.2)
  - `ondina analyze --base <ref> --head <ref>` analyzes two local refs without calling gh (for checking with the fixture repository. Uses `local` as the key instead of `<pr>`). Set a title with `--title <題>` (default: `<base>..<head>`)
  - **Working tree** (2026-10-15. Shows AI changes made before a PR as a pseudo PR. Used with Return to AI (「AI に返す」) in chapter 12): without `--head`, analyze both commits on the current branch and uncommitted changes (`resolveWorkingTree` in `git.ts`). AI may or may not commit, so refs are not assumed
    - baseSha is `merge-base(base, HEAD)`. Commits added to the branch after it diverged from base are included in the diff (view both commits on the branch and uncommitted changes together. 2026-10-15)
    - headSha is the **tree** of the working tree (uncommitted changes and new files not ignored by git) (`snapshotWorkingTree`). Copy the real index into a temporary index (`GIT_INDEX_FILE`), then run `git add -A` → `write-tree`. The actual index, HEAD, and branch do not change. If the working tree has no changes, use the HEAD commit as is
    - Do not create a commit (2026-10-15. Initially, `commit-tree` made a temporary commit. The head worktree's HEAD pointed to it, so it appeared in `git log --all`). Write the tree and contents (blobs) to git, but no branch, tag, or commit points to them, so they do not appear in the log. They survive gc while the index of the language server's head worktree (9.1) points to them. Once unreferenced, they disappear after git's default grace period (two weeks). Old analysis screens render from Analysis.json alone, so this causes no problem
    - The same working tree produces the same sha and reuses saved analysis and LLM results (principle 7)
    - Exclude `.ondina` and worktree storage directories inside the repository (they contain the language server's base / head worktrees; including them would create nested repositories). Do not put git-ignored paths in the exclusion list (`git add` already omits them, and git stops if an ignored path is listed in `:(exclude)`. This happened on the second analysis for a user who added `.ondina` to `.gitignore`. 2026-10-15)
    - To put a tree in a language server worktree (9.1), create it at the repository's HEAD commit, then align its contents with `git read-tree --reset -u <tree>` (a worktree's HEAD can only hold a commit). Determine the worktree's current version as “the HEAD commit if the index tree equals the HEAD tree; otherwise the index tree.” Return to commits with `checkout --force --detach` as before (also removes files added by the tree. Verified 2026-10-15)
    - `Analysis.pr.workingTree: true`. `pr.head` is the branch name at analysis time (used to find the ticket; the short HEAD sha when detached). The default title is `<base>..<ブランチ>（作業中）`
- Tickets are optional (implemented 2026-10-12. `packages/cli/src/ticket.ts`). Specify one with `--ticket <値>`, with either a PR number or `--base` / `--head`. Retrieved contents go in `Analysis.pr.ticket` (`Ticket` in chapter 4). Retrieval failure does not stop analysis; put the reason in `error` and print one line to analyze's stderr. Retrieve it again on each `analyze` (tickets change independently of headSha, so retain retrieval time `at`). Retrieval is not limited to Jira (avoid tying it to one environment; allow issue trackers other than Jira):
  1. **File**: if the value exists as a file, read it as Markdown regardless of settings (`source: 'file'`. No number or title)
  2. **Command** (when `UiSettings.ticket.source` is `command`): launch `ticket.command` with `sh -c`, passing the value in environment variable `ONDINA_TICKET` (leave other environment variables as is). If stdout is JSON with a string in any of `title` / `body` / `url` / `key` / `kind` / `status`, read it in that form (no body if `body` is absent). If it is not JSON, or is JSON without any readable fields, read the whole output as a Markdown body (do not silently discard it. 2026-10-12 review). A nonzero exit code, empty output, or more than 60 seconds means retrieval failed (include the last stderr line in the reason). Add non-Jira sources here (for GitHub Issues, for example, `gh issue view "$ONDINA_TICKET" --json title,body,url`)
  3. **Jira** (default): the value is a ticket key (`SHOP-421`) or Jira URL (`…/browse/SHOP-421`; also reads `selectedIssue=SHOP-421`). For a URL, use the portion before `/browse/` as the Jira location (preserving prefixes such as `/jira` on Jira Server / Data Center), so no configuration is needed. For a key, use `ticket.jiraUrl`; retrieval fails if absent. `GET <場所>/rest/api/2/issue/<番号>?fields=summary,description,issuetype,status&expand=renderedFields` (available on both Jira Cloud and Server / Data Center). The body is `renderedFields.description` (Jira's HTML-converted body. `format: 'html'`), or `fields.description` as `text` if absent. If a token exists, authenticate with Basic when `ticket.email` exists (Cloud email + API token), otherwise Bearer (Jira Server / Data Center personal access token). The token comes from environment variable `ONDINA_TICKET_TOKEN`, falling back to Mac Keychain service `ondina-jira` (`security find-generic-password -s ondina-jira -w`. Read only when querying Jira. Allow 60 seconds to wait for the unlock dialog. 2026-10-12). Store it in Keychain with `ondina token` (paste and press Enter. Read without displaying it, and pass `add-generic-password -U` via `security -i` stdin. Do not put the token in arguments. After storing it, read it back in the same way to verify. `packages/cli/src/keychain.ts`). Without a token, query without authentication (some Jira instances allow read access). Abort after 30 seconds
  - Reasons for retrieval failure (shown verbatim on screen. In environments without copy and paste, users cannot paste diagnostic command output, so ondina reports the reason): for 401 / 403, “Jira returned 401. The token is missing or expired” (「Jira が 401 を返しました。トークンがないか、期限が切れています」) (if no token exists, append “Neither environment variable ONDINA_TICKET_TOKEN nor Keychain entry ondina-jira exists” (「環境変数 ONDINA_TICKET_TOKEN も、キーチェーンの ondina-jira もありません」). If Keychain could not be read, include that reason; on non-Mac systems, mention only the environment variable). For 404, “SHOP-421 does not exist in Jira. Check the key or your permission to view it” (「Jira に SHOP-421 がありません。番号か、見る権限を確かめてください」). For a non-JSON response, “Jira's response is not JSON. You may have been redirected to a login page” (「Jira の答えが JSON ではありません。ログイン画面に回された可能性があります」). If unreachable, “Could not connect to Jira: <reason>” (「Jira に接続できませんでした: <理由>」)
  - TODO: proxies. Node's fetch does not read `HTTPS_PROXY`, so it cannot reach Jira in environments that require a proxy (whether one is needed is not verified. If unreachable, the “Could not connect” message above identifies it)
  - Once retrieved, extract requirements after the first LLM call (10.8. 2026-10-12). Do not pass the ticket to the first or second call (grouping by purpose and the entry explanation come from the diff alone)
  - **Which project this belongs to (「どんな案件の一部か」)** (implemented 2026-10-29. `packages/cli/src/project.ts`. Background (「背景」) in the explain tab. 3.1): if the ticket was retrieved from Jira, investigate alongside layer inference, LSP, and the first call, and store it in `Analysis.background.project`. Chosen after comparing three retrieval approaches (2026-10-29. Requires no Jira–GitHub integration setup, and follows the same writing conventions ondina uses to find tickets in titles, branch names, and bodies. Jira's development panel uses an unpublished API and is empty without integration. Searching only the parent key returns zero results in workflows that mention only child keys). All are program-derived facts without LLM involvement (principle 3)
    1. **Parent ticket**: the parent field from `GET …/rest/api/2/issue/<番号>?fields=parent` (includes epics on Jira Cloud; the sub-task parent on Jira Server / Data Center). Fall back to the epic field (Epic Link). Field IDs differ by Jira instance and names differ by language, so find the field whose `schema.custom` is `com.pyxis.greenhopper.jira:gh-epic-link` in `GET …/rest/api/2/field`, and read its value with `?fields=<id>`. If neither exists, omit project entirely. Retrieve the parent contents in the same way as the ticket (`fields=summary,description,issuetype,status&expand=renderedFields`). Keep the ticket request unchanged and investigate the parent in a separate request (preserve working retrieval. The wait overlaps with the first call)
    2. **Tickets under the same parent**: use JQL `parent = <親> ORDER BY key ASC` (`cf[<id の数>] = <親>` if following the epic field) with `…/rest/api/2/search?jql=…&fields=summary&maxResults=100`. For Jira Cloud where the old search API is retired (404 / 410), use `…/rest/api/2/search/jql`. Sort keys numerically (SHOP-9 < SHOP-10)
    3. **PRs in the same project**: search for the keys of the parent, this ticket, and the remaining children (up to 30. `MAX_PROJECT_KEYS`. The excess count is `skipped`) on this PR's GitHub (the host from `gh pr view <n> --json url` for PR analysis, or `gh repo view --json url` for local analysis. Same reason as Viewed in 12.1). Alias GraphQL `search(query: "\"SHOP-420\" is:pr in:title,body", type: ISSUE)` for each key and send them in one `gh api graphql --hostname` call (the whole GitHub host; includes PRs in other repositories). Since search matches terms loosely, retain only PRs whose title, body, or branch name contains the key without adjacent alphanumeric characters (do not treat `SHOP-4210` as `SHOP-421`). Deduplicate PRs and put all matched keys in `keys`. Identify this PR by the same repository and either the same number or branch name (branch name for local analysis, which has no number)
    - Put each retrieval failure on one line in `errors` (on screen and analyze's stderr. “Could not retrieve parent ticket SHOP-400: …” (「親チケット SHOP-400 を取得できませんでした: …」), “Could not find tickets under the same parent: …” (「同じ親の下のチケットを探せませんでした: …」), “Could not find PRs in the same project: …” (「同じ案件の PR を探せませんでした: …」). If the parent lookup failed, only “Could not look up the parent ticket: …” (「親チケットを調べられませんでした: …」)). On success, print one stderr line: “Project: parent ticket SHOP-400 Spring campaign, 4 PRs in the same project (searched 4 keys)” (「案件: 親チケット SHOP-400 春のキャンペーン、同じ案件の PR 4 件（番号 4 個で検索）」)
    - Do not look up parents for tickets retrieved through commands or files (no Jira fields)
  - **Without `--ticket`** (implemented 2026-10-12): find tickets in the PR title, branch name (`pr.head`), and body, then retrieve those found using the same methods 1–3 above. Real PR titles and bodies have no writing convention, so the program collects candidates and the LLM chooses which ticket belongs to this PR (principle 3. Do not use a classifier that communicates externally). Record in `Analysis.pr.ticketSearch` (chapter 4)
    1. **Collect candidates** (`packages/core/src/ticket/candidates.ts`): key pattern `[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]*-\d+` (at word boundaries. Uppercase only in titles and bodies; also collect lowercase in branch names and uppercase it) and URLs (`https?://…`. Exclude parentheses and full-width punctuation; strip trailing `.,;:!?`). Do not collect Markdown image URLs or contents of HTML comments (writing examples in PR templates). Do not collect `#123` (cannot distinguish it from a PR number). Ignore the default local-mode title (`main..feature/x`), since no person wrote it (read titles set with `--title`. 2026-10-15). Merge identical keys into one candidate (including keys within URLs); if a URL exists for the key, retrieve by URL. One location per line. Maximum 20 (print one stderr line if exceeded)
    2. **Choose** (10.7): if there is at least one candidate, let the LLM choose (even a single candidate can be irrelevant, such as `UTF-8`). Do not call it if no candidates exist
    3. **Retrieve**: pass the chosen candidate's `ref` to the retrieval method above (keep `UiSettings.ticket` settings)
    - Start at the beginning of analysis (alongside layer inference and LSP), and wait before assembling `Analysis`
    - stderr: on success, “Ticket: SHOP-421 Title (Jira. Inferred by the LLM from 2 candidates)” (「チケット: SHOP-421 題（Jira。候補 2 件から LLM が選んだ推定）」). If none was chosen, “No ticket found (the LLM said none of the 2 candidates was this PR's ticket)” (「チケットは見つかりませんでした（候補 2 件のどれも、LLM がこの PR のチケットではないと答えました）」) or “No ticket selected (2 candidates): --no-llm” (「チケットを選んでいません（候補 2 件）: --no-llm」). Print nothing if there are no candidates (visible in the Spec area on screen)

### 5.2 Stage 1 (deterministic; fully reproducible)
1. **Semantic hunks**: parse both before / after versions of changed files with tree-sitter-kotlin. Make the smallest declaration node touched by the diff (fun / class / property / top-level) one hunk. Merge multiple changed ranges within one declaration into one hunk. SQL is handled per file. Implemented in `packages/core/src/hunks/extract.ts`. Rules (finalized 2026-09-15):
   - Declaration units: class / object / companion / fun / property / secondary constructor / init / typealias / enum entry. Do not descend into local declarations inside function bodies. Include immediately adjacent preceding comments (KDoc) in the declaration range (annotations are already inside the declaration in the syntax tree)
   - Pair the ith lines of each changed block in the line diff between old and new sides. If the paired declarations differ, promote to their common parent declaration (for example, deleting `getDiscount()` and adding `applyDiscount()` inside `class Order` makes all of `Order` one hunk. This derives R / A in chapter 8 from the same core hunk). Without a common parent declaration (two top-level declarations), promote to a file hunk
   - Merge nested hunks into the outer hunk
   - Merge changes outside declarations (package / import / top-level comments) into one file hunk per file (`symbolKind: 'file'`, `symbolFqName` is the path, `symbol` is the filename). Do not pair them with declarations (even if a declaration is added in the same block as an import change, it is not absorbed into the file hunk)
   - A file hunk has `importsOnly: true` when every changed line (`+` / `-`) is a package line, an import line, or blank (2026-11-07; the field is absent otherwise). It is decided with the syntax tree's `package_header` / `import` nodes, applying `-` lines to the old tree and `+` lines to the new tree (no regular expression). A single comment, `@file:` annotation, or declaration line rules it out. Non-Kotlin files never get it
   - Changes to whitespace-only lines do not create hunks
   - `lines` is a unified diff recomputed over the hunk's declaration ranges (old and new). `lnOld` / `lnNew` are the starting lines on each side, or the insertion position if that side has no lines
   - Distinguish sibling declarations with the same name (overloads) by adding `#2` `#3` in occurrence order. TODO: distinguish by argument types (a tradeoff with rename tolerance)
2. **Stable IDs**: `sha1(path + '#' + fqName)`. Renames get a different ID (accepted)
3. **Follow-on detection** (chapter 8)
4. **Layer assignment** (implemented 2026-09-21 in `packages/core/src/layers/assign.ts` / `symbols/build.ts`): determined by globs (picomatch) in the configuration file's `layers`. `Analysis.symbols` maps one-to-one to changed hunks (`changed: true`, with `hunkId`. Unchanged symbols on paths require LSP and come in M2). Rules:
   - **Check from top to bottom and use the first matching layer**. A file can match two layers (`src/test/kotlin/com/example/order/domain/OrderTest.kt` matches both `src/test/**` and `**/domain/**`), so configuration authors can choose by ordering. The fixture puts `Tests` first
   - If no glob matches, **no layer** (omit both `layer` and `layerProvenance`). Infer unmatched files in Stage 2 (`unlayeredPaths` / `layerGuesses` in chapter 10), and mark validated results with `layerProvenance: 'llm'` (implemented 2026-09-22 in `packages/core/src/layers/guesses.ts`. Accept only existing layer names and paths that really have no layer)
   - Actual fixture counts: layers assigned to 13 of 37 changed files, or 22 of 48 hunks. Most files without a layer belong to follow-on groups
   - **When configuration has no `layers`** (2026-10-09): assign by rules from the structure inferred from the entire repository (10.6). ondina has no naming conventions (controller / service, etc.). The LLM infers layer names, order, and assignment. Assigned layers have `layerProvenance: 'llm'`
5. **LSP** (chapter 9. Implemented 2026-09-22 in `packages/cli/src/lsp-queries.ts` / `packages/core/src/lsp/edges.ts`): semanticTokens for changed files (both base / head); incoming / outgoing callHierarchy for each changed symbol (**one hop only**. Add neighboring unchanged symbols to `symbols` with `changed: false`); hover for declarations appearing in edges (edge labels); references for changed symbols (testCount). If unavailable, continue with lexical tokens alone (9.5)
6. **Highlighting** (chapter 7)
7. **Test mapping and reference edges** (implemented 2026-09-22; reference edges added 2026-09-24): send `textDocument/references` at the declaration's own position (including properties and classes. `findDeclarationPosition`). Round responses in test files (“Identifying tests” below) to the enclosing test functions, and use them as `Symbol.testCount`. Record response positions and each file's `documentSymbol` ranges (`CallGraphSource.refs` / `ranges`). `buildCallGraph` maps declarations enclosing reference positions (from inside out) to changed symbols to create `Edge(kind:'reference', n)` (9.6). Create `Edge(kind:'test')` from **the test function's `outgoingCalls`** (tests do not appear in the target's `incomingCalls`. 9.6). Omit `testCount` for symbols not queried (do not conflate unmeasured with zero)
   - **Identifying tests** (2026-10-12. `testPathsFor` in `packages/core/src/layers/assign.ts`): use the same identification for hunk `kind: 'test'` (chapter 8, item 1. Excluded from maps and bundles), `testCount`, and `Edge(kind:'test')`. In priority order:
     1. Configuration: globs of `Tests` in `layers` (case insensitive), and `sourceRoots` containing a directory named `test` (`<root>/**`). If either exists, `config`
     2. If absent from configuration, `tests` from structure inference (10.6). Directories include descendants; files require exact matches. `llm`
     3. If neither exists, identify nothing as a test. No naming conventions (such as `src/test`)
   - Until 2026-10-12, hunks used `sourceRoots`, while edges separately used `Tests` or the default `src/test/**` (repository root only). In an actual repository (configured only for the language server), no tests were identified and the map showed one box for each test function
   - When inferred: test hunks have `kindProvenance: 'llm'`, and `Analysis.structure.tests` holds the inferred locations. The screen appends “(inferred)” (「（推定）」) to Test and Tests n (「テスト n」) (kind chips, counts by purpose, and course facts and guidance. The map's Tests n boxes lack room, so use dashed borders. Principle 2)

### 5.3 Stage 2 (LLM. Two calls per PR: group by purpose and write prose. Implemented 2026-09-17; second call added 2026-10-03)
Inputs and outputs are in chapter 10. **The LLM is not necessarily Claude**. ondina has no vendor-specific SDK. It launches the configured **LLM command** with `sh -c`, sends the entire prompt (instructions + output shape + input JSON) to stdin, and reads JSON from stdout. That is the whole contract. ondina adds no CLI-specific flags (they exist only in the default command string). Do not enforce the output shape on the LLM side; inspect it in Stage 3 (strip code fences and surrounding prose). The prompt text also says “Do not read files or use tools; return only one JSON object”
- Command resolution order: `llmCommand` in the UI's `.ondina/ui.json` > environment variable `ONDINA_LLM_COMMAND` > yaml `llm.command` (chapter 11) > default `claude -p --tools "" --max-turns 1 --model opus` (verified with Claude Code 2.1.274. Works with subscription login; API billing if `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` exists). Locally verified alternatives: `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check -s read-only -` (codex-cli 0.154.0), `opencode run` (OpenCode 1.18.31. Reads stdin)
- **Saving and reuse**: retain raw stdout in `.ondina/<key>/llm/<headSha>.json`, and do not launch again for the same headSha (same result for the same headSha. Principle 7). Rerun with `--relabel`. Skip with `--no-llm`. If the command is missing, exits with code ≠ 0, exceeds 10 minutes, or returns no extractable JSON, print one stderr line explaining why and write with empty `hows` (do not save failures). Retain `{ command, status: ok | skipped | failed, reason }` in `Analysis.llm`
- **Previous results**: from the latest analysis with the same key and branch (`pr.head`) but a different headSha, pass assignments for hunks with identical IDs and `lines`, plus the previous `hows`, as `previous`, with the instruction “Preserve assignments of unchanged hunks and How IDs.” Validation does not enforce this (preservation verified in fixture records: `fixtures/order-service/llm/output-previous.json`). All local analyses use key `local`, so distinguish the same change by branch (2026-10-30. When another branch's analysis was used as previous, the LLM chose numbers absent from the previous result, so courses began at 8 or 50)
- Do not specify temperature (Opus 5 does not accept it). Limit variation through previous results and reuse of saved output
- **Second call (write prose. 2026-10-03)**: call the same LLM command after validating the first call and determining reading order (`tours`). Have it write the entry explanation (`summary`), What changes and What to check in each course (with supporting lines), and each stop's Role here (`Tour.change` / `Tour.checks` / `steps[].role`. 2026-10-22. Previously the course introduction and two lines per stop: `Tour.intro` / `steps[].guide` / `steps[].say`) (inputs and outputs in 10.4). `summary` was removed from the first call because stops are determined only afterward, and to avoid regenerating first-call records (course grouping and numbering remain unchanged in fixtures)
  - Save in `.ondina/<key>/llm/<headSha>.text.json`. Retain the prompt's sha1 (`promptSha1`); if it differs from the current prompt, launch instead of reusing saved output (a change to the first result also changes the second input). `--relabel` reruns both; `--no-llm` skips both. Do not run if the first call is not `ok` (omit `Analysis.llmText`)
  - Do not save failures. Retain the reason in `Analysis.llmText`, and write with an empty `summary` and no prose in `tours` (the screen uses generated prose and the entry banner. 3.1 / 3.3)
  - Do not pass `previous`. Even if wording changes with each push, the meter, unattributed count, and course grouping stay the same (all counts come from the first call and the program). Saved output gives the same prose for the same headSha (principle 7). Pass previous if variation becomes a problem
  - Implementation: input, prompt, and validation in `packages/core/src/narration/`; launch in `packages/cli/src/analyze.ts` (shares `runLlm` with the first call). Record in `fixtures/order-service/llm/text-output.json` (`scripts/record-llm-text.mjs`. Input is golden file `expected/llm-text-input.json`, so neither the language server nor the fixture git repository is needed)
- **Ticket selection (2026-10-12. 10.7)**: when `--ticket` is absent and candidates exist, call the same LLM command before the first call (alongside layer inference and LSP). Save in `.ondina/<key>/llm/<プロンプトの sha1>.ticket.json`. Lookup uses the prompt rather than headSha, so reuse the previous response even for a new commit if the title, branch name, and body are the same (principle 7). Rerun with `--relabel`; skip with `--no-llm` (retain only candidates). Do not save failures
- **Requirement extraction (2026-10-12. 10.8)**: when a ticket was retrieved and the first call is `ok`, call the same LLM command after first-call validation, **concurrently** with the second call (adds no waiting time). Extract “what should hold in this PR” from the ticket, and have it choose the corresponding diffs for each requirement from the first call's diffs (since 2026-11-09; before that it chose Hows). Keep this out of the first call so the ticket's wording does not steer grouping by purpose (purposes come from the diff alone), and because tickets change independently of headSha
  - Save in `.ondina/<key>/llm/<鍵>.spec.json`. The key is the prompt's sha1 excluding `previous`. For the same diff, Hows, and ticket, reuse the same response even if the previous analysis changes (same requirements even when reanalyzing an old headSha. Principle 7). Extract again if the ticket body changes. Rerun with `--relabel`. With `--no-llm`, the first call does not run, so this does not run either
  - `previous`: if the latest analysis with the same key (same `findPrevious` as the first call) has the same ticket (`key`, or `ref` if absent), pass its `specs` prose and mappings, asking to preserve wording and mappings for identical requirements
  - Do not save failures. Retain the reason in `Analysis.llmSpecs` and write with empty `specs` (the screen shows the reason and expanded source text. 3.1). stderr: “Could not extract requirements: reason” (「要求を抜き出せませんでした: 理由」)
  - Implementation: input, prompt, and validation in `packages/core/src/spec/`; launch in `specsFor` in `packages/cli/src/analyze.ts`. Record in `fixtures/order-service/llm/spec-output.json` (`scripts/record-llm-spec.mjs`. Input is golden file `expected/llm-spec-input.json`. Built from the HTML body of fake Jira ticket `ticket/jira-issue.json`, so neither Jira nor the language server is needed)
- **Ask the guide (2026-10-08)**: separately from the two calls above, call the same LLM command only when Send is pressed during `serve` (one call per question. 3.10 / 10.5). Use the same resolution order, read each time (settings changes apply from the next question). Abort after five minutes (`ASK_TIMEOUT_MS`). If the screen aborts the request (Stop), kill the entire process group (`AbortSignal` in `runLlmCommand`)
- Implementation: input, prompt, and validation in `packages/core/src/label/` (deterministic); launch, saving, and previous-result lookup in `packages/cli/src/llm.ts`. Tests do not launch the LLM; they use recorded input/output JSON (`fixtures/order-service/llm/`. Regenerate with `scripts/record-llm.sh`)

### 5.4 Stage 3 (validation. Implemented 2026-09-17 in `packages/core/src/label/verify.ts`)
The program determines everything. Print each exclusion and renumbering on its own line to `analyze` stderr (do not silently correct).
1. Check that output hunk IDs / bundle IDs (`g1` …) exist. Exclude nonexistent IDs. Also exclude Hows left empty by these exclusions
2. Keep How IDs if they are integers ≥ 1 without duplicates. For duplicates, missing IDs, or invalid IDs, renumber from 1 in occurrence order. Keep the LLM's output order (instructions request dependency order). Warnings and reordering for conflicts with call edges come in M2, when edges exist
3. Compute `hunk.hows` from the How-side `hunkIds` / `groupIds`. Give every representative, member, and deviation in a bundle the same How. The program counts unattributed hunks and entanglement (do not have the LLM write “unattributed”)
4. A `none` hunk included in a How becomes `kind: 'core'` (`kindProvenance: 'llm'`). Otherwise it stays `none` = unattributed. Do not change `ripple` / `test` / `core` (AST-derived). Do not have the LLM write kind
5. File hunks omitted by the LLM (import-only changes) inherit the How if every other hunk in the same file has the same How
6. Check whether the original `claims` text appears verbatim in the author's description (`pr.body`) (detect alterations). Exclude it if absent. If the body is empty, `claims` is empty
7. Requirement extraction (10.8. `packages/core/src/spec/verify.ts`): strip surrounding whitespace and the final period, exclude empty sentences, and keep only the first identical sentence. Exclude the 11th onward (`MAX_SPECS`). Keep only selectable input diff IDs in `hunkIds` (report unknown IDs to stderr); deduplicate and preserve input hunk order. Derive `Spec.hows` from the selected diffs. Assign IDs `s1`… in the remaining order. Reverse-map `How.specIds` from `specs`. Final line: “Requirements: 8 (6 with corresponding diffs)” (「要求: 6 件（対応する差分があるもの 5 件）」). For Why (「なぜ」) sentences (2026-10-29), strip surrounding whitespace and retain only those contained in the ticket body passed to the LLM (compare ignoring whitespace and Markdown emphasis/code markers, to avoid showing paraphrases as “source text”). Exclude empty and identical sentences (whitespace/marker-only differences count as identical), and the fourth onward (`MAX_WHY`). Store the remainder in `Analysis.background.why`. Apply the same rules to parent ticket purpose sentences (`parentWhy`. 2026-10-29), retaining only those in the parent ticket body (exclude all if no parent exists). Store the remainder in `Analysis.background.project.why`
8. Accept `layerGuesses` only for “layer names that exist in configuration” and “paths actually in `unlayeredPaths`” (do not let inference overwrite glob-determined layers). Keep only the first occurrence of a repeated path. Accepted entries get `layerProvenance: 'llm'` (implemented 2026-09-22 in `packages/core/src/layers/guesses.ts`)
9. Second call (write prose. `packages/core/src/narration/verify.ts`): exclude courses with nonexistent `howId` and prose for `symbolId` absent from that course's stops / treat empty or whitespace-only prose as absent (the screen uses generated prose) / keep only the first duplicate course or stop (even if the first prose is empty, do not use later occurrences. Same rule as 8) / do not change paths (order or call relationships) / entry explanation (2026-10-29): strip surrounding whitespace from the Key point (「要点」), Example (「例」), and box prose; omit empty fields. Supporting locations for examples follow the same rules as checks (stops may belong to any course). Diagram boxes may reference only existing courses; a course appears only once across all diagrams, at its first occurrence. Exclude boxes with no remaining courses or empty titles, and diagrams with no remaining boxes. Names beneath boxes must be stops in that box's courses. If an entry explanation was written, print a final line (`入口の説明: 要点 あり、例 あり（場所 2）、図 1 本（箱 5）`. Also lists courses absent from every box) / background (2026-10-29): Existing items (「前からあるもの」) names must appear among candidates (input `existing`). Keep a candidate only in the first row where it appears (once even if repeated in that row). If disappearing and unchanged items are mixed, keep only those matching the first name's category (one fact per chip). Exclude rows with empty prose or no remaining names; maximum three rows (`MAX_EXISTING`). The program supplies disappearance, path, and kind from candidates. Exclude Terms (「言葉」) with empty wording, and code names (allow trailing `()` and identifiers joined by `.`) that are not identifiers appearing in diff lines. Keep the first occurrence of each name, up to four (`MAX_TERMS`). If background was written, print a final line (`背景: 前からあるもの 3 行（候補 5）、言葉 4 個`) / checks (2026-10-22): retain only supporting locations that are stops in that course, and line numbers on the head side (context or added lines) of that stop's hunk (the screen highlights those lines, so do not retain nonexistent ones). Exclude locations with no remaining lines, sentences with no remaining locations, and empty sentences. Keep only the first identical location in the same sentence. Reorder locations in reading order; sort and deduplicate lines ascending. Exclude the sixth onward (`MAX_CHECKS`). Report each exclusion on its own line, then report filled counts on a final line (`案内文: 変わること 7 / 7、この場所 29 / 29、確かめること 23（場所 32）`. stderr reveals omissions)
10. `themes` (map groups. 2026-11-04 `packages/core/src/mapgroup/build.ts`) is read only when there are 8 or more Hows. Use it only when all of these hold: 7 or fewer themes, no empty title, every How in exactly one theme, and no unknown How id. Otherwise use none of it, print the reason to stderr in one line, and put every course on the overview (still more than 7. Do not fix it silently)
11. Request flow titles (behavior titles on 2026-11-04, flows from 2026-11-05. `packages/core/src/behavior/titles.ts`): drop titles with unknown ids; for a repeated id use only the first. Trim spaces and a trailing full stop; if empty, keep the entry's short name (`titleProvenance: 'ast'`). The order is the LLM's output order (display order); flows the LLM did not write follow in program order. The call tree is not changed. One last line (`流れの題: 1 / 1 本`). Per-stage sentences (2026-11-05) have surrounding whitespace trimmed and are left out when empty. ① is kept only when the entry exists before the change, ③ only when it exists after the merge, and ② only when the entry or some call changed; anything else is dropped with one notice line each. The second entry for a repeated id is not used either. If any remain, one last line (`流れの段の文: ① 1・② 1・③ 1 / 1 本`). Until the map is replaced by the flow map, the same title is copied to the behavior with the same entry, in flow order (`copyFlowTitles`)

### 5.5 Execution modes
- **CLI**: generate Analysis.json with `ondina analyze <pr>` and run the local UI with `ondina serve`. Cache in `.ondina/<pr>/<headSha>.json` (`.ondina/` in the original folder even when run inside a worktree. Worktree item in chapter 12). The target repository is the current directory
  - `analyze` options (5.3): `--no-llm` (skip the LLM), `--relabel` (call again instead of using saved LLM output `.ondina/<pr>/llm/<headSha>.json`), `--body <file>` (author's description in local mode. PR mode uses `gh pr view --json body`), `--title <題>` (local-mode title. 5.1), `--no-lsp` (do not launch the language server. 9.1), `--ticket <値>` (ticket key, URL, or file. 5.1. 2026-10-12)
  - `ondina structure [<ref>]` (2026-10-09): infer the entire repository structure again and overwrite `.ondina/structure.json` (10.6). `analyze` performs the initial inference automatically. Rerun `analyze` to reflect it on screen
  - `ondina serve [--port <n>] [--background] [<pr>]` (default 4177) runs with `node:http` alone, lists all analyses under `.ondina/`, and lets users switch in the UI (argument `<pr>` sets only the initial selection; the printed URL is `/pr/<number>` or `/local`. “Screen URLs” in 3.0). `--background` is for AI agents: launch serve detached, print its URL, and return immediately (chapter 12). API: `GET /api/repo` (`{ repo, pid, version, startedAt }`. Which repository serve belongs to; determines whether `--background` may reuse it. `version` is the short HEAD commit of ondina's clone at startup (absent outside a clone); `startedAt` is startup time, used by `serve list` (chapter 12). Both are absent in serve versions before 2026-10-23), `GET /api/analyses` (list, newest first, with `repoName` and GitHub `prUrl`), `GET /api/analyses/:key/:sha`, `GET` / `PUT /api/state/:key/:sha` (`ReviewState`. Only that PR's progress. Write to `.ondina/<key>/state/<sha>.json` via tmp → rename), `GET` / `PUT /api/ui` (`.ondina/ui.json`. `UiSettings`: `worktreeRoot` / `llmCommand` / `reviewCommand` / `theme` / `diffMode` / `keymap`. 400 if a `keymap` value is not a string array. Usable without analysis), `GET /api/config` (display-only part `DisplayConfig` of `ondina.config.yaml`. Read every time, so browser reload reflects edits. If unreadable, print to stderr and use defaults), `POST /api/comments` (chapter 12), `POST /api/comments/send/:key/:sha` (send adopted comments to GitHub. For `local`, return to AI. Chapter 12), `GET /api/github/viewed/:key/:sha` (`{ viewed: パス[] }`. Files marked Viewed in the GitHub PR) and `POST /api/github/viewed/:key/:sha` (mark / unmark `{ mark, unmark }`. If PR head differs from analysis, return 409 without sending. Both return 400 for local and 502 for gh failure. 12.1), `GET /api/ask/:key/:sha` (Ask the guide conversation. `.ondina/<key>/ask/<sha>.json`), `POST /api/ask/:key/:sha` (send `{ key, place, question }` to the LLM, return validated answer `AskEntry`, and append it to the record. `lines` in `place` is optional; malformed input gets 400. Failure gets 502 with reason. 3.10 / 10.5), `GET /api/latest/:key/:sha` (whether a newer head exists than this version. `LatestHead`: `headSha` / `newCommits` / `analyzed` / `changed`. Retrieval failure gets 502. 3.0), `GET /api/prs` (open PR list `PrList`. 3.11. “PR list” below. gh failure gets 502 with reason), `GET /api/prs/:n` (PR details `PrDetail`. 3.11. “PR list” below. gh failure gets 502 with reason; a non-number gets 404), `POST /api/analyze/:key/:sha` (reanalyze with that version's arguments. 202 with `AnalyzeJob`. If that key is queued or analyzing, 409 with the current job), `POST /api/analyze/:key` (analyze by PR number, from the PR list. Same response. 404 for `local`), `GET /api/analyze` (latest `AnalyzeJob[]` per key. Order: queued (queue order) → running → completed. `AnalyzeJob`: queued (`queuedAt`) / running (`startedAt` and latest progress `line`) / done (new `headSha`) / failed (`reason`). All use `from` for the original version being reanalyzed; absent for first analysis from the PR list. Empty means nothing is running), `POST /api/review/:n` (review `{ headSha }` with the AI review command. 202 with `ReviewJob`. If that PR is waiting for analysis or under review, 409 with the current job; empty command gets 400 with settings guidance; body other than `{ headSha }` (40-character sha) gets 400; a non-number gets 404. 3.11), `GET /api/review` (latest `ReviewJob[]` per PR. `ReviewJob`: waiting / running (`startedAt`) / done (`drafts` and `skipped`) / failed (`reason`) / stopped. All have `key` and `headSha`. “AI review” below), `DELETE /api/review/:n` (stop a review waiting for analysis or running. 200 with the stopped job. 404 if not active), `POST /api/lsp` (receive language server job `LspJobRequest` from analyze and obtain semantic tokens and call relationships using language servers kept running. Response is one JSON per line: several progress `{"progress"}` lines followed by `{"done"}`. Semantic-token Maps are `[パス, data]` pairs. Only this API allows a 200 MB body. 9.1. 2026-10-15). At startup, serve writes `.ondina/serve.json` (`{ pid, port }`) and removes it on exit (analyze uses it to find serve). Other requests serve `dist` from `packages/web`; unknown paths return `index.html` (SPA, so reloading a screen URL loads the UI). Without `dist`, return 503 with `pnpm build` guidance
  - Limit `key` to a PR number or `local`, and `sha` to 40 hexadecimal characters (validate their shape because they are used in paths)
  - `ondina serve list` / `serve stop <ポート>... | --all` / `serve restart <ポート>... | --all` (2026-10-23): list, stop, or restart running serve instances. Can run from any folder. Discovery and stopping are in chapter 12
  - **Reanalyze** (2026-10-13. 3.0): serve launches `ondina analyze` as a child process (`reanalyze.ts`. Same entry point as itself: `process.execPath` + `process.execArgv` + `process.argv[1]`). It does not call within serve because synchronous git and tree-sitter processing would block requests such as saving. Run one at a time; queue the rest (2026-10-22. Previously only one could run, and no requests were accepted while running). Two cannot run concurrently because all PRs use the same worktrees when worktreeRoot is configured, and also compete for `git fetch` FETCH_HEAD. Keep one latest job per key; reject requests for an already queued or running key (409). On completion (success or failure), start the head of the queue (`createAnalyzeRunner` in `reanalyze.ts`). Build arguments from the current version: for PRs, `analyze <n>` (preserve the same value if a ticket was specified with `--ticket`; do not pass tickets found in titles/bodies or files); for `local`, `--base <pr.base> --head <pr.head> --title <pr.title> --body .ondina/local/reanalyze-body.md` (write and pass the saved body. For working-tree analysis, omit `--head` and reanalyze the current working tree). Use CLI defaults for the language server and LLM. Progress is the last line of the child's stderr. `analyze` prints one line at stage starts (retrieve PR information, prepare base/head code, extract diff, infer structure, launch language server, AI groups by purpose, AI writes guidance) and while waiting for the language server (also visible in the terminal). On completion, read headSha from the last stdout line (the written Analysis.json). Failure reason is the last stderr line
  - **PR list** (2026-10-22. 3.11): get the repository host and owner / name with `gh repo view --json url` (remember within serve; retry next time on failure), then call `gh api graphql --hostname <host>` once (`pr-list.ts`). Retrieve the 100 most recently updated open PRs and logged-in user (viewer) in the same query. Only fields supported by GitHub Enterprise Server (labels, requested people and teams, reviews, conversation comment authors, review thread counts, latest-commit checks). Do not use `gh pr list --json`: it fetches all bodies and comments, making 100 items heavy, and cannot retrieve viewer. Per-person status comes from `prsFromGraphql`: process reviews oldest first; approval and change requests overwrite, dismissal (DISMISSED) clears the opinion, drafts (PENDING) are ignored. Without an opinion, a review or conversation comment gives commented; requested people who have not reviewed get requested (teams use their name and `team`). Exclude authors and bots (type Bot or login ending in `[bot]`). Comment count totals conversation comments and review threads. Checks: SUCCESS → passed, FAILURE / ERROR → failed, PENDING / EXPECTED → running
    - **PR details** (`GET /api/prs/:n`. `pr-detail.ts`): call `gh api graphql` once at the same destination as the list (number passed as `-F number=<n>`). Get `pullRequest(number:)` body, `timelineItems(itemTypes: [ISSUE_COMMENT, PULL_REQUEST_REVIEW, PULL_REQUEST_COMMIT], last: 100)` (reviews include line comments `comments(first: 10)`), `files(first: 100)`, and latest-commit `statusCheckRollup.contexts(first: 50)` (CheckRun and StatusContext). Derive the older timeline count from `filteredCount` (count after filtering by `itemTypes`; `totalCount` also counts label additions/removals, etc.). Use `last`, not `first`, to retain recent items in long PRs. Read with pure function `prDetailFromGraphql`: omit draft reviews (PENDING), merge consecutive commits into one entry, use `line` for line comments (fall back to `originalLine`), show unfinished CheckRun as running, TIMED_OUT / STARTUP_FAILURE as failed, and no duration for skipped checks. StatusContext ERROR is failed
  - **AI review (「AI レビュー」)** (2026-10-22. 3.11): serve launches `UiSettings.reviewCommand` with `sh -c` per PR (`createReviewRunner` in `review.ts`. Pass AbortSignal and environment variables to `runLlmCommand`). cwd is the repository, stdin is the instructions, and environment variables are `ONDINA_PR` / `ONDINA_BASE_SHA` / `ONDINA_HEAD_SHA` (leave others as is). Abort after 30 minutes. Separate from the analysis queue; any number may run concurrently (the reviewer pressed the button). Keep one latest job per PR; reject requests while waiting for analysis or reviewing (409). Jobs exist only in serve memory and disappear on restart (drafts remain in state)
    - Instructions are in core's `review/prompt.ts` (`renderReviewPrompt`. Plain Japanese style). Same rules as steps 2–3 of the `ondina-comments` skill: PR number, title, base/head sha; read with `git diff <base> <head>` and `git show <sha>:<path>` using line numbers at the head sha (not the current checkout or the PR's latest head); only lines appearing in the diff (inside `@@` blocks); one line per comment; `side` and `line`; start the body with `[must]` / `[imo]` / `[ask]`; Japanese; one point per comment; output only one JSON object from chapter 12 (`headSha` is the requested head). Do not send to GitHub, modify files, or run ondina commands. Examples use a different subject (inventory allocation)
    - Starting: if analysis for that PR is queued or running, wait for completion (waiting. `AnalyzeRunner.finished`). Otherwise, run immediately if an analysis exists for the requested head. If absent, queue analysis first and wait (reanalyze with previous-head arguments if available, inheriting `--ticket`; otherwise `analyze <n>`). Once analysis completes, review the analyzed head (the newer one if head advanced during analysis). If analysis fails, fail with “Could not analyze: <reason>” (「解析できませんでした: <理由>」)
    - On completion, read stdout as chapter 12 JSON (core `extractJson` strips surrounding prose and code fences. Supply `headSha` if absent; fail if it differs from the requested head). Exclude only comments on lines absent from GitHub's diff (`onGithubDiff`), then put the rest into that PR key's analysis with `addComments` (not the local analysis of the same head). Store the excluded count in `skipped`. `ondina comments add` rejects the entire input if even one line is absent from the diff (the AI can correct and resubmit). This job is one-shot and cannot resubmit, so add those that can be added. Empty stdout with exit code 0 is not failure (the agent may have run `ondina comments add` itself)
    - For draft count (`drafts`), every `GET /api/review` counts AI drafts (`source` is not reviewer, and status is `draft` / `edit` / `adopted`) in that head's state
    - Failure reasons: for nonzero exit, the last stderr line (if absent, “Exited with code n” (「終了コード n で終了しました」)); timeout, “Did not finish within 30 minutes” (「30 分で終わりませんでした」); missing JSON, “No JSON object in stdout (start of output: …)” (「stdout に JSON オブジェクトがありません（出力の先頭: …）」) (up to 120 characters. Agents sometimes respond in prose instead of JSON; environments without copy and paste cannot paste output from outside serve); wrong shape, “Not in comment JSON format (source / headSha / comments[])” (「コメント JSON の形式ではありません（source / headSha / comments[]）」)
    - Stopping: during review, stop the command's entire process group. While waiting for analysis, do not stop analysis. Completions (command or analysis) arriving after stopping do not alter the job
    - On 2026-10-22, reviewed fixture PR 1 twice from serve with a real agent (`claude -p --model sonnet --allowedTools "Bash(git diff:*),Bash(git show:*),Read,Grep,Glob"`). The first attempt took about 95 seconds and resulted in “No JSON object in stdout” (output was not retained; this led to adding the output prefix to the reason). Manually passing the same instructions returned only JSON with six comments. The second attempt took about 70 seconds and added seven drafts (zero lines absent from the diff). Responses vary even with identical instructions
  - For newer heads, use asynchronous `gh pr view <n> --json headRefOid,commits` for PRs (queried every two minutes, so synchronous `runGh` must not block serve), and `git rev-parse <pr.head>` plus `git rev-list --count` for `local`. For working-tree analysis, turn the current working tree into a tree using the same exclusions as analyze (do not count commits. Banner: “The working tree has new changes” (「作業ツリーに新しい変更があります」)). `changed` is the count of new hunks and hunks whose IDs or `lines` differ between analyses (core `changedHunks`)
  - **Carrying state forward**: if `GET /api/state` finds no state file for that version, create and write one with core `carryReview` from the newest version with a state file under the same key (`readOrCarryState` in `store.ts`). Do not rewrite the previous version's state. Waiting until this point instead of creating it when reanalysis completes also carries forward marks made on the old version while the notice waits. `ondina comments add` follows the same path (even if AI adds drafts to the new version first, carry forward before adding). Details in 3.0 and chapter 12
  - Do not create worktrees for analysis (2026-10-23). Previously, detached worktrees were created and retained per PR in `.ondina/<pr>/worktrees/{base,head}`, accumulating one full repository copy per analyzed PR. They were retained because M2 kotlin-lsp needs on-disk trees, but the language server has used a separate pair since 2026-10-15; per-PR worktrees were only used to read contents. Leave `.ondina/<pr>/worktrees` created by previous versions in place (ondina does not touch them). Keep only one pair for the language server (`<worktreeRoot か .ondina>/lsp/{base,head}`. UI setting in 3.8), switching contents with checkout instead of recreating them (9.1. 2026-10-15). Put saved project structures (`.ondina/lsp/models/`) beside them in `json/{base,head}` and pass them to the language server (9.1. 2026-10-19). Users add `.ondina/` to `.gitignore` or `.git/info/exclude`
  - At M0, `Analysis` fields `hows` / `symbols` / `edges` / `tours` / `specs` / `claims` are empty; `hunks[].kind` is `'none'` (`kindProvenance: 'ast'`). M1 fills `hows` / `hunk.hows` / `claims` / `summary` and `kind` (chapter 8 and 5.4 item 4). `symbols` / `edges` / `tours` come in M1d / M2 onward; `specs` on 2026-10-12 (10.8)
- **Install by cloning** (2026-10-08): clone and build on the PC where it will run. Runs on Mac; reviewed PRs are on github.com or GitHub Enterprise Server. Do not publish packaged GitHub releases, since that requires packaging infrastructure. npm distribution is handled separately as team distribution (`npx ondina`. Chapter 13)
  - `scripts/install.sh [<版>]` handles initial installation and updates in six stages
    1. Prerequisites: git, Node 22 or newer, pnpm 12 or newer
    2. Clone state: stop if tracked files are modified. Also stop on any named branch other than `main` (prevent accidental execution in a development clone)
    3. Retrieve: `git fetch`, then detached checkout of `<版>` (default `origin/main`)
    4. Build: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` and `pnpm build`
    5. Command: make `packages/cli/dist/index.js` executable and create an `ondina` symlink in `ONDINA_BIN_DIR` (default `~/.local/bin`). In the same stage, write `ondina completion zsh` output to `${ZDOTDIR:-$HOME}/.zsh_functions/_ondina` for Tab completion (regenerated on every version update, so the list stays current). Also replace AI agent skills (chapter 12), copying the retrieved version's `skills/<名前>/` to `~/.agents/skills/<名前>/` (read by both Codex and OpenCode. Do not touch other skills. When reverting to a version without `skills/`, leave installed skills in place). This stage runs the installed version's install.sh, so the first `ondina update` to a version that installs skills does not install them; the next `ondina update` does
    6. kotlin-lsp (2026-10-09): run the retrieved version's `scripts/install-kotlin-lsp.sh` (kotlin-lsp item below). Continue on failure. If absent in that version (when reverting with `update <古い版>`), print “This version does not install kotlin-lsp” (「この版は kotlin-lsp を入れません」)
  - At the end, print “Updated ondina from <before> → <after> (date)” (「ondina を <前> → <後> にしました（日付）」) and added or removed commits (up to 10). If the location is absent from PATH, print one line to add to `~/.zshenv` (changed from `~/.zshrc` on 2026-10-19. Only interactive zsh reads `~/.zshrc`; noninteractive zsh used by AI agents to run commands (Codex uses `zsh -lc`) cannot find ondina)
  - Finally, launch the user's normal zsh (`zsh -i -c`) and check whether ondina is registered for completion (`$_comps[ondina]`) (2026-10-12). If unregistered, print a `source <置き場>/_ondina` line to append to `.zshrc`. If compinit itself has not run, also print `autoload -Uz compinit && compinit`. If zsh does not respond (abort after 20 seconds if perl is available), print “Could not verify” (「確かめられませんでした」) and the line to use when completion fails. Do not check if zsh is absent
    - Previously, checked whether `.zshrc` contained the text `.zsh_functions`; if absent, printed one line to add it to fpath. prezto runs compinit at the start of `.zshrc` and reuses the completion list for 20 hours. The later fpath line therefore had no effect, and the existing text suppressed guidance (happened 2026-10-12). A final `source` runs `compdef` after compinit, independent of line order and reused lists
    - This check also runs from the installed version's install.sh, so the first `ondina update` to this version prints the old guidance. Checking begins with the next `ondina update`
  - Failures: if retrieval fails, print guidance to check the network and stop. If the build fails, print a command to revert to the previous version and stop
  - Wrap the main body in a function and call it at the end. This survives checkout rewriting the script itself (git checkout recreates files, so running sh reads the old file through to the end. Verified inode changes on 2026-10-09). Write variables in strings as `${name}`. In UTF-8 environments, macOS `/bin/sh` (bash 3.2) includes full-width characters immediately following `$name` in the variable name
  - The installed version's script runs, so if it is broken, `update` cannot proceed (the full-width character issue above actually occurred on 2026-10-08). In that case, retrieve with git again, then run directly (README “Troubleshooting”)
  - `ondina update [<版>]` runs `scripts/install.sh` from its own clone with input/output connected directly. Clone location comes from `selfRoot()` in `self.ts`, three levels above `dist/`
  - `ondina --version` prints `ondina <sha7> (<日付>)`. Outside a clone root, `ondina (バージョン不明)`. Identify versions by git commit instead of package.json version (still 0.0.0). Updates fetch the latest main, avoiding version-number maintenance
  - Do not include kotlin-lsp in ondina's distribution. `scripts/install-kotlin-lsp.sh` downloads directly from JetBrains into its installation directory; ondina finds `current` there itself (2026-10-09. Automatic discovery since 2026-10-19; previously passed through `ONDINA_LSP_COMMAND`). Do not use the Homebrew cask, which installs a different version and the deprecated `kotlin-lsp.sh` wrapper. Steps in README “Install”
    - It is a trial build with an expiry per version. About 30 days after release, it prints `This build of kotlin-server has expired.` and stops launching (263.4702.0 expired on 2026-10-09). Official documentation gives no number of days. Releases are 10–48 days apart, so even the latest version may be expired for a period. Then the only option is to wait for a new version
    - Installation directory: `ONDINA_LSP_DIR` (default `~/.local/share/kotlin-lsp`). Extract into `kotlin-server-<版>/` and symlink `current` → the version to use. ondina launches `…/current/bin/intellij-server`, so version changes require no edits. If `ONDINA_LSP_COMMAND` points elsewhere, it takes precedence; print guidance to remove it
    - Until 2026-10-19, guidance said to append `export ONDINA_LSP_COMMAND=…` to `~/.zshrc`. AI agents (Codex) run commands in noninteractive zsh (`zsh -lc`), which does not read `~/.zshrc`. The language server did not launch when an agent started outside a terminal ran `ondina analyze`. Do not ask users to write it in `~/.zshenv`: that leaves manual file editing and does not reach agents running bash
    - Find the version to install in the ondina clone's `scripts/kotlin-lsp-version` (one line containing the version number). Every morning at 6 AM Japan time, GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/kotlin-lsp-version.yml`) reads the latest release (`tag_name` of `releases/latest`, `kotlin-lsp/v<版>`). If JetBrains has both Apple Silicon and Intel `.sit` and `.sha256` files, update the file and commit to main. Download only if `kotlin-server-<版>/bin/intellij-server` is absent from the installation directory (about 360 MB per version). Manually installed versions are also used as is
    - Do not query github.com APIs or pages. Initially, unauthenticated requests to `api.github.com` were used, but some environments returned 403 for both `api.github.com` and `github.com/…/releases/latest` (2026-10-09). Environments using only GitHub Enterprise Server may not reach github.com. The version number arrives through the same route as ondina itself (`git fetch`), so any PC that can update ondina also receives the version. Only download.jetbrains.com is accessed externally
    - Do not use JetBrains distribution metadata (`data.services.jetbrains.com/products/releases?code=ILSKS`). As of 2026-10-09, it had stopped updating at 263.2689.0 (2026-08-03) and lacked the latest release (263.6379.0)
    - Download `https://download.jetbrains.com/language-server/kotlin-server/<版>/kotlin-server-<版>-aarch64.sit` (Apple Silicon. Omit `-aarch64` for Intel). Compare against the adjacent `.sit.sha256` (`<hash> *<ファイル名>`), then extract with `ditto -x -k`. Downloads made with curl have no quarantine flag
    - After replacing, remove `kotlin-server-*` from the installation directory (old versions and manually downloaded `.sit` files). Older versions expire first, so keeping them provides no fallback (about 1.1 GB per extracted version). Do not touch other files
    - Result: “Updated kotlin-lsp from <before> → <after> (<date> build)” (「kotlin-lsp を <前> → <後> にしました（<日付> の版）」) or “kotlin-lsp is up to date (<version>, <date> build)” (「kotlin-lsp は最新です（<版>、<日付> の版）」). Date comes from `majorVersionReleaseDate` in extracted `product-info.json`
    - If the version number cannot be read, download fails, SHA-256 mismatches, or extraction fails, print the reason and “Will keep using the current version (…)” (「今の版（…）のまま使います」), then exit nonzero. Remove partial files. install.sh continues (finish updating ondina itself)
    - Do nothing outside Mac (currently supports Mac only)
    - Keep it separate from install.sh because install.sh runs from the installed version, while this runs from the retrieved version. Fixes to retrieval or `kotlin-lsp-version` take effect in that update. Can also run alone with `sh scripts/install-kotlin-lsp.sh`
- **CI**: run `analyze` on PR open / synchronize, and store output as artifacts or on S3-equivalent storage. Initially, LSP startup with Gradle import was considered too heavy to run on reviewers' machines, but CI does not exist yet and reviewers run it locally. Without local LSP startup, maps have no lines, so local startup became the default on 2026-10-09 (9.1)
- **UI**: static SPA (React + Vite, `packages/web`). Reads `Analysis.json` and `ReviewState` through the API. Save `ReviewState` immediately with `PUT /api/state` on every action (no browser storage. Decided 2026-09-16). Share types in `packages/model` (types-only package) across core / cli / web, preserving the rule that web does not import core (18.5)

---

## 6. Technology choices

| Area | Choice | Reason / alternatives |
|---|---|---|
| Implementation language (CLI, pipeline, UI) | TypeScript (Node 22) | Shiki is JS; tree-sitter is convenient through web-tree-sitter (wasm); LSP client uses `vscode-jsonrpc`; types can be shared with the UI. Alternative: Kotlin/JVM (jtreesitter + lsp4j). A Kotlin implementation would share types with the UI through JSON Schema |
| Parsing | web-tree-sitter + `@tree-sitter-grammars/tree-sitter-kotlin` (bundled wasm) | Declaration-level extraction and AST normalization. Sufficient because type resolution is unnecessary. The fwcd npm package does not bundle wasm (native bindings only), so use tree-sitter-grammars, which does. Node names are nearly identical; verified that all syntax in the sample PR parses. Alternative: build the fwcd version with emscripten and vendor it |
| Semantic analysis | JetBrains official Kotlin Language Server (kotlin-lsp) | Same analysis engine as IntelliJ IDEA. Alpha. Supports Gradle / Maven JVM projects; Android Gradle Plugin is experimental; KMP unsupported. Obtain from Homebrew cask `kotlin-lsp` or JetBrains download CDN |
| Highlighting | Shiki + official Catppuccin themes (bundle `catppuccin-latte/frappe/macchiato/mocha`) | Do not write colors manually. Map semantic tokens to scopes using VS Code's default mapping, and color with the same theme |
| Layout | elkjs (layered) | Course maps. Specializes in layered layout. Render with custom SVG (React Flow unnecessary) |
| UI | React + Vite, CSS variables (Catppuccin) | Prototype uses plain JS. Componentize in the implementation. No Tailwind (design tokens live in CSS variables) |
| LLM | External LLM command (launch with `sh -c`, prompt on stdin, JSON on stdout. Default `claude -p`; replaceable with Codex / OpenCode, etc. 5.3) | One labeling call is sufficient. No vendor-specific SDK (decided 2026-09-17. Avoid limiting to Claude; allow subscription use). Inspect output shape in Stage 3 |
| GitHub | REST (PR metadata, posting review comments), GraphQL (view synchronization with `markFileAsViewed` / `unmarkFileAsViewed`. Files only. 12.1. Implemented 2026-10-13) | GitHub has no hunk-level viewed state, so maintain it ourselves |
| IDE integration | `jetbrains://` URL scheme (Toolbox) | Future: leave a path to an IntelliJ plugin displaying the same UI in JCEF (chapter 14) |
| Persistence | JSON files (`.ondina/`), SQLite if needed | Start as a personal tool |

---

## 7. Highlighting specification (Catppuccin / IDEA conventions)

### 7.1 Verified facts (when building the prototype)
- `editor.background` / `editor.foreground` in Shiki's four bundled Catppuccin themes match `base` / `text` in the official palette
- Tokenizing the same Kotlin code in all four flavors maps every token to the same role (mauve / blue / yellow / teal …). Zero mismatches. **Store role names rather than colors in tokens, so switching flavors requires only replacing CSS variables**
- Determined lexically (TextMate): keywords=mauve, function names=blue (italic), type declarations=yellow (italic), operators=teal, strings=green, numbers=peach, comments=overlay2 (italic)
- Not determined lexically (determined in IDEA): class **references** (`DiscountPolicy` in `DiscountPolicy.rateFor`), parameters (maroon), constants (`ZERO` in `Money.ZERO`), and the distinction between properties and local variables

**How much lexical and semantic tokens overlap** (measured 2026-09-22 across two repositories: base / head of `fixtures/order-service` and `hands-on-server-side-kotlin` (Spring Boot, 16 files, 652 lines)). Of 2775 identifiers covered by semantic tokens, lexical tokens already colored 814:

| Relationship | Count | Details |
|---|---|---|
| Lexical and semantic agree | 727 | `blue→method` 301 / `yellow→class` 286 / `blue→function` 58 / `yellow→interface` 36 / `yellow→struct` 35 / `yellow→enum` 6 / `yellow→type` 5 |
| **Semantic is correct** | **19** | `mauve→property` 15 (`value`) / `mauve→parameter` 2 / `green→variable` 1 (`actual`) / `green→class` 1 (`Instant`) |
| **Lexical is correct** | **68** | `yellow→method` (annotation use. kotlin-lsp returns `@Test` as `method`) |

The 19 locations where semantic is correct fall into only two categories: **confusion with Kotlin soft keywords** (colors `value` in `order.id.value` as a keyword because `value class` exists), and **inside string templates `${...}`** (colors `actual` in `"原因: ${actual.value}"` as a string). In both, lexical analysis misreads an identifier as “not an identifier.”

### 7.2 Pipeline
```
Shiki.codeToTokensBase(code, {lang, theme:'catppuccin-mocha'})   → 字句トークン [text, role, fontStyle]
kotlin-lsp textDocument/semanticTokens/full                        → 意味トークン (line, col, len, type, modifiers)
重ね合わせ: 字句が塗っていない所と、字句が非識別子の役割を塗った所だけ、type/modifiers から role を決める
```
Obtain semantic tokens for **both base / head** (use base for `-` diff lines and head for `+` lines). Overlay per file first, then pass to hunk extraction (the existing path that walks `lnOld` / `lnNew`).

Semantic token → role mapping (finalized 2026-09-22). Derived from attribute assignments in JetBrains Catppuccin (`templates/editor.tera` in `catppuccin/jetbrains`. The source for theme XML writes role names directly, as `{{ yellow.hex }}`). **Three entries changed from the first version: property / operator / decorator**.

| LSP token type | role | italic | Original IntelliJ attribute |
|---|---|---|---|
| class, enum, struct, interface | yellow | Declarations only | `DEFAULT_CLASS_NAME` (`FONT_TYPE=2`) / `DEFAULT_CLASS_REFERENCE` (no italic) / `DEFAULT_INTERFACE_NAME` |
| type, typeParameter | yellow | No | `DEFAULT_CLASS_REFERENCE` / `KOTLIN_TYPE_PARAMETER` |
| method, function | blue | Yes | `DEFAULT_FUNCTION_DECLARATION` / `DEFAULT_FUNCTION_CALL` / `DEFAULT_STATIC_METHOD` |
| parameter | maroon | Yes | `DEFAULT_PARAMETER` |
| property | text (teal if `static`) | No | `DEFAULT_INSTANCE_FIELD` / `DEFAULT_STATIC_FIELD` |
| variable | text | No | `DEFAULT_LOCAL_VARIABLE` |
| enumMember | peach | No | `KOTLIN_ENUM_ENTRY` |
| decorator | yellow | Yes | `DEFAULT_METADATA` |
| namespace | Inherited color (null) | No | No corresponding attribute |
| operator, keyword, modifier, comment, string, number, regexp | **Unused** | — | Lexical is authoritative (below) |

**Corrections from the first version**: property is **text**, not lavender; operator is **sky**, not teal; annotations are **yellow + italic**.

**What kotlin-lsp 263.4702.0 actually returns** (measured 2026-09-21–22):
- **Not a single keyword / string / number / comment / modifier / regexp token**. No conflict with the lexical layer
- **Only `*` and `-` operators; no `=`**. Overriding would split operators on the same line into sky (JetBrains) and teal (Shiki), so omit them from the mapping
- **Annotation uses (`@Test` / `@RestController`) return as `method`**. Only the `import` line gets `decorator`. IDEA colors uses with `DEFAULT_METADATA`, so **kotlin-lsp gets this wrong**
- Constructor `val` / `var` properties are `parameter [declaration, readonly]`. **Even `var` gets `readonly`**, so it cannot identify `val`
- `Money.ZERO` is `property [readonly]` without `static`. Cannot distinguish constants (teal / peach)
- `enum` / `enumMember` appear in fixture `Logger.kt` (`Level` and `INFO` / `WARN` / `ERROR`). Verified `typeParameter` with a trial file using generics (no italic in either declarations or uses)
- **`property` never gets `static`**. Even `const val` in `companion object` returns `property [declaration, readonly]` (measured 2026-09-22). No route to `DEFAULT_STATIC_FIELD` (teal), so constants become `text`
- **`object` declarations return `type [declaration]`, not `class`**. The `type` role has no italic, so only `object` declarations have one fewer italic than IDEA

### Overlay rules (option 3 + exception. Implemented in `packages/core/src/highlight/semantic.ts`)

For ranges covered by semantic tokens, decide based on the **lexical token role**:

1. Lexical tokens **apply no color** (`role === null`) → apply the mapped role and italic
2. Lexical tokens apply a **non-identifier role** (keyword mauve / string green / comment overlay2 / number peach / operator teal / literal red) → apply the mapping (**exception**)
3. Lexical tokens apply an **identifier role** (yellow / blue / maroon / text / lavender …) → **keep lexical**

These six non-identifier roles are exactly the lexical categories in 7.1. Shiki never applies them to identifiers, so when a semantic token says “this is an identifier,” the lexical misreading is established.

**Why this approach was chosen** (compared using actual counts from `hands-on-server-side-kotlin`):

| | Match JetBrains entirely | Override role only (lexical italic) | **This approach** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Annotations (50 locations) | blue ✗ | blue ✗ | **yellow ✓** |
| Type-reference italic (67 locations) | Removed ✗ | Retained ✓ | **Retained ✓** |
| Argument italic (99 locations) | Added ✓ | Absent ✗ | **Added ✓** |
| Soft-keyword misidentification (19 locations) | Fixed ✓ | Fixed ✓ | **Fixed ✓** |

**Only this approach matches IDEA on all four.** The other two each diverge from IDEA elsewhere. As a side effect, “launching LSP never removes visible colors (only adds them)” holds, minimizing differences from analysis without LSP (`--no-lsp`).

**Golden files**: `fixtures/order-service/expected/tokens-lsp.json` (after overlay). Leave `expected/tokens.json` (lexical only) unchanged to verify that the non-LSP path's output stays the same. Recorded semantic tokens are in `fixtures/order-service/lsp/semantic-tokens.json` (raw `data` captured from a real server for 36 head / 34 base files), so tests run without a real server.

`TokenLine = Array<[text: string, role: string | null, fontStyle: number]>`. A null role means inherited color.

Lexical token implementation (2026-09-16, `packages/core/src/highlight/`):
- **Process whole files**. Pass before / after through Shiki once each, then retrieve each hunk line using `lnOld` / `lnNew` (`' '` and `'-'` from before, `'+'` from after). Passing fragments would break hunks starting inside block comments or multiline strings. `tokens[i]` corresponds to `lines[i]` without its prefix; mismatched lines fall back to one inherited-color token (the displayed string always comes from `lines`)
- **Look up role names from hex colors**. At runtime, tokenize with mocha and map mocha hex values from `@catppuccin/palette` back to color names. `packages/core/test/highlight.fixture.test.ts` verifies matching roles across all four flavors for every fixture file
- **The theme's default foreground (palette `text`) is `null`**. The prototype's `TOK` writes `"text"`, but `tokHtml` adds no class for falsy roles, giving the same rendering. This distinguishes it from explicitly setting `variable → text` in the semantic overlay
- **Do not attach `tokens` for languages unsupported by Shiki** (leave `Hunk.tokens?`). The UI falls back to plain text, like prototype `lineHtml`. Use Shiki's `fontStyle` bits as is (italic 1 / bold 2 / underline 4), with 0 when unset
- **Load embedded languages with their parent** (2026-10-14). Shiki grammars have lazily loaded embedded languages (`embeddedLangsLazy`) that are not loaded with the parent (Vue `<style lang="scss">` / `<template lang="pug">`, Markdown fences, etc.). Without loading them, those sections lack colors, and results also vary depending on whether a `.scss` file in the same PR was processed first (principle 7). When `tokenizer.ts` loads a language, always load its Shiki-bundled `embeddedLangsLazy` languages too. Follow the chain fully, since embedded languages may themselves load others lazily (markdown → pug → stylus). vue embeds markdown, so vue / svelte / markdown / mdx load around 60 grammars. Analysis of a fixture containing `.vue` took less than two extra seconds (measured 2026-10-14)
- Golden file: `fixtures/order-service/expected/tokens.json` (hunk ID → `TokenLine[]`, one TokenLine per line)
- Pin `shiki` and `@catppuccin/palette` versions (TextMate grammar or palette updates could change tokens for the same headSha. Principle 7). `Tokenizer.dispose()` releases wasm; a Tokenizer created by `attachTokens` is disposed by it (prevent wasm accumulation in long-running `serve`)

### 7.3 Diff lines
Keep token colors on diff lines; change only the background to `color-mix(green 18%, base)` / `color-mix(red 18%, base)`. Line-number gutters use mantle; added/deleted line gutters use a darker 28% mix. Hatch lines with no opposite counterpart (repeating-linear-gradient).

---

## 8. Follow-on group detection algorithm

Input: all hunks (with before/after ASTs). Output: `RippleGroup[]` and each hunk's `kind`. Implemented in `packages/core/src/ripple/` (2026-09-17. `detectRipples`. `analyze` calls it after `toHunk`, before highlighting).

1. **Core candidates**: hunks containing declaration additions, deletions, or signature changes are core candidates. For each, collect removed declaration names R and added declaration names A (example: R = {promo, getDiscount}, A = {discount, applyDiscount}). Implementation: `candidates.ts`:
