   - Declaration units match 5.2, plus **primary constructor `val` / `var` parameters** (`promo` in `class Order(val promo: Promo?)`. Absent from the declaration tree in 5.2, so counted only here. DTO field additions also become candidates)
   - Signature change = a declaration with the same name on both sides whose word sequence differs after excluding the body (function body, class body, or property after `=`). Put the name in both R and A. Primary constructor parameter additions/removals count as individual parameter additions/deletions, not class signature changes
   - File hunks (imports, etc.) are not candidates. **Core candidates cannot be follow-ons to one another** (`execute()` references `applyDiscount`, but is itself a candidate, so it is not a follow-on to `Order`)
   - **Tests cannot be follow-ons**: identify tests (5.2 item 7), set their hunks to `kind: 'test'` (`kindProvenance: 'config'` if configured, `'llm'` if inferred from structure), and exclude them from both core candidates and follow-ons
2. **Collect referencing hunks**: among other hunks, select those with an R identifier in deleted lines (syntax-tree `identifier` leaves; not string contents) and an A identifier in added lines as follow-on candidates for that core. A hunk matching multiple cores is assigned to the first
3. **Normalize shapes** (implementation `tokens.ts`): turn syntax-tree leaves in deleted/added lines of follow-on candidates into word sequences (whitespace, newlines, and comments disappear here). Replace identifiers outside R∪A with placeholders (`$1`, `$2`…) in occurrence order across old and new sides (do not replace `it`, an implicit argument). Normalize Kotlin-specific variations only for rules enabled by `normalize.kotlin`:
   - `safeCall`: read `?.` as `.` (implemented)
   - `lambdaIt`: read a one-argument lambda `{ p -> p.x }` as `{ it.x }` (implemented)
   - `trailingComma`: drop commas immediately before closing brackets (implemented. Whitespace, newlines, and import ordering disappear when converted to word sequences)
   - `namedArgOrder`: reorder named arguments in declaration order (**unsupported**. Requires the callee's declaration order, so after M2 LSP. If configured, `analyze` prints “Unsupported” (「未対応」) to stderr)
4. **Shapes and grouping** (implementation `shape.ts` / `detect.ts`): the “shape” is the sequence of only removed (`-`) and added (`+`) words from the diff (Myers) of old and new word sequences (example: `-getDiscount -( -) +discount`. Surrounding code does not appear in the diff, so `return order.getDiscount()` and `return items.sumOf { it.price } - order.getDiscount()` have the same shape). A word-sequence approximation of the “edit script” in the design. Diff alignment can change the shape (for example, `()` in `getDiscount()` aligns with another `()`), but it is deterministic. Use the **most frequent shape** for the same core as the bundle shape (first encountered on ties), and its first hunk (path order) as representative. `uniform: 'ast'`
5. **Deviations**: put candidates referencing the same core but differing in shape into `deviations`. Keep `kind` as `ripple` (display as part of the group). Record each deviation's **distance from the representative shape** (word-level edit distance) in `distance`; put those with **distance ≤ 2 into `near` (near deviations)**. The current method cannot determine whether near deviations “really deviate, or merely reflect coarse word-sequence comparison.” If near deviations increase, replace the shape function with tree edit distance or send them to the LLM fallback in 6 (shapes are strings, so callers need not change)
6. **LLM fallback** (M4): for candidates whose shapes still differ after normalization, but whose changed-line count is within ±1 of the representative and whose R/A reference positions match, ask Stage 2 whether their intent matches the representative. If so, add to members and downgrade the group's `uniform` to `'llm'`
7. **Pattern strings**: use only R / A names actually referenced by bundle hunks (`Order`'s R also contains `promo`, but no one references it, so `getDiscount() → discount`). Functions have `()`, properties use names alone. `${R.join(', ')} → ${A.join(', ')}`. If R is empty, `+${A}` (additions only, such as DTO fields)
8. **kind and weight**: only core candidates with bundles get `kind: 'core'` (`kindProvenance: 'ast'`). Candidates without bundles (new classes, added parameters, DTO fields) stay `none`; Stage 2 selects from the candidate list. Every hunk in a bundle (representative, member, deviation) has `kind: 'ripple'` and the same `group`. `n` is 1 for all hunks (view members individually too. 3.7)
9. **Exception table**: enable normalization rules through `normalize` in `ondina.config.yaml`. Initially assume exceptions are added manually. Unknown rule names are printed to stderr by `analyze`, without stopping it
10. **Output summary**: `analyze` prints one stderr line, `追従グループ n、逸脱 n、近い逸脱 n`. Golden file: `fixtures/order-service/expected/ripples.json` (1 core / 23 follow-ons / 7 tests / 17 unattributed. One bundle: 22 members, 1 deviation = `PromoService.apply` (distance 13), 0 near deviations)

---

## 9. LSP integration specification

### 9.1 Startup (verified on a real server and implemented 2026-09-21)

Implemented in `packages/core/src/lsp/` (`protocol.ts` framing / `ready.ts` readiness detection / `client.ts` JSON-RPC / `targets.ts` reference symbols. All pure processing independent of processes) and `packages/cli/src/lsp.ts` (launch, progress, termination). Same division as `llm.ts`: “core decides / cli launches.”

**What to launch**: **one instance only**. Put both base / head into `workspaceFolders` to serve both (`rootUri` and child process `cwd` use head). **Launch by default**. Skip with `--no-lsp` (2026-10-09. Previously launched only with `--lsp`. Changed because this was one cause of missing map lines in real repositories. Accept `--lsp` as the former spelling). If serve is running, use its retained language server, so preparation takes almost no wait from the second PR onward (“Keep running and reuse” below. 2026-10-15). Otherwise launch and close for each run (48 seconds on a small repository on the development machine. The language-server worktree locations stay the same, so indexing is faster after the first run).

**When yaml has no `lsp.<language>`** (2026-10-09): kotlin has a default launch method (`args: ["--stdio", "--system-path", "{cacheDir}"]`, `readyTimeoutSec: 600`. Same as fixture yaml. `defaultLspConfig` in `packages/core/src/config/load.ts`). Resolve the command from environment variable `ONDINA_LSP_COMMAND`, falling back to `current/bin/intellij-server` in kotlin-lsp's installation directory (`ONDINA_LSP_DIR`, default `~/.local/share/kotlin-lsp`) (installation directory since 2026-10-19. 5.5). If absent everywhere, do not launch; record `unavailable`, how to fix it, and searched locations.

**Evidence against separate base instances** (measured 2026-09-22): base-side semantic tokens obtained with two `workspaceFolders` **matched completely in position, type, and modifiers** with those from a separate base-only instance. Index remained 170 MB (two instances used 170 + 169 MB); readiness took 13.4 seconds (two: 21 + 13.7 seconds). Shared library indexing makes base's added cost almost zero. No issue even when both worktrees' `settings.gradle.kts` used the same `rootProject.name`.

**`--multi-client` is unnecessary**. This socket-mode option keeps the server running after the first client disconnects; it is unrelated to `--stdio`.

**Work after readiness**: immediately after stage 3 passes, before sending `shutdown`, call `work(client, legend)` (second argument of `runLsp`). Retrieve everything available only while the server is alive here (semantic tokens, call edges, references, definitions). Every path goes through shutdown, so the IntelliJ server (xmx 2 GB) cannot be left behind by design. If `work` throws, record `unavailable` + reason as “ready, but could not retrieve” and continue analysis (9.5). Split into `startLspSession`, which launches and completes stages 1–2 (returns a session), and stage-3 `probeSession`; `runLsp` is a thin wrapper (launch and close per run). analyze uses `createLspHolder` below rather than `runLsp`. Keep `runLsp` as the entry point whose tests pin the three-stage behavior (timeouts and failure reasons).

**Keep running and reuse** (2026-10-15. Real projects waited around 10 minutes for Gradle import on every analyze): `createLspHolder` in `packages/cli/src/lsp-session.ts`. serve holds one, launches it on the first request, and retains it until serve exits. An analyze without serve creates and closes one per run.
- **Keep only one pair of language-server worktrees**: `<置き場所>/lsp/{base,head}` (location is `worktreeRoot`, falling back to `.ondina`). These are ondina's only worktrees (analysis reads contents from git. 5.1). Switch contents with `git checkout --force --detach <sha>` (do not recreate. `switchWorktree` in `git.ts`). Untracked files (Gradle `.gradle/` / `build/`) remain
- **One analysis**: switch worktrees → decide whether to restart → if already running, notify changed files (added 1 / changed 2 / deleted 3) through `workspace/didChangeWatchedFiles`, then `didOpen` changed files → wait until reference-symbol edges can be retrieved and active `$/progress` count reaches 0 (every 0.25 seconds, up to 30 seconds) → retrieve semantic tokens and call relationships → `didClose` all opened files (otherwise the next PR still uses their contents from when opened)
- **When to restart**: the diff (base / head separately) contains build configuration files (`*.gradle` / `*.gradle.kts` / `gradle.properties` / `*.versions.toml` / under `buildSrc/` / under root `gradle/`. `packages/core/src/lsp/restart.ts`. Rules follow only Gradle's own conventions; do not restart for languages other than kotlin); launch form changes (command, arguments, location); language server has exited; worktrees were recreated. Print one progress line explaining why (“Build configuration changed, so the language server will restart (build.gradle.kts)” (「ビルドの設定ファイルが変わったので、言語サーバーを起動し直します（build.gradle.kts）」))
- **Do not throw**: whatever throws within an analysis (git worktree switching, locking, startup preparation), return `unavailable` with reason, close the language server, and restart on the next analysis (switching may be partial). serve has already sent response headers, so return failures in the final line too (throwing previously crashed serve)
- **One at a time**: queue within serve (tell later analyses “Another analysis is using the language server. Waiting for it to finish” (「別の解析が言語サーバーを使っています。終わるのを待っています」)). Across processes, use `.ondina/lsp/lock` (owner pid). Wait for living owners; reclaim from dead ones (prevent two language servers sharing a workspace)
- **Ask serve**: serve writes `.ondina/serve.json` (`{ pid, port }`) at startup and removes it on exit. analyze reads it; if pid is alive and `GET /api/repo` identifies the same repository (including serve launched in another worktree), request `POST /api/lsp` (`packages/cli/src/lsp-remote.ts`. 5.5). If unavailable (old serve, etc.), launch its own
  - serve sends an empty response line every 30 seconds during work (2026-10-28). analyze's fetch (Node) closes responses after 300 seconds without data. While queued behind earlier analysis or retrieving a large PR, no progress lines appear. Disconnected analyze then tried launching its own server and waited up to 60 minutes for serve's lock (the PR-list analysis button showed one line waiting for serve's pid)
- **Across worktrees** (2026-10-19): put language-server resources (indexes in `.ondina/lsp`, language-server worktrees, locks, saved structures, export workspace) and `serve.json` in the original folder's `.ondina/`, even when running inside a git worktree (`lspHome` in `lsp.ts`, `mainWorktree` in `git.ts`). Measures to speed up analyze did not work inside worktrees. The skill reused serve in another worktree, but analyze compared worktree paths and did not use it, waiting up to 60 minutes for serve's lock. Indexes are also keyed by location path strings, so they were recreated whenever the execution folder changed (fixture readiness 1.9 seconds → 8.3 seconds. Even with `.ondina` symlinked to the original folder, path strings differ)
  - Do not overwrite `serve.json` if another live serve wrote it (if a second serve opened non-PR changes in another worktree and took registration, it would wait for the first serve's lock). Unregistered serve receives no `/api/lsp` requests, so holds neither language server nor lock. Check registration every 10 seconds and take over if the registered serve has exited (otherwise analyze launches a language server on every run in the meantime)
  - Do not remove `.ondina/lsp` previously created per worktree
- **When serve exits**: also terminate the language server (send `shutdown` first if no analysis is running. If one is running, kill the entire group immediately, even during startup). The language server within serve does not handle signals itself (serve closes it through its own exit handling)
- Summary line: `LSP: <コマンド>（起動済み。準備完了まで 0.1 秒、…）`. Do not change `Analysis.lsp` shape (`waitedMs` starts at switching when already running, or at the initialize response when newly launched)

**Evidence for retaining the running server** (measured 2026-10-15. Fixture, kotlin-lsp 263.6379.0, Gradle 9.7.0. Time to readiness per analysis):

| Approach | Time to readiness | Gradle import |
|---|---|---|
| New workspace and location per PR (previous) | 10.5 seconds | Every time |
| Share only workspace (`--system-path`) across PRs | 11.4 seconds | Every time. Indexes are per location (`<system-path>/workspaces/<hash>`, 159 MB each) |
| Second run of the same PR (same location) | 4.9 seconds | Every time (only indexes reused) |
| Gradle build cache, configuration cache, Isolated Projects | Unchanged | Every time. Import runs no tasks. Configuration cache is not used for IDE imports (official). Isolated Projects only saves each time, without reuse (kotlin-lsp supplies a differently named initialization script each time) |
| **Keep running and switch worktrees at fixed locations** | **0.1–0.3 seconds** | **Only at startup**. Semantic tokens, edges, and symbols all match a fresh launch completely |
| Same, PR adding a dependency (gson) | 0.2 seconds | No reimport. **Five added gson locations lack color**. Change notifications make no difference; no command forces reimport. Restarting matches (4.5–5 seconds) |
| Reconnect per analysis through socket (`--multi-client`) | 1–4 seconds | Import on every connection if contents changed since the previous connection |

Therefore retain one stdio connection and restart only when build configuration changes. kotlin-lsp has no startup option to skip import either (`--help`).

**Skip Gradle import** (2026-10-19. In real projects, most preparation time was Gradle import (“Importing (Gradle)”), with around 10 minutes of waiting for the first PR after restarting serve and every PR changing build configuration): `packages/cli/src/lsp-model.ts` (locations, export) and `packages/core/src/lsp/project-model.ts` (read/write contents. Pure functions). Prepare project structure before startup and launch without running Gradle.
- **Export**: kotlin-lsp `workspace/executeCommand` command `exportWorkspace` (one directory argument. Appears in initialize's `executeCommandProvider.commands`. Undocumented) writes the imported project structure to `<dir>/workspace.json`. Paths inside are replaced with `<WORKSPACE>/` (argument directory), `<HOME>/`, and `<MAVEN_REPO>/`. Launch one kotlin-lsp solely for export (workspace `.ondina/lsp/export`, separate from main indexes), passing only that side's language-server worktree. Once import succeeds (`succeeded` in `intellij/importLog`), export and exit (do not wait for indexing. `until: 'imported'` in `startLspSession`). While waiting, print one line every 30 seconds as before
- **Save**: `.ondina/lsp/models/<key>/<書き出した版の sha>.json` (keep `<WORKSPACE>`). `key` is sha256 of kotlin-lsp's actual path (`realpath`, changes on version replacement) and the list of `パス blob` for build configuration files (same rules as restart above). Keep up to 20, ordered by most recently used
- **Launch**: put files in `<言語サーバー用の worktree の親>/json/{head,base}/workspace.json`, replacing `<WORKSPACE>` with each worktree's absolute path; pass these two to workspaceFolders (`folders` in `startLspSession`). Do not put them in worktrees: Gradle configuration takes precedence when they share a folder with Gradle files. File opening and query paths remain worktree paths. If base and head keys match, export once (Gradle mode imported twice)
- **Re-export**: no saved model / absolute paths in `path` or `homePath` (after resolving `<HOME>`; ignore paths inside worktrees and `<MAVEN_REPO>`) no longer exist (Gradle cache deleted or kotlin-lsp replaced) / a changed file is outside source directories and its directory did not exist in the exported version (new source directory). Do not re-export for a non-source directory that existed in the exported version: Gradle already inspected it and did not classify it as source (avoid exporting every time). Restart even a retained running server when source directories increase (a gap in the earlier retained-server approach too)
- **Fall back to previous Gradle launch**: no rules for the language or no build configuration files (not Gradle) / kotlin-lsp lacks `exportWorkspace` / worktree contains `workspace.json` / export command fails / JSON import fails (delete that saved model and restart in Gradle mode within the same analysis. Leave `.ondina/lsp/models/broken-<実体の hash>` marking that this kotlin-lsp should no longer use JSON, to avoid repeated exports and failures). Print one progress line explaining each
- **If import for export fails or times out**, return `unavailable` for that analysis with the same reason text as before. Do not wait again in Gradle mode
- Progress lines: “No saved project structure for this build configuration yet; importing with Gradle and saving it (head/base)” (「このビルドの設定のプロジェクト構造はまだ保存していないので、Gradle で取り込んで保存します（head・base）」), “Saved project structure (3 seconds). The same build configuration will skip Gradle import next time” (「プロジェクト構造を保存しました（3 秒）。同じビルドの設定なら、次からは Gradle の取り込みを省きます」), “Using saved project structure; skipping Gradle import” (「保存してあるプロジェクト構造を使うので、Gradle の取り込みを省きます」), etc.

**Evidence for skipping Gradle import** (measured 2026-10-19. kotlin-lsp 263.6379.0 and 263.7084.0. Import / readiness time):

| Repository | Gradle mode | JSON mode | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| fixture | 5 seconds / 15.2 seconds | 0.4 seconds / 10.3 seconds | Semantic tokens and edges match completely on head/base (1237 / 25, 1068 / 12) |
| Real Spring Boot repository (first run) | 18 seconds / 44.1 seconds | 0.6 seconds / 30.3 seconds | Complete match (824 / 23) |
| Same (second run) | 2.4 seconds / 5.8 seconds | 0.6 seconds / 4.3 seconds | Same as above |
| fixture through serve (263.7084.0) | 10.1 seconds | 1.9 seconds (first PR after restarting serve) | 66 lines, colors, and symbols match Gradle mode completely. A PR adding gson also matches (67 lines, `Gson` colored) |

- Gradle is warm and fast on this PC. For the project that waited 10 minutes on import, almost all of that wait is expected to disappear (not yet verified)
- `build.gradle.kts` itself has zero semantic tokens in either mode
- Structure includes only source directories present at export time (omits `src/main/java` if absent). A later-added `src/main/java` file dropped from 14 colored locations to 10. A directory present in the structure but currently absent causes no issue (complete match)
- Exporting immediately after import (before indexing) produces identical contents (finishes in 2.5 seconds on the fixture). JSON import also works when placed under the repository's `.ondina` (with parent `build.gradle.kts`)

Read the **semantic-token legend** from `capabilities.semanticTokensProvider.legend` in the `initialize` response. **The client's supplied legend is ignored**, so send an empty one.

**Command**: executable is `bin/intellij-server` (`kotlin-lsp.sh` is a deprecated wrapper that prints a stderr warning at every startup). Server name: `IntelliJ Language Server by JetBrains` / `ILS-263.4702.0`. Bundles JBR, so users need not provide Java (1.1 GB extracted).

| Option | Handling |
|---|---|
| `--stdio` | **Always supply**. Default is socket (`127.0.0.1:9999`), so omitting it prevents connection |
| `--system-path <dir>` | Cache and index directory. `.ondina/lsp/workspace` (one per repository. Original folder's `.ondina/` even inside a worktree (2026-10-19). Not separated by PR, headSha, or side. Until 2026-10-15, `.ondina/<key>/lsp/workspace`. ondina does not remove old directories). Internally separates locations into `workspaces/<hash>`. 159–173 MB each for fixture |

Resolution order: environment variable `ONDINA_LSP_COMMAND` > yaml `lsp.<language>.command` > `current/bin/intellij-server` in kotlin-lsp's installation directory (kotlin only. Value is a single executable path). ondina replaces only `{cacheDir}` in `args`.

**Wait times**:

| State | Time to readiness |
|---|---|
| Fully cold (starting with Gradle download) | 59 seconds |
| Gradle warm, indexes cold | 9–17 seconds (fixture. 37 files) / **45 seconds** (real Spring Boot repository. 16 files) |
| Fully warm (reuse `--system-path`) | 5–7 seconds |

**Readiness detection has three stages**. Measurements showed that either alone was insufficient:

1. **Wait** — count `$/progress` `begin`, decrement on `end`, and wait until active count is 0. Allow five seconds from the `initialize` response to the first `begin` (fastest measured: 1.7 seconds. Without this grace period, “zero because nothing started yet” is mistaken for ready)
   - Keep waiting while notifications (`$/progress` and `intellij/importLog`) continue or the server log grows. Time out if neither occurs for `readyTimeoutSec` (default 600 seconds). Overall limit: 60 minutes (2026-10-09. Initial startup timed out at 600 seconds in a real repository. Initially the total limit was 600 seconds, with no indication whether it was progressing or stuck)
   - Every 30 seconds while waiting, print `ondina analyze: 言語サーバーの準備を待っています（2 分 30 秒）: Importing（Gradle）、Indexing（42%）。最後の取り込みの記録: …`. Unfinished tasks use active `$/progress` titles and latest `report` (`message`, falling back to percentage). Latest import record is the last `intellij/importLog` line (truncate at 160 characters). This lets users distinguish a stalled Gradle import from slow indexing on screen
   - Server log (2026-10-10): immediately after initialize, kotlin-lsp sends `Log file: <場所>` (`<system-path>/system/log/intellij-server.log`) via `window/logMessage`. Detailed Gradle progress (build start, dependency resolution/downloads, etc.) appears only as `[IMPORT PROGRESS]: …` log lines, not notifications. Gradle configuration prints nothing to the console either. In a real repository, “Importing (Gradle)” remained without notifications for 10 minutes, then Indexing started (just before `readyTimeoutSec`). Check log size every second; growth counts as activity. Append the body of the latest `[IMPORT PROGRESS]` or WARN / ERROR line as `サーバーのログの最後: …` to the waiting line and timeout reason (`packages/core/src/lsp/server-log.ts`. Read only the last 64 KB. If unreadable, simply omit; waiting behavior stays the same)
2. **Obtain facts** — fail immediately on `intellij/importLog` `failed: true`; mark success on `succeeded: true`. Configuration can replace how these are read (`lsp.<lang>.importNotification`)
3. **Verify** — obtain the reference symbol's position with `documentSymbol`, then `prepareCallHierarchy` → `outgoingCalls` / `incomingCalls`. Ready if at least one edge is retrieved. Otherwise retry every two seconds (up to 30 seconds. Indexing has finished when `$/progress` stops, so waiting longer changes nothing)
   - If the reference symbols contain no callable functions, skip verification and consider stages 1–2 sufficient for readiness (2026-10-29). PRs changing only data classes, constants, or deleted declarations fall here. Previously the language server was skipped with `基準シンボルが宣言一覧に見つかりません`. That reason alone did not explain what happened. Reproducing locally with kotlin-lsp showed both `CartItem` (data class, kind 23) and `NEW_INVOICE` (constant, kind 14) in the declaration list; positions were unavailable only because they were not functions. Even with cold indexes, edges were available on the first attempt immediately after `$/progress` stopped (fixture, one measurement), so skipping verification is expected not to change responses

- **Custom notification alone is too early**: `succeeded: true` arrived at 3.2 seconds, but the response 1.5 seconds later had 18 semantic tokens (expected 30) and zero calls (expected one)
- **Standard signals alone allow broken states through**: even when Gradle import failed, `$/progress` ended normally at 5.5 seconds

**Choosing reference symbols** (deterministically, so the same headSha targets the same symbols. Principle 7): choose three changed hunks, excluding file hunks and hunks in files the PR deletes, in **core → test → unattributed → follow-on** order, then ascending `id` within each kind. Follow-on group members often merely repeat one line; in the fixture, all top three were `return order.discount`, with zero call edges (zero even with working LSP, so unsuitable for detection). Core hunks may encompass entire classes (promotion in 5.2 item 1), so descend to the first contained function if the matched declaration is not callable. Deleted files are excluded because reference symbols are opened and verified in the head worktree. A deleted file does not exist in head, and opening it failed with ENOENT, which made the whole language server unavailable (2026-11-05). When a file to open is missing from the worktree, only that file is skipped.

**Other measured findings**:
- `documentSymbol` is hierarchical; `name` is only the identifier (`applyDiscount`), with no `detail`. Functions are kind 6 / 12; same-named constructors appear as kind 9. Pass `selectionRange.start` directly to `prepareCallHierarchy`
- Server requests are `window/workDoneProgress/create` (respond `null`) and `workspace/configuration` (`{}` per `items` entry). Import does not proceed without responses
- `textDocument/publishDiagnostics` arrives empty even before readiness. Unsuitable as a readiness signal
- Detached worktrees (`.git` is a file) work
- **Gradle daemons remain after LSP exits** (even after killing the process group). ondina leaves them alone because other user builds could be affected

### 9.2 Methods used
| Purpose | Method |
|---|---|
| Semantic highlighting | `textDocument/semanticTokens/full` |
| Call edges | `textDocument/prepareCallHierarchy` → `callHierarchy/incomingCalls` / `callHierarchy/outgoingCalls`. Also record declaration lines (start of `selectionRange`) and calling locations (starting lines of `fromRanges`; caller's file regardless of direction), for extracting unchanged-symbol lines and filtering unchanged callees (9.6) (3.3. 2026-09-26 / 2026-10-12). When a hunk covers a whole class (such as a new class), ask every function inside it. Asking only the first function dropped calls from the second and later functions to counterparts without a diff (`findCallablePositions`. 2026-11-05) |
| Call edges before the change (step ① Background of the flow map) | Ask the same language server `prepareCallHierarchy` → `outgoingCalls` / `incomingCalls` with the base worktree as root. Only hunks whose file exists in base, and only calls (no references). A call to a counterpart without a diff is kept only when the calling line is a removed (`-`) line (the same rule as head's `+` lines). The result is `Analysis.edgesBase` (lines are in the base file). Confirmed with kotlin-lsp 263.7084.0 that the fixture yields apply → execute → find and apply → OrderResponse. Recorded with `ONDINA_LSP_RECORD_BASE` into `fixtures/order-service/lsp/call-edges-base.json` (`buildBaseCallEdges` in `core/src/lsp/edges.ts`. 2026-11-05) |
| Edge-label types | `textDocument/hover` (returns signatures including return types. 9.3) |
| Name to definition (3.6) | `textDocument/definition` (2026-10-29). Name positions come from name types in `semanticTokens/full` (excluding keyword, modifier, comment, string, number, regexp, operator). Query names on displayed lines (head: all changed files and unchanged files in call relationships; base: diff lines), then names throughout their destination files absent from diff columns, with 64 concurrent queries (fixture measurements: first stage 1441 locations in 1.6 seconds, second 274 in 0.2 seconds, whole analysis 8.6 seconds. With only diff lines: 1329 total locations in 1.5 seconds, 8.3 seconds overall. 2026-10-29). Convert the first response location (`targetSelectionRange` for `LocationLink`) to side and relative path if under a worktree; otherwise the jar name (module name for JDK). Package names point to directories, so treat as no response. A failed query also becomes no response; continue (`collectDefinitions` in `lsp-queries.ts`, rules in `core/src/lsp/definitions.ts`). Export records with `ONDINA_LSP_RECORD_DEFS` into `fixtures/order-service/lsp/definitions.json` (golden file `expected/definitions.json`) |
| Symbol list (supports hunk ↔ symbol mapping) | `textDocument/documentSymbol` |
| Test mapping and reference edges | `textDocument/references` (query the declaration's own position, including properties and classes. Test-file references feed `testCount`; references whose endpoints are both changed symbols feed `Edge(kind:'reference')`. 2026-09-24) |

### 9.3 Edge type labels (verified on a real server and implemented 2026-09-22)

Put argument and return types in `Edge.types = {in, out}`. **Both come from `textDocument/hover` signatures**. Omit any unavailable side entirely (principle 2. Do not put `out: ''` on Unit functions).

What hover returns (measured):

```
fun execute(id: OrderId, code: String): Order
fun find(id: OrderId): Order?
override fun save(order: Order)                 ← 戻り型なし = Unit
@PostMapping fun apply(@PathVariable id: String, @RequestBody body: ApplyDiscountRequest): ResponseEntity<OrderResponse>
```

`contents` is `{ kind: 'markdown', value: "```kotlin\n<シグネチャ>\n```" }`. KDoc adds prose before the code fence, so read only the fence contents. Parsing uses `signatureTypes` in `packages/core/src/lsp/edges.ts` (counts bracket nesting, so generic or lambda `,` do not split it).

**Cannot get this from `documentSymbol` `detail`** (kotlin-lsp 263.4702.0 returns no `detail`. Measured 2026-09-21).

What `CallHierarchyItem` provides:

| Available information | Source | Actual value (fixture) |
|---|---|---|
| Argument types (without argument names) | Item `name` | `Order.applyDiscount(DiscountCommand)` |
| Package | Item `detail` | `com.example.order.domain` |

**FQ name is `detail + '.' + name の括弧より前`**. This matches ondina's `symbolFqName`, so edge endpoints map directly to `Symbol.id`. A hunk may encompass an entire class (promotion in 5.2 item 1), so on mismatch round to the longest changed-symbol FQ name in the same file that is a prefix.

**Backtick-named test functions** (`` `persists discount` ``) return without backticks in both `documentSymbol` and `callHierarchy`. Strip them on ondina's side too when matching.

### 9.4 Detecting layer crossings
Set `crossesLayers: true` on edges whose direction is absent from `allowedDependencies` in `ondina.config.yaml`. **Render as red dashed lines, labeled “Call that skips layers (facts only)”**. For projects using ArchUnit, read those rules and apply the same detection (may defer).

**Check only when both endpoints have layers**. If either lacks a layer, `false` (do not show red dashed lines merely because a layer is unknown. Principle 2). Same-layer edges are also `false`. **Do not check if no `allowedDependencies` are written** (2026-10-09. Inferred layers have no allowed-direction rules. Configurations specifying only layers made every cross-layer line red dashed). If rules exist, continue treating directions from unlisted layers as “not allowed.” In the fixture, two of 22 edges are `true` (`OrderController.apply` directly calls `OrderRepositoryImpl.save` and `OrderRepository.save`. API allows only Application).

### 9.5 Failure behavior (implemented 2026-09-21)
If LSP crashes, import fails, or time runs out, record `{ command, status: 'unavailable', reason }` in `Analysis.lsp`, and **write analysis through to the end without stopping**. The UI works without semantic highlighting or edges. Course screens switch course paths to LLM inference and display `provenance: 'llm'` as dotted lines.

**Only two states: `ok` / `unavailable`** (no intermediate state). LSP responds even after failed import, but those responses are wrong (measured: 18 semantic tokens / expected 30; zero calls / expected one), so no “edges unavailable but tokens usable” intermediate state exists. One line in `reason` explains why unavailable. With `--no-lsp`, omit `lsp` entirely (= unmeasured; do not conflate with zero).

**Screen** (2026-10-09): beneath the entry title (after the LLM notice), explain missing relationship lines (`packages/web/src/components/MapNotice.tsx`). For `unavailable`, “No relationship lines. The language server could not be used: <reason>” (「関連の線がありません。言語サーバーが使えませんでした: <理由>」). If the reason includes `expired`, add guidance to reinstall kotlin-lsp. If the field is absent, “No relationship lines because analysis ran without the language server” (「言語サーバーを使わずに解析したので、関連の線がありません」).

Include `waitedMs` **only on runs that reached readiness**: `ok`, and `unavailable` where readiness was reached but retrieval (`work` in 9.1) failed. Other `unavailable` runs never reached readiness, so omit the field (there is no fact about “how many seconds until ready”).

**A language server kept running** (9.1. 2026-10-15): if it dies midway, mark that analysis `unavailable` with `LSP が途中で終了しました` (plus the last three stderr lines), and restart on the next analysis (abort pending queries after death to avoid hanging forever). Also terminate if import failure was reported. If only edge retrieval fails, keep the language server despite `unavailable` for that analysis (usable for the next PR).

Cases producing unavailable and reason text:

| Case | `reason` |
|---|---|
| Missing command | `LSP コマンドを起動できません: …` (return without waiting for timeout) |
| No command in environment variables, yaml, or installation directory (2026-10-09. Installation directory since 2026-10-19) | `kotlin-lsp が見つかりません。ondina update で入れるか、環境変数 ONDINA_LSP_COMMAND に kotlin-lsp の bin/intellij-server を指定してください（探した場所: <置き場>/current/bin/intellij-server）` |
| Dies immediately after startup / midway | `LSP が起動してすぐ終了しました` / `LSP が途中で終了しました`. Append the last three nonempty stderr lines after `: `, joined with ` / ` (2026-10-09). Example: kotlin-lsp is a trial build and stops launching around 30 days after release. On 2026-10-09, 263.4702.0 produced `LSP が起動してすぐ終了しました: This build of kotlin-server has expired. / The IDE will now close. / Please download a new build from https://github.com/kotlin/kotlin-lsp` |
| Import failed | `インポートに失敗しました: <サーバの 1 行目>` |
| `$/progress` never stops | `準備完了まで待ちましたが時間切れです（新しい知らせが 10 分ありません）` or `（60 分）`. Append `。終わっていない作業: Importing（Gradle）。最後の取り込みの記録: …。サーバーのログの最後: …` to either (2026-10-09 / 10) |
| Cannot retrieve edges | `基準シンボルの呼び出し関係を <秒> 秒待っても取れませんでした` (up to 30 seconds, or `readyTimeoutSec` if shorter) |
| yaml lacks `lsp.<language>` | `ondina.config.yaml に lsp.<language> がありません` |
| Retrieval crashes after readiness | Exception text thrown by `work`. **Only this row includes `waitedMs`** (because readiness was reached) |
| Another process keeps using the language server (2026-10-15) | `別の解析（pid <n>）が言語サーバーを使い続けているので、使えませんでした（60 分待ちました）` (waited for `.ondina/lsp/lock` until the overall limit) |

### 9.6 What kotlin-lsp does not answer (verified on a real server 2026-09-22)

Call relationships have gaps. **“Not called” and “not retrieved” differ**, so attach `Symbol.edgesMeasured: false` to changed symbols for which `prepareCallHierarchy` gave no response. `analyze` prints their count to stderr (principle 2).

**Functions returning `@JvmInline value class` never appear in call relationships** (measured 6/6):

| Declaration | Return type | `prepareCallHierarchy` |
|---|---|---|
| `Order.total()` | `Money` (value class) | `[]` |
| `Money.plus` / `minus` / `times` / `compareTo` | value class members | `[]` |
| `OrderAdminView.discountField(order: Order)` | `Money` | `[]` |
| `DiscountPolicy.rateFor(String)` | `Rate` (plain data class) | 1 item |
| `ApplyDiscountUseCase.execute(...)` | `Order` | 1 item |
| `OrderRepositoryImpl.save(Order)` | None (Unit) | 1 item |

`Order.total()` is actually called by `OrderTest`, yet absent even from the caller's `outgoingCalls`. ondina cannot fill this gap.

Other things that do not become edges: operators (`subtotal - discount`), instance property reads (`order.discount`), constructors (`Order(...)`). **Companion property reads (`Money.Companion.ZERO`) do appear as edges**.

**`incomingCalls` does not return calls from tests**. Incoming calls for `OrderRepositoryImpl.save` contain only two production-code calls, omitting three test locations. They appear in the test function's `outgoingCalls`, so **build test edges (`kind: 'test'`) from the test side**. Count `Symbol.testCount` from `textDocument/references`, not edges (round test-file references to their enclosing test functions; two calls from the same test count as one).

Actual fixture counts: of 42 changed hunks (excluding file hunks), callHierarchy responded for 16; 20 call edges (10 test, two layer crossings); four unchanged symbols on paths; 26 could not be retrieved (on 2026-10-12, dropping calls from unchanged lines reduced 22 → 20 edges and 5 → 4 symbols).

**Gaps filled by reference edges (measured 2026-09-23, implemented 2026-09-24)**: with call edges alone, 18 of 48 changed symbols had edges, and all 22 hunks in the follow-on bundle had none (not viable as a map). `textDocument/references` answers for properties and classes as well as functions (`Order.discount` ← 27 files, `Order` ← 28 files), so round reference positions to enclosing declarations and create `Edge(kind:'reference')` **only when both endpoints are changed symbols** (without this restriction, grows to 178 edges). Fixture has 46 reference edges (three layer crossings); changed symbols with edges increase from 18 → 40 / 48. Even `Order.total()` (value-class return), which callHierarchy does not answer, yields five references. Do not use reference edges for course paths (they have no order). Do not pass them to LLM input either.

**Calling positions (`fromRanges`. Measured 2026-09-26)**: call edges include locations in the caller's file (`Order.applyDiscount → DiscountPolicy.rateFor` is Order.kt line 9). However, **interface → implementation edges (`OrderRepository.save → OrderRepositoryImpl.save`) have `fromRanges: []`**. Even without positions, unchanged-symbol declaration lines are available, so excerpts contain only declaration context. Records recaptured with the same version (263.4702.0) differed only in added fields and `references` order (same contents; assembly is order independent).

**Unchanged callees only for calls from added lines (2026-10-12)**: for calls (`call` / `test`) from changed symbols to unchanged targets, create edges only when at least one calling position matches an added line in the changed symbol's hunk (`buildCallGraph`. 3.3). Calls from unchanged lines (fixture `execute → OrderRepository.find`, or unchanged `every { repository.find(any()) }` in a modified test) are unrelated to the diff. In whole-class hunks, calls from other methods in the same class (rounded to the class by prefix in `resolve`) are also unrelated. Do not create unchanged target symbols for dropped calls. Keep calls if position is unavailable (records without positions), empty, or the hunk cannot be found (do not hide unretrieved information. Principle 2). Keep edges between changed symbols and edges from unchanged callers regardless of position. **Known gap**: positions point to the line of the called name, so calls whose name line is unchanged but arguments on the next line changed are omitted

**Known overload gap**: changed hunk `symbolFqName` distinguishes same-named sibling declarations with `#2` (5.2 item 1), but `CallHierarchyItem` names lack it (include argument types, but cannot tell which occurrence is `#2`). Therefore **when an overload changes, its edge endpoint does not map to the changed symbol; it appears separately as an unchanged symbol**. Do not strip `#2` to match, since that would guess which overload it is (principle 2). Fixing this requires the TODO in 5.2 first (distinguish stable IDs by argument type). Does not occur in the fixture (no overloads in call relationships).

---

## 10. LLM input/output specification

The LLM is called once. Its input is the output of Stage 1. The output shape is written as JSON Schema in the prompt body. It is checked in Stage 3 (5.4), rather than enforced by the LLM provider (the LLM command is not necessarily Claude. 5.3).
**Implemented scope (2026-09-22. M2)**: Input is `pr` / `previous` / `hunks` (with `layer`) / `rippleGroups` / `edges` / `unlayeredPaths`; output is `hows` / `claims` / `layerGuesses` / `themes` (`themes` since 2026-11-04: map groups when there are 8 or more Hows, an empty array for 7 or fewer. `summary` moved to the second call on 2026-10-03. 10.4). `specs` is not produced by the first call; it comes from a separate call when a ticket is retrieved (10.8. 2026-10-12). `rippleVerdicts` is for M4. **Do not have the LLM produce `tours`** (decided 2026-09-24. The program builds paths by following `call` edges: `packages/core/src/tour/build.ts`. Reasons: having the LLM produce them makes the meter fluctuate; a path is a sequence of edges = facts; the structure prevents paths without edges. The second LLM call writes the text for each step. 10.4). Differences from the implementation:
- `hunks[].kindHint` is `core` (a core with a group) / `candidate` (a core candidate without a group. Chapter 8, item 8) / `test` / absent. It is only a reading hint; the LLM does not produce kind (5.4, item 4)
- `hunks[].layer` contains only layers determined by globs (no inferences). Only the three fields `{ from, to, kind }` are passed for `edges` (type labels and `crossesLayers` are not passed. Do not ask it to judge. Principle 3). Reference edges (`reference`) are not passed. If LSP has not run, the entire `edges` field is absent
- `unlayeredPaths` contains changed files that matched no glob (24 of 37 in the fixture). Only output `layerGuesses` that pass verification (5.4, item 8) are accepted
- `rippleGroups[]` contains `id` (`g1` …), `pattern`, `coreHunkId`, `members` (count), and `representative` and `deviations` (with lines). Hunks in groups are not included in `hunks[]`
- Add `groupIds` to `hows[]` and assign each entire group to one How (do not have it put the representative's id in `hunkIds`)
- `previous.hows` is `{ id, title, why }`; `previous.assignment` contains only unchanged hunks. `previous.themes` is the previous map's themes (`{ title, howIds }`, only when the previous run had themes). A theme with the same Hows keeps the same title (Principle 7)
- Types are in `packages/core/src/label/types.ts`, the prompt body in `prompt.ts`, and the output shape in `LLM_OUTPUT_SCHEMA` in `schema.ts`

### 10.1 Input (excerpt)
```json
{
  "pr": { "title": "…", "body": "作者の説明原文" },
  "previous": { "hows": [...], "assignment": { "hunkId": [1, 3] } },
  "hunks": [
    { "id": "…", "path": "order/domain/Order.kt", "symbol": "applyDiscount()", "kindHint": "core", "layer": "Domain",
      "lines": ["-    fun getDiscount(): Money = …", "+    fun applyDiscount(cmd: DiscountCommand) {", "…"] }
  ],
  "rippleGroups": [ { "id": "g1", "pattern": "getDiscount() → discount", "coreHunkId": "…", "representative": "…", "members": 22, "deviations": ["…"],
                      "llmCandidates": [ { "hunkId": "…", "lines": ["…"] } ] } ],
  "edges": [ { "from": "…", "to": "…", "kind": "call" } ],
  "unlayeredPaths": ["src/main/kotlin/com/example/common/Logger.kt"]
}
```
Pass only the representative, deviations, and candidates for LLM judgment from each follow-on group (not all 22 members with the same form).

### 10.2 Output (input_schema)
```json
{
  "hows": [ { "id": 1, "title": "ドメインに割引を追加", "why": "割引額を Order 自身が持ち、計算をドメインの中で完結させる", "hunkIds": ["…"] } ],
  "summary": { "problem": "…", "approach": "…", "untouched": "…",
               "beforeAfter": [ { "target": "Order.getDiscount()", "before": "promo.amount を返す", "after": "削除。order.discount を読む" } ] },
  "claims": [ { "text": "キャッシュ層を横断的に更新し", "backed": false, "reason": "キャッシュ層に該当する変更は差分に見当たらない" } ],
  "rippleVerdicts": [ { "groupId": "g1", "hunkId": "…", "same": true } ],
  "layerGuesses": [ { "path": "scripts/foo.kt", "layer": "Infrastructure" } ],
  "themes": [ { "title": "割引の計算と適用", "howIds": [3, 5] } ],
  "tours": "（出させない。2026-09-24 決定。経路はプログラムが組む。上の実装済みの範囲を参照）"
}
```

### 10.3 Prompt constraints (written in the system prompt)
- Do not write opinions about correctness, bugs, or quality. Sentences must contain only observations ("X also calls it directly"). Evaluative language is prohibited
- Write `summary` from the diff alone. Use `pr.body` only to assess `claims` (`summary` has been in the second call since 2026-10-03. 10.4)
- Put `hows` in dependency order (foundations first). Headings must be imperative and no more than 10 words
- **Try** to put every hunkId in a How, but leave out those that do not fit (the program identifies unattributed hunks)
- When `previous` is present, keep the assignments and How ids for hunks whose contents have not changed
- ~~`tours` uses only edges in `edges`~~ → Do not have the LLM produce `tours` (2026-09-24). Path rules: seeds = symbols of the How's hunks. Follow only `call` edges leaving the seeds (do not follow references or tests. Do not include neighbors on the caller side). Starting points = symbols not called by others (stable order: layer order → fully qualified name). Breadth first, one symbol per step. Append hunks not on an edge one at a time in hunk order (a How with zero edges contains only these). Only one representative per follow-on group. Golden file: `fixtures/order-service/expected/tours.json`
- Do not read files or use tools; return only one JSON object (do not rely on CLI-specific flags that restrict tools)
- A How groups changes along the PR's purpose. Do not create a one-hunk How solely for a change unconnected to other Hows (log formatting, adding a flag constant, etc.); leave it unattributed (2026-09-17. Added because the first recording made `Logger.format()` and `DISCOUNT_V2` one-hunk Hows)

---

### 10.4 Input/output for the second call (writing text) (implemented 2026-10-03. `packages/core/src/narration/`)

Called after verification of the first call and construction of the reading order (`tours`) (5.3). Have it write the entry explanation, each course's What changes and What to check, and each stop's This place (2026-10-22. The prototype selected "first provide guidance, then follow the code to check it." Previously this was a course introduction and two lines per stop). Types are in `narration/types.ts`, the output shape in `NARRATION_OUTPUT_SCHEMA` in `narration/schema.ts`, and the prompt in `narration/prompt.ts`.

Input (built deterministically. Golden file: `fixtures/order-service/expected/llm-text-input.json`. The fixture has 7 courses and 29 stops, and 1 behavior; the prompt is 40 KB):
```json
{
  "pr": { "title": "…" },
  "hunks": [ "…1 回目と同じ。行の頭に head の行番号と |" ], "rippleGroups": [ "…同じ" ],
  "courses": [ { "howId": 3, "title": "…", "why": "…", "stops": [
    { "symbolId": "…", "symbol": "com.example.order.domain.Order", "path": "…", "test": false, "hunkId": "…" },
    { "symbolId": "…", "symbol": "…OrderAdminView.discountField", "test": false, "hunkId": "…", "groupId": "g1" } ],
    "related": [ { "of": "…（Order の symbolId）", "dir": "out", "symbol": "…DiscountPolicy.rateFor", "path": "…" } ] } ],
  "unassigned": ["…どの How にも入らなかった hunk の id"],
  "existing": [ { "symbol": "…DiscountPolicy.rateFor", "path": "…", "kind": "function", "removed": false },
                { "symbol": "…Order.getDiscount", "path": "…", "kind": "function", "removed": true } ],
  "behaviors": [ { "id": "f1", "start": "com.example.order.api.OrderController.apply",
                   "before": [ "OrderController.apply: useCase.execute(OrderId(id))", "…" ],
                   "after": [ "OrderController.apply: useCase.execute(OrderId(id), body.discountCode)", "…" ],
                   "changes": [ "変えた OrderController.apply: useCase.execute(OrderId(id)) → useCase.execute(OrderId(id), body.discountCode)", "足した …", "…" ] } ]
}
```
- `behaviors` are the request flows (from 2026-11-05. `CallFlow` in chapter 4; before that, behaviors `Behavior`). `before` / `after` are the before-change / after-change call trees opened in calling order as "caller's short name: call expression" (the callee's short name when there is no expression; empty when that side has no entry). `changes` lists the calls whose status is not same, in calling order, as "足した / 変えた / 消した caller: expression" ("old expression → new expression" when a changed call's expression differs). The field keeps its behaviors-era name. Absent when there are no flows
- `existing` contains candidates for What existed before this PR? in the explain tab's background (2026-10-29). First are unchanged callees of stops (out in `related`, once in course order), followed by declarations removed in this PR (Kotlin declarations found only in base. If the parent is also removed, include only the parent. Also inspect primary-constructor `val` / `var`. `core/src/hunks/removed.ts`). The fixture has `rateFor`, `Money.ZERO`, and `OrderRepository.save`, plus the removed `Order.promo` and `getDiscount`
- Do not pass `pr.body` (write the entry explanation from the diff alone). Do not pass `previous` / `edges` / `unlayeredPaths` either
- Prefix the same diff lines used in the first call with the head (after merge) line number and `|` (`15|+    fun execute(…`. Deleted lines have spaces instead of a number. Align to the width of the largest number in the hunk). This lets it select evidence lines for What to check by number (2026-10-22)
- Look up each stop's diff lines by `hunkId` in `hunks[]` or the representatives/deviations in `rippleGroups[]`. `groupId` identifies hunks in groups (representatives/deviations). `test` means the hunk's kind is test or it is the source of a test edge. Stops contain only diffs (2026-10-11). `related` contains unchanged counterparts of stops (call-edge counterparts and callees of tests. Names and paths only, no lines. In stop order, caller → callee, name order, once per course). This provides material for untouched areas (`untouched`); do not have it write text for them

Output:
```json
{
  "summary": { "headline": "この PR が何をするかの 1 文",
               "example": { "text": "テストの値で 1〜2 文", "at": [ { "symbolId": "…テストの停留所", "lines": [15, 16, 17] } ] },
               "flows": [ { "nodes": [ { "title": "箱の名前", "howIds": [2, 3], "symbolId": "…中心の停留所", "before": "1 文", "after": "1 文",
                                         "untouched": ["領域の名前"], "pass": "次の箱へ渡る値" } ] } ] },
  "background": { "existing": [ { "symbols": ["…existing[].symbol を 1〜2 個"], "text": "それが何をする物か 1 文" } ],
                  "terms": [ { "word": "小計", "code": "subtotal", "meaning": "意味の 1 文" } ] },
  "courses": [ { "howId": 3, "change": { "before": "1〜2 文", "after": "1〜2 文" },
                 "checks": [ { "text": "観測の 1 文（句点なし）", "at": [ { "symbolId": "…停留所", "lines": [16], "say": "1 文" } ] } ],
                 "stops": [ { "symbolId": "…", "role": "1〜2 文" } ] } ],
  "behaviors": [ { "id": "f1", "title": "割引コードを受け取って注文に割引を適用する", "before": "① Background, 1–2 sentences", "change": "② This PR, 1 sentence", "after": "③ After merge, 1 sentence (「〜 → 〜 → 〜。」)" } ]
}
```
`behaviors` (2026-11-04) are behavior titles (request flow titles and per-stage sentences from 2026-11-05); their order is the display order. Old records lack them (read as empty; missing stage sentences read as empty strings). Verification: 5.4, item 11.
Background has been included since 2026-10-29 (verified results go into `existing` / `terms` in `Analysis.background`. Requirement extraction produces `why` separately). If existing items' `symbols` or boxes' `untouched` arrive as strings, read them as one-element arrays; likewise, read a single number in `howIds` or `lines` as a one-element array. Verification: 5.4, item 9. Since 2026-10-29, the entry explanation has a headline, example, and flow diagram (a new option chosen by comparing prototypes). Previously it was `problem` / `approach` / `untouched` / `beforeAfter` (problem, approach, untouched areas, before/after table).

Prompt constraints (in addition to 10.3. Sentence style decided 2026-09-29):
- No judgments or evaluative language; observations only. Do not read files or use tools; return only one JSON object (same as the first call)
- Short sentences. One point per sentence. End sentences with a full stop (the UI inserts line breaks at full stops). Only list items omit the full stop
- Include only 1–2 identifiers that are needed. Do not repeat facts shown in UI chips (layer, new / changed, line count, stop count, test count, how it is called)
- `change` describes before and after merge within this course's scope (1–2 sentences each, in behavioral terms). `checks` has 2–5 items. Each is one sentence of observation that readers can check against the code, without saying whether it is correct. Order them so they can be checked while visiting stops in order. `checks[].at` has 1–3 locations per sentence, using stops in that course and the numbers at the start of lines (deleted lines cannot be selected. Unchanged lines and test stops may be selected). `role` describes what this place does within the course (entry / loading / calculation / what is tested). Do not write where it is called from or on which lines; the UI derives those from edges (2026-10-22)
- Entry explanation (2026-10-29): readers see this first, without hearing the project's background. `headline` is one sentence saying what this PR does (in the form "Enable …." Do not begin with the state before the change). `example` is 1–2 sentences with concrete values (inputs and results) from test-stop diffs; `at` identifies the test stops and lines the values came from (empty if there are no values). `flows` is a diagram of boxes in traversal order, usually one flow (two or more only when purposes differ). Each box groups 1–3 courses; put every course in exactly one box. `title` is a short name for the behavior (not a class name), `symbolId` is a central stop in the box's courses, and `before` / `after` are one sentence each in behavioral terms (example values may be added in parentheses). `untouched` lists 0–2 unchanged areas beside the box (`courses[].related`, etc.), by area name only (exclude changes that belong to no course). `pass` is the value passed to the next box (empty for the last box)
- Background (2026-10-29): before reading the diff, readers learn here what existed before this PR and the terms they will encounter (knowing the main terms first helps readers understand the explanation of the mechanism. Readers with less background learn from sentences that state connections fully). `existing` has 1–3 rows from input `existing[]`; `symbols` contains 1–2 unchanged `existing[].symbol` values (only items with the same `removed` value in one row). `text` is one sentence describing what the item does (it may add how the diff uses it, or what replaces a removed item. Do not write Unchanged or Removed; chips show these). `terms` has 2–4 terms needed to read the diff, in diff-reading order. `word` is a Japanese term, `code` is one exact identifier appearing in diff lines (append `()` for functions), and `meaning` is one sentence explaining its meaning
- Behaviors (2026-11-04): write every behavior with the same ids as the input's `behaviors[]`. The order is the display order (behaviors closer to the PR's purpose first). `title` is a short phrase about the behavior ending in 「〜する」 (no class or function names, no full stop). The calling order is decided by the program; the LLM does not change it. Per-stage sentences (2026-11-05) use the plain style: `before` is ① Background in one or two sentences (what the before-change flow does), `change` is ② This PR in one sentence (what changes in this flow; many added calls are summed up in words about behavior), `after` is ③ After merge in one sentence (behavior words joined with 「→」 in calling order). Empty when the input's field is empty. At most one class or function name, no copied expressions, no counts that the map shows
- Use another subject (inventory allocation) for examples. Using the fixture's subject (orders and discounts) would make recorded output look as though it copied the examples

### 10.5 Input/output for Ask the guide (implemented 2026-10-08. `packages/core/src/ask/`)

`serve` calls it each time Send is pressed (3.10). When the output asks for code to read (`need`) instead of an answer, serve reads it from head and asks again (2026-11-04. Up to 2 times, so at most 3 LLM calls per question. See "Reading" below). Types are in `ask/types.ts`, material is built by `buildAskInput` in `ask/input.ts`, the output shape is `ASK_OUTPUT_SCHEMA` in `ask/schema.ts`, the prompt is in `ask/prompt.ts`, and verification is in `ask/verify.ts`. Golden files (output files saved as expected results) are `fixtures/order-service/expected/ask-input-{pr,symbol,hunk,lines,web,why}.json` (why asks "why" with input that has the PR body, ticket, and background. 2026-11-04); recordings are `llm/ask-{pr,symbol,hunk,lines,web,why}.json` (`scripts/record-llm-ask.mjs`. web uses the question outside the code, "What is ?:?", recorded with codex, which can search the internet. why is recorded with the default command).

Input (deterministic. Include every line of this place. If that is not enough, the guide asks with need, and serve adds what it read to reads):
```json
{
  "pr": { "title": "…", "body": "…", "ticket": { "ref": "SHOP-421", "title": "…", "body": "…" } },
  "background": { "why": ["…"], "projectWhy": ["…"], "existing": [ { "names": [ { "fqName": "…", "path": "…" } ], "text": "…", "removed": true } ], "terms": [ { "word": "割引額", "code": "discount", "meaning": "…" } ] },
  "commits": [ { "sha": "abcdef1", "title": "…", "body": "…" } ],
  "courses": [ { "id": 3, "title": "…", "why": "…" } ],
  "place": { "kind": "code", "about": "symbol", "path": "…/Order.kt", "symbol": "com.example.order.domain.Order", "changed": true, "courses": [3],
             "file": ["h1   package com.example.order.domain", "…"],
             "hunks": [ { "symbol": "…", "path": "…", "kind": "core", "intent": { "notes": [ { "kind": "written", "from": "commit", "quote": "…", "text": "…", "commit": "abcdef1" } ], "ask": "…" }, "lines": ["h3   class Order(", "b6 -     val promo: Promo? = null,", "h6 +     var discount: Money = Money.ZERO,", "…"] } ],
             "related": [ { "relation": "calls", "symbol": "…DiscountPolicy.rateFor", "path": "…", "changed": false, "lines": ["h7       )", "…"] } ] },
  "reads": [ { "kind": "file", "request": "…/PromoService.kt", "path": "…/PromoService.kt", "lines": ["h1   package com.example.promo", "…"] },
             { "kind": "search", "request": "legacyPromo", "path": "…/PromoService.kt", "lines": ["h10           val d = …", "h14       private fun legacyPromo(…"] } ],
  "history": [ { "question": "…", "answer": "…" } ],
  "question": "promo はどこへ行った？"
}
```
- **Number lines**: `h12` is head line 12; `b6` is base line 6. Markers are `+` added / `-` deleted / space unchanged. Measured on 2026-10-08: with unnumbered material (the second call's input as-is), all answer line locations became `null`
- place: `pr` = entry explanation (`summary`), all hunks (only representatives and deviations for groups; `group` contains role, pattern, and count), and unattributed symbol names. `course` = stops in reading order (diff lines. Stops have contained only diffs since 2026-10-11). `code` = symbol / one diff / file: full head text (`Analysis.files`. Absent for old analyses and deleted files), its diffs (all diffs in that file for a file), and surroundings (`calls` / `calledBy` / `testedBy` / `referencedBy`. For references, limit group members to representatives and deviations, putting those first. Keep only the first relation to the same counterpart. Up to 12 total)
- **Selected lines** (3.10, decision 2): when `place.lines` is present, add `selected` to the code location. Shape: `{ path, from: "h8", to: "h11", lines: [...] }`. lines covers from–to in display order. Display order inserts each diff's lines into the full head text (`diffFileLines`). Deleted, added, and unchanged lines are included as-is. For old analyses without full text, extract from diff lines only; use empty if both endpoints cannot be found. The prompt says, "question asks about these lines. Answer about these lines first, and attach cite to these lines where possible." selected also counts as "lines included in the material" during verification
- history contains the preceding 3 questions at the same location (location key). Each line range has a separate key, so selected-line conversations continue separately for each range
- **PR body, ticket, and background** (2026-11-04): `pr.body` is the author's description (`Analysis.pr.body`). `pr.ticket` is the ticket's ref, title, and body (Jira HTML becomes text with tags removed, using `ticketPlainText` as in 10.8. A ticket that could not be fetched has only ref). Both bodies are cut at 4000 characters with `⋯` at the end. `background` is the part of the explain tab's background (3.1) with the ticket's reason (why), the parent ticket's purpose (`project.why` → projectWhy), things that existed before (names and paths), and terms. The project's PR list and searched numbers are not passed. When only the title was passed, "なぜ promo を消して discount にしたの？" was answered with "unknown" even though the body and ticket give the reason
- **Per-diff intents and commit messages** (2026-11-11): material for the guide's answers to "why". `commits` is `Analysis.commits` (`sha` shortened to 7 characters, oldest first, the first 30 only, `body` cut at 1000 characters with a trailing `⋯`; the field is omitted when there are none). `intent` is the intent from `Analysis.intents`, attached to the diffs at the asked place (`place.hunks`, and the changed stops of a course's `stops`; for the whole PR, every diff). Followers use the representative's intent (the `HunkIntent` rule). Diffs without an intent get no field. Each note's `kind` is passed through (written = the author wrote it, quote already verified, `commit` shortened to 7 characters / guess = inferred by the analysis / none = not written) so the guide can tell what is written from what is inferred. `ask` is the draft question for the author. Without these, a "why" that was not in the body, ticket, or background ended with "it is not written".
- **reads** (2026-11-04): what the guide asked for with need and serve read, in the order asked. `kind` = file (the whole `request` file) / search (lines hit by the `request` string, one entry per file). Empty lines mean the path does not exist or the string hit nothing. See "Reading" below

Output:
```json
{ "lead": "1〜2 文",
  "items": [ { "text": "事実 1 つ", "cite": { "path": "…", "side": "head", "line": 13, "endLine": 14 } },
             { "text": "ネットで調べた事実", "web": { "url": "https://kotlinlang.org/docs/null-safety.html", "title": "Null safety | Kotlin Documentation" } },
             { "text": "元から知っている事実", "known": true },
             { "text": "作者が書いていないなぜを材料から推し量った文", "guess": true, "cite": { "path": "…", "side": "head", "line": 13 } } ],
  "tail": "任意の 1 文" }
```
Each item has one source for its fact: `cite` for material lines, `web` for an internet page, or `known` for prior knowledge with neither. Attach nothing to facts from the material that cannot be tied to lines, such as counts (2026-10-15). Facts from the commit messages are likewise left unattached and the prose says where they are written ("the message of commit abcdef1 says ..."). A "why" the author did not write, inferred from the material, is an item with `guess: true` (2026-11-11; principle 2). `cite` may point at the lines the inference came from. It is never combined with `web` or `known` (verification keeps `guess` and drops `web` and `known`). The screen shows a dotted underline and an "inferred" chip next to the item (in the same place as the "no source" chip). Only `true` is accepted for `guess` in the LLM output; other values are dropped.
Facts from the PR body, ticket, or background also get nothing; the sentence says where they are written ("PR の本文に〜とある". 2026-11-04).

When the material is not enough, return only this instead of an answer (2026-11-04):
```json
{ "need": { "files": ["src/main/kotlin/com/example/promo/PromoService.kt"], "search": ["legacyPromo"] } }
```
If there is a lead, take the answer (even if need is also present). With no lead and at least one entry in need, it is need. files and search are each limited to 5 (from the start, after removing empty and duplicate entries. `ASK_NEED_MAX`). The last round's prompt removes need from the output shape (`ASK_ANSWER_SCHEMA`) and adds "you can no longer ask for anything to read. Answer with the current material, and say what you could not find after reading." If it still returns need, it fails (502).

Prompt constraints (in addition to 10.3): no judgments or evaluative language; facts only even for "Is this a bug?"; do not guess code absent from the material, ask for it with need; for "why", read the PR body, ticket, background, commit messages, and per-diff intents (kind written) first and, if one says, answer from it and show where; if none says, do not stop at "it is not written" but infer from facts in the material (mark the item `guess: true`, and make the lead say it is inferred: "probably ...; the author did not write it"); put what to ask the author in the tail (use the intent's `ask` if present); say something is unknown only after reading; 2–4 items; short sentences ending with full stops; plain Japanese style (the same voice as the one-line guidance. In actual use it varied between plain and polite forms depending on the question); no Markdown symbols; do not write h12 / b6 numbers in sentences (use cite for lines, "line 12" in prose); facts without attachable locations do not need cite; one JSON object only. Examples use another subject (inventory allocation).
Changes on 2026-10-15: also answer questions outside the code (term meanings, language/library/framework mechanisms, design ideas), without refusing because they are absent from the material. For information absent from the material, search the internet if possible and put the opened page in `web`. Even for prior knowledge, search and attach a page if possible (with the initial instructions, codex answered "What is ?:?" from knowledge without searching). Use `known` only when search is unavailable. Restrict "do not fill gaps with guesses" to "the contents of this PR (change intent, the author's thoughts, code absent from the material)." Changed "do not read files or use tools" to "do not read repository files or run commands; the only permitted tools are internet search and opening pages." Added `web` and `known` items to the examples.
Changes on 2026-11-11: added per-diff intents and commit messages to the material (see "Per-diff intents and commit messages" above), and when no source says why, the guide may infer it from facts in the material instead of ending with "it is not written". Inferred items carry `guess: true`, and the screen labels them "inferred".
Changes on 2026-11-04: stopped "say 'The material does not show this' for information absent from the material." With the fixture material and the default command, "PromoService.apply は discount をどう使ってる？" was answered with "legacyPromo is not in the material," "Money の times はどう計算してる？丸めは？" with "Money.times is not in the material," and "なぜ promo を消して discount にしたの？" with "the reason is unknown" (the body and ticket give it). Now code absent from the material is asked for with need, and "why" is answered from the body, ticket, and background. "Do not read repository files" became "do not read them yourself; ask with need when you want to read." Added a need example, and changed the answer example's tail from "not in the material" to "the PR body says …".

Reading (2026-11-04. `readForAsk` in `packages/cli/src/ask-read.ts`): read from the analyzed version (`Analysis.pr.headSha`. For working-tree analyses it is a tree sha; both `git cat-file` and `git grep` read trees). Do not read the current working tree (even if it changed after the analysis, line numbers do not drift from the material). files reads whole files with `git cat-file --batch` (`readBlobs`) and numbers them on the head side (first 800 lines, then `⋯`. Leading `./` and `/` are removed). search runs `git grep -n -z -I -F` and makes one entry per file (in path order) from the hit lines (up to 40 hits per string, then `⋯` at the end of the last entry). A missing path, a folder, or a string with no hits also becomes one entry with empty lines (so the guide knows it was not found). The time limit is 5 minutes across all rounds (`ASK_TIMEOUT_MS`. A person waits for one answer). Stop stops the LLM and git in any round. Only serve reads; `record-llm-ask.mjs` does not save when need comes back. Answer locations can point into read files, but pressing a line in a file without full text in the analysis (not in `Analysis.files`) does not move the screen (the UI reads only Analysis.json. Principle 5)

Verification (entirely by program): lines in what was read (reads) also count as material (2026-11-04). Accept internet pages only if they parse as `http:` / `https:` URLs; otherwise remove the page and set `known` (No source). Strip only backticks from titles. Set `known` only on items with neither `cite` nor a parseable page (items citing lines absent from the material remain `unverified`). Missing lead means failure (502). Exclude items with no text. Accept line locations **only for numbers included in the material** (path, side, line; both endpoints for a range. If the end is at or before the start, use one line). Otherwise remove the location and set `unverified` (the UI shows Location could not be verified). Keep facts without locations (counts, information absent from the material) as-is (no underline in the UI). Strip backticks (measured answers surrounded identifiers with backticks). Replace material numbers that leaked into prose (`h10` / `b6`) with "line 10" / "base line 6" (actual output said "point to h10 when asking the author." The prompt also prohibits this).

Measured wait times (default `claude -p … opus`):
- Trial prompt (around 02:00 on 2026-10-08, Claude Code 2.1.292): local context 30 seconds; entire PR 56 seconds
- Prompt recording for the first PR: one diff 19 seconds; symbol 21 seconds; entire PR 35 seconds. Each had 4 items, and all attached line locations were in the material. Actual questions through serve took 12–27 seconds
- Prompt recording after adding questions outside the code and internet access (all 4 re-recorded on 2026-10-15, with web added): with the default command, entire PR 26 seconds; symbol 26 seconds; one diff 10 seconds; selected lines 32 seconds (one of the 4 items in the one-diff answer was `known`, explaining takeIf). web took 20 seconds with codex-cli 0.162.1's `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check -s read-only -` (searched and opened kotlinlang.org once; of 3 items, one had `web` and two had `cite` for line 16). Asking codex through serve took around 30 seconds for both "What is ?:?" and "What is a use case?"; each had one `web`
- Prompt recording after adding instructions for reading selected lines (all 4 re-recorded around 09:35 on 2026-10-08): entire PR 24 seconds; symbol 28 seconds; one diff 16 seconds. Selected lines (8–11) took 19 seconds. In the selected-line answer, 2 of 4 items pointed to selected lines. Asking about selected lines through serve took 18 seconds
- Prompt with need and the PR body, ticket, and background (2026-11-04, Claude Code 2.1.318. The pr / symbol / hunk / lines recordings still use the earlier prompt): the why recording took 8 seconds (the lead says "the PR body says …", and 1 of 4 items is a fact from the ticket). Analyzing the fixture with `--no-lsp --body --ticket` and asking about domain.Order in a row through serve: "PromoService.apply は discount をどう使ってる？" took 3 calls and 16 seconds (search PromoService → read PromoService.kt and search discount → answer. Pointed to PromoService.kt lines 9–12 and 14). "Money の times はどう計算してる？丸めは？" took 3 calls and 21 seconds (read Money.kt and answered that Long division truncates). "なぜ promo を消して discount にしたの？" took 1 call and 10 seconds (from the PR body and ticket). In the first question, 1 of 4 items had a path the LLM wrote wrong (missing order/) and became Location could not be verified

### 10.6 Inferring structure (implemented 2026-10-09. `packages/core/src/structure/` and `packages/cli/src/structure.ts`)

An LLM call to show map bands (layers) even without `layers` in the config. ondina makes no assumptions about the structure of repositories using it. Rather than hard-code naming conventions (controller / service, etc.), have the LLM infer layer names, order, and assignments.

- **When**: only once, on the first `analyze` in that repository (when `.ondina/structure.json` is absent). For inferences saved before 2026-10-12 without test locations (`tests`), if the config also has no test locations, an `analyze` with LLM access passes the previous layer names and infers again once (keep the previous save on failure. With `--no-llm`, print how to infer again to stderr). Runs before the PR's first LLM call. Reuse the save afterward (inferring every time incurs cost). Do not infer if config has `layers`. With `--no-llm`, skip inference and set `structure.status: 'skipped'`. On failure, do not save; record `failed` and a reason, then retry on the next `analyze`. To infer again: `ondina structure [<ref>]`
- **Input (facts built by the program)**: group all tracked files at that commit (`git ls-tree -r`) by directory (root is `"."`, ascending order), attaching top-level declarations for `.kt` files (`class Order`, etc. tree-sitter's `collectDeclarations`, up to 20 per file). Read contents in one process with `git cat-file --batch`. If input JSON exceeds 500 KB (`STRUCTURE_INPUT_LIMIT`), drop declarations; if it still exceeds the limit, do not infer (include the count and the option to specify `layers` in the reason)
  ```json
  { "files": 33, "dirs": [ { "dir": "src/main/kotlin/com/example/server/crud", "files": [ { "name": "CustomerController.kt", "decls": ["class CustomerController", "class CustomerRequest"] } ] } ] }
  ```
- **Output**: `layers` (3–8; callers above callees. Names use the repository's vocabulary), `dirs` (layers for whole directories, including descendants. Only `"."` means direct child files only. On 2026-10-09, the actual LLM listed `"."` as outside, and interpreting this as the whole repository suppressed warnings), `files` (files where layers differ within one directory), `outside` (directories outside the code structure, such as docs and build configuration), and `tests` (test-code locations. Directories include descendants. Use files for directories mixed with production code. Used to identify tests. 5.2, item 7. 2026-10-12)
  ```json
  { "layers": [ { "name": "API", "why": "1 行" } ], "dirs": [ { "dir": "src/api", "layer": "API" } ], "files": [ { "path": "src/App.kt", "layer": "API" } ], "outside": ["docs"], "tests": ["api/src/test"] }
  ```
- **Checks (entirely by program. `verify.ts`)**: trim layer names and exclude empty or duplicate names. Exclude the ninth and later layers (`MAX_LAYERS`). `dirs` / `outside` must be existing directories (remove trailing `/` and leading `./`), `files` must be existing files, and layers must be names in `layers`. Keep only the first occurrence of a directory or file. If present in both `dirs` and `outside`, use `dirs`. Drop layers with no assignments. `tests` accepts only existing directories (`"."` is not allowed) or files, keeping the first occurrence only. Fail if no layers remain. Print each exclusion reason on its own line to analyze's stderr
- **When inferring again** (`ondina structure`): pass the previous layer names in input `previous.layers` and have it retain names and order wherever applicable (principle 7). With the actual LLM, names and order changed when omitted and were retained when supplied
- **Elapsed time** (2026-10-09, development machine, default `claude -p … opus`, small repositories with 25–31 files): first `analyze` took 3 minutes 12 seconds total (structure inference, language server, and two LLM calls combined). `ondina structure` took 38 seconds
- **Storage**: `{ version: 1, headSha, at, command, layers, dirs, files, outside, tests, known }` in `.ondina/structure.json` (`tests` added 2026-10-12. Saves without it are also read). `known` is all tracked files at inference time. Raw output is `.ondina/structure.llm.json` regardless of success or failure
- **Assignment (`assign.ts`)**: exact file match > deeper directory > shallower directory. Rebuild as the same `LayerRule[]` as config `layers` (first match from the top; escape glob special characters in paths), and pass through the same route to symbols, edges, and LLM input. For PR files matching no rule, have the first LLM call's `layerGuesses` choose from inferred layer names (pass the name list in input `layers`. No additional call)
- **Warning for unclassified files**: on every analyze, match the head file list against the rules and count files absent from `known` (added after inference) that belong to neither a layer nor `outside`. Set `warn` at 5% of the file count at inference time (at least 5 files). Show it in analyze's stderr and the entry notice, n more files cannot be classified in the structure (since inference on <date>). Run ondina structure to infer it again (「構成に分類できないファイルが n 件増えました（<日付> の推定以降）。ondina structure で推定し直せます」)
- **UI**: band order comes from `Analysis.structure.layers`. Append (inferred) to inferred band names and layer chips with `layerProvenance: 'llm'` (principle 2). If inference fails or is skipped, the entry notice still gives the reason and how to run it again (`MapNotice.tsx`). Tests identified by inferred test locations are Test (inferred) (「テスト（推定）」) (5.2, item 7)
- **Checking calls across layers**: inferred layers have no rules for allowed call directions, so do not check them (9.4)

### 10.7 Ticket selection (implemented 2026-10-12. `packages/core/src/ticket/choice.ts`)

Without `--ticket`, have it select the ticket addressed by this PR from candidates found in the PR title, branch name, and body (5.1). The LLM can return only a candidate id or null (principle 3).
