- **Input**: `pr` (original `title` (absent in local mode), `branch`, and `body`) and `candidates` (`id` (`c1` …), `label`, `places` (`in` / `line` / `match` = extracted string / `text` = that line))
  ```json
  { "pr": { "title": "[SHOP-421] 割引を足す", "branch": "feature/discount", "body": "…\n関連: SHOP-399" }, "candidates": [ { "id": "c1", "label": "SHOP-421", "places": [ { "in": "title", "match": "SHOP-421", "text": "[SHOP-421] 割引を足す" } ] } ] }
  ```
- **Instructions**: select the one ticket addressed by this PR. Do not select tickets mentioned only as related, reference, previous, or parent tickets. Do not select things shaped like ticket numbers that are not tickets (character encoding names, standard or version numbers, etc.). Use null if none. No tools; one JSON object only (same as 10.3)
- **Output**: `{ "choice": "c1" | null }`. Read missing `choice` as null
- **Checks**: exclude ids absent from the candidates and treat them as no selection; print The LLM selected a ticket outside the candidates (c9), so it was excluded (「LLM が候補にないチケット（c9）を選んだので除外しました」) to stderr
- **Storage**: ticket selection in 5.3

### 10.8 Requirement extraction (`packages/core/src/spec/`)

Extract what should hold in this PR from the retrieved ticket (5.1). The LLM selects diff IDs for each requirement. It no longer selects Hows. The program verifies the mapping and builds chapters (principle 3).

- **Input** (`buildSpecInput`; golden: `fixtures/order-service/expected/llm-spec-input.json`):
  ```json
  { "ticket": { "key": "SHOP-421", "title": "Apply discount codes to orders", "kind": "Story", "body": "Background\n…\nTasks\n- …" },
    "hunks": [ "…all hunks from the first input, including those outside every How" ],
    "rippleGroups": [ "…all groups from the first input, with representative and deviation diff lines" ],
    "previous": { "specs": [{ "text": "…", "hunkIds": ["…"] }] } }
  ```
  - Pass a parent ticket in the same shape as `parent` when available. Copy project purpose sentences from it; do not extract requirements from the parent
  - Include ticket number, title, type, and body text. Exclude status and retrieval time. `ticketPlainText` strips Jira HTML. Truncate at 30,000 characters and set `truncated: true`
  - Reuse all `hunks` and `rippleGroups` from the first input. Do not pass the author's explanation, the How list, or edges. Keep layer information already attached to the input diffs
  - Omit legacy saved requirements without `hunkIds` from `previous`; do not infer diff mappings from old How assignments
- **Output**: `{ "specs": [{ "text": "…", "hunkIds": ["…"] }], "why": ["…"], "parentWhy": ["…"] }`. Missing `hunkIds`, `why`, or `parentWhy` is read as an empty array
- **Instructions**: Up to 10 requirements in ticket order. One short sentence per item, without a full stop. Exclude background, assignees, and due dates. Add nothing absent from the ticket. Select hunks whose diff lines add or change the stated behavior. Exclude hunks merely in the same area or otherwise related. Tests checking the behavior may be selected. Select ripple representatives and deviations only when the requirement itself concerns that place. Only `hunks[].id`, `rippleGroups[].representative.hunkId`, and `rippleGroups[].deviations[].hunkId` are selectable. Return an empty array when none corresponds. Do not judge satisfaction or sufficiency
- **Quotes**: Copy 1–3 complete sentences verbatim for `why` (the ticket's reason, trigger, or purpose) and `parentWhy` (the parent project's purpose). Do not excerpt or summarize. Empty if absent. The program retains only sentences contained in the respective body. With `previous`, keep wording and mappings stable. Use no tools; return one JSON object. Examples use a different domain (inventory allocation)
- **Verification** (`applySpecs`): Report and drop unknown IDs. Deduplicate and save IDs in input hunk order. Trim whitespace and trailing full stops from text. Drop empty and duplicate requirements; retain at most 10. Assign IDs starting at `s1`. Derive `Spec.hows` from the selected `Hunk.hows`; the representative's `Hunk.hows` already includes its group's assignment. Derive `How.specIds` in reverse. The count message is `要求: N 件（対応する差分があるもの M 件）`
- **Chapters** (`buildChapters`, `packages/core/src/chapter/build.ts`): One `spec` chapter per Spec, in Spec order. Order selected diffs using the call-edge BFS shared with existing tours. Branches use calling-line order, then layers, FQ name, and ID. Diffs outside edges use hunk order. Keep an empty chapter when no diff corresponds. A diff may appear in multiple requirements
  - Append unselected import-only hunks to the first `spec` chapter containing another diff in the same file
  - Put remaining ripple members in `follow`: representative first within each group, then remaining members in hunk order. Exclude cores and deviations
  - Put everything else, including deviations, in `extra` in hunk order. Omit empty `follow` / `extra` chapters. Cover every hunk at least once
  - Save `Analysis.chapters` only when the ticket yields at least one Spec. Screen changes are a separate task
- **Fixture**: Preserve recorded text, `why`, and `parentWhy`. Mechanically convert old How mappings to non-group hunks, retaining cores. Six requirements; five have corresponding diffs. Expired-code and rate-value requirements are empty. The current ripple group has 22 hunks including its representative, OrderAdminView. Logger, FeatureFlags, and the PromoService deviation are `extra`. Goldens: `expected/specs.json` and `expected/chapters.json`
- Runs alongside the second LLM call. Cache by the prompt excluding `previous`; identical diffs and tickets reuse the recording
- **Measured** (2026-11-09, development machine, default `claude -p … opus`, record `llm/spec-output.json`): 22 seconds. 6 requirements; 5 have matching diffs, and “expired codes return 400” has none. Chapters (golden `expected/chapters.json`): 6 requirement chapters, 22 follow hunks, 6 hunks that match no requirement (2 in Logger, FeatureFlags, PromoService, one each in ApplyDiscountUseCaseTest and OrderRepositoryImplTest). Records from when it chose Hows (2026-10-12, 10-29) had 8 requirements

### 10.9 Per-hunk intent (`packages/core/src/intent/`)

Separate the author's stated reasons from guesses for each hunk. This change adds data only. Rendering follows separately.

- **Input** (`buildIntentInput`): reuse the first call's `hunks` and `rippleGroups`, including their diff lines. Eligible hunks are standalone hunks, ripple representatives, and deviations. Ripple members use their representative's intent
- Sources are `prBody`, `ticket.body`, `parent.body`, and `commits` (sha, title, body). Run even without a ticket. Convert HTML using `ticketPlainText`. Pass the full source bodies without truncation
- `commitNotes` reads non-merge commits in `baseSha..headSha`, oldest first. If the working-tree head is a tree, use `baseSha..HEAD`. Do not invent notes for uncommitted changes. Omit `Analysis.commits` when empty
- **Output**: `{ "intents": [{ "hunkId": "…", "notes": [{ "kind": "written", "text": "…", "from": "commit", "quote": "…", "commit": "sha" }], "ask": "…" }] }`
- `written` is a reason stated by the author, with a quote and source. `guess` is a short inference when no reason is written. `none` describes what is unwritten. Include both `guess` and `none` when an inference is possible. Both are LLM readings (provenance: llm). Do not judge correctness or quality
- `ask` is a short, polite draft question about an unwritten reason, using Japanese desu/masu. It is never sent. Remove it if any verified `written` note remains
- **Verification** (`applyIntents`): report and drop unknown or duplicate IDs. Keep input hunk order. Drop empty texts and hunks with no remaining notes. Use the quote comparison shared with spec: remove whitespace and Markdown emphasis/code markers, then check the named source body. Reject empty quotes and quotes consisting only of markers
- Check commit quotes against that commit's title and body. Accept a unique SHA prefix and store the full SHA. With no SHA, choose the first commit containing the quote. Unknown or ambiguous supplied SHAs, missing quotes, and unknown sources become `guess`, with an info line
- End with `意図: 差分 N 件（書いてある K 件、推定 G 件、書いていない M 件）`. Count each hunk by its strongest note: written > guess > none. The program counts; the LLM does not
- **Execution and caching**: after the first call succeeds, run alongside narration and spec extraction. Do not add to the second-call input. Pass prior intents in `previous` only for the same change with matching ticket and parent identities; ticketless runs may also carry prior intents. Hash the full prompt without `previous` for the cache key. Identical sources and diffs reuse the same record even when previous wording changes. Store raw output in `llm/<key>.intent.json`
- On failure, report `意図を書けませんでした: reason` and record the reason in `Analysis.llmIntents`. Continue analysis and narration. Omit empty `Analysis.intents`. If the first call does not succeed, skip intent and omit its run record
- **Fixture**: keep the commit message in one place, `discount-commit-message.txt`. Input golden: `expected/llm-intent-input.json`; verified output: `expected/intents.json`. `llm/intent-output.json` is real LLM output recorded with `record-llm-intent.mjs`
- **Measured** (2026-11-09, `claude -p --model opus`, 18 KB prompt): 60 seconds. 27 diffs. 14 written, 13 guessed (each with a "not written" note). It runs alongside the second call and requirement extraction, so analyze waits for the longest call

## 11. Config file `ondina.config.yaml`

```yaml
project: order-service
language: kotlin
sourceRoots: [src/main/kotlin, src/test/kotlin, src/main/resources]
packagePrefix: com.example          # UI でのパス短縮表示に使う

layers:
  - name: API
    match: ["**/api/**"]
  - name: Application
    match: ["**/application/**", "**/usecase/**"]
  - name: Domain
    match: ["**/domain/**"]
  - name: Infrastructure
    match: ["**/infra/**", "src/main/resources/db/**"]
  - name: Tests
    match: ["src/test/**"]

allowedDependencies:               # 無い方向の辺を「層を飛び越える呼び出し」として描く（事実のみ）
  API: [Application]
  Application: [Domain, Infrastructure]
  Infrastructure: [Domain]
  Tests: [API, Application, Domain, Infrastructure]

normalize:
  kotlin: [safeCall, lambdaIt, trailingComma, namedArgOrder]

lsp:                               # 9.1。2026-09-21 に実機で確認した形
  kotlin:
    command: kotlin-lsp            # 実体は bin/intellij-server（kotlin-lsp.sh は非推奨のラッパ）
    # 既定が socket（127.0.0.1:9999）なので --stdio は必須。
    # {cacheDir} は ondina が .ondina/<key>/lsp/head に置換する。使い回すと冷 59 秒 → 温 5〜7 秒
    args: ["--stdio", "--system-path", "{cacheDir}"]
    readyTimeoutSec: 600           # 準備中の知らせ（$/progress と取り込みの記録）がこの秒数なにも来なければ時間切れ（全体の上限は 60 分）
    # importNotification:         # 任意。取り込みの成否を伝える独自通知の読み方。省略時は kotlin の既定
    #   method: intellij/importLog
    #   succeededField: succeeded
    #   failedField: failed

llm:                                # 任意。LLM コマンド（5.3）。sh -c で起動し stdin にプロンプト、stdout に JSON。無ければ既定（claude -p …）
  command: claude -p --tools "" --max-turns 1 --model opus

theme: { mode: system, light: latte, dark: mocha, accent: mauve }   # 3.5。mode は system（OS に合わせる）/ light / dark
ide: { scheme: jetbrains, product: idea, project: order-service }
```

### 11.1 Loading (decided and implemented 2026-09-17)

- **Location**: root of the target repository (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`). Both `analyze` and `serve` read the same file. They do not read it from the head worktree
- **Responsibilities**: parsing and verification are in `packages/core/src/config/load.ts` (a pure function from string → normalized `OndinaConfig`. Parser pinned to `yaml` 2.9.1). `packages/cli/src/config.ts` opens the file
  - `analyze` puts fields affecting analysis (`project` / `language` / `sourceRoots` / `layers` / `allowedDependencies` / `normalize` / `lsp` / `llm`) in `Analysis.config`. Changing them requires rerunning `analyze` (`llm.command` is the team default. Locally, the UI's `.ondina/ui.json` takes precedence. 5.3)
  - `serve` reads display-only fields (`packagePrefix` / `theme` / `ide`) on each request and passes them to the UI through `GET /api/config`. Reload the browser to apply edits (no reanalysis or restart needed). The UI reads three inputs: `Analysis.json` / `ReviewState` / `DisplayConfig` (principle 5 is closed within these three)
- **When `layers` is omitted** (2026-10-09): structure inference assigns layers (10.6). Write `layers` to override inference
- **When test locations are omitted** (2026-10-12): without `Tests` in `layers` or `test` in `sourceRoots`, identify tests using structure inference's `tests` (5.2, item 7). Explicit settings take precedence over inference. Specifying `layers` without `Tests` prevents structure inference from running, so nothing is identified as a test (one line to analyze's stderr: Tests are not being identified (「テストを見分けていません」))
- **When `lsp` is omitted** (2026-10-09): use kotlin's default launch method, with the command from environment variable `ONDINA_LSP_COMMAND`, or the kotlin-lsp location if absent (9.1)
- **When absent**: use defaults (do not gate). `analyze` prints one line to stderr: `ondina.config.yaml がないので既定の設定で解析します`. Defaults: `sourceRoots: []`, `layers: []`, `allowedDependencies: {}`, `normalize: {}`, `lsp: {}`, `theme: { system, latte, mocha, mauve }`, `ide: { jetbrains, idea }` (`project` defaults to the target repository's directory name), no `packagePrefix` (no shortening)
- **Verification**: type-check only keys that are used; on a mismatch, `analyze` stops with an error and prints the key name and correction in one line (example: `ondina.config.yaml: layers[1].match は文字列の配列にしてください`). Ignore undefined keys. Duplicate `layers` names and `allowedDependencies` referring to layer names absent from `layers` are errors. `theme.mode` is `system` / `light` / `dark`; `theme.light` is a light flavor (latte); `theme.dark` is a dark flavor (frappe / macchiato / mocha); `theme.accent` accepts known accents only (light/dark comes from `dark` in `@catppuccin/palette`). Do not error on old `theme.flavor`; as in 3.5, read it as fixed to that flavor's light/dark mode (value is one of the four flavors). `ide.scheme` accepts only `jetbrains`. Syntax errors also stop `analyze`. Since `serve` cannot be stopped, print to stderr and render with defaults
- **Consumers (M0)**: `packagePrefix` → path shortening in the ⌘K list, etc. (`shortPath` in `files.ts`. The diff list's file column became a file tree on 2026-10-19 and stopped shortening paths. 3.4); `theme` → theme when `ui.json` lacks `theme` (3.5. After a UI change, `ui.json` takes precedence); `ide` → `IDEA で開く` (3.6). Glob assignment for `layers` (5.2, item 4) was implemented 2026-09-21 (`layer` in `Analysis.symbols`). **The order of `layers` affects assignment**, so edits require rerunning `analyze`. `normalize`: chapter 8. `lsp` is used in 9.1 (implemented 2026-09-21). **LSP command resolution order: environment variable `ONDINA_LSP_COMMAND` > yaml `lsp.<language>.command` > kotlin-lsp location** (the value is one executable path. It starts with `spawn(command, args)`, so two words cannot be passed). Use `args` as written in yaml; ondina replaces only `{cacheDir}` with `.ondina/<key>/lsp/head`
- fixture: `build-repo.sh` copies `fixtures/order-service/ondina.config.yaml` to the repository root

---

## 12. Comment draft intake

External AI agents only pass JSON. The UI lists it as drafts and sends only those adopted by the reviewer to GitHub.

```json
{
  "source": "claude-code",
  "headSha": "…",
  "comments": [
    { "path": "src/main/kotlin/com/example/order/api/OrderController.kt", "line": 21, "side": "RIGHT",
      "body": "[must] この経路では注文が 2 回保存される。",
      "note": "21 行目の repository.save() の前に呼ぶ ApplyDiscountUseCase.execute も、中で repository.save() を呼んでいる（ApplyDiscountUseCase.kt 18 行目）。" }
  ]
}
```

- **Body and note are separate** (2026-11-04): `body` is the text sent to the PR author on GitHub. `note` (optional) is an explanation only the reviewer reads and is never sent. The AI was putting too much reasoning in the body, which meant sending the author explanations they do not need. The body says only what happens or what to change, briefly. The reasoning (which line or call shows it) goes in the note. The writing rules are the same text in the `ondina-comments` skill and the AI review prompt (3.11) (core's `COMMENT_WRITING_RULES`; a cli test checks they match). The screen shows the note under the draft's body in muted text labeled 「説明（送信対象外）」 (the box under the line, closing, and the course summary). Only the body is editable. Only the body is sent to GitHub or returned to the AI. A whitespace-only note is dropped. The id is still built from the body, not the note

- CLI: `ondina comments add --from comments.json` (`-` for standard input)
- While serve is running: `POST /api/comments`
- Request AI review from the PR list (2026-10-22. 3.11 / 5.5): without a skill, serve launches an agent using the configured AI review command (「AI レビューのコマンド」) and accepts the same JSON from stdout. Intake is the same as below (`addComments`. ids are derived from content). The difference is that comments on lines absent from the diff are removed individually and counted (unlike `comments add`, the entire input is not rejected, because this is a one-time handoff that cannot be resubmitted)
- Find the version to comment on with `ondina list` (2026-10-12). Prints analyzed versions newest first, one per line, with tab-separated `<key> <headSha> <baseSha> <題>`. Agents comment using line numbers from the headSha version and read the diff with `git diff <baseSha> <headSha>` (using the current checkout or the PR's latest version shifts line numbers)
- **Adding drafts while the UI is open does not lose them** (2026-10-12). The UI reads state once when opening an analysis, and sends its entire local state with `PUT /api/state` on each save. Previously, drafts added while open disappeared on the next UI action. On PUT, serve retains AI drafts (source other than reviewer) found in the file but absent from the sent state, appending them at the end (`keepOutsideDrafts` in `store.ts`). Do not restore personal notes deleted in the UI, since they disappear from state (AI drafts are not deleted in the UI; state records their dismissal). When returning to the browser tab (`focus` / `visibilitychange`), the UI rereads state and adds only AI drafts not already listed (command `comments.arrived`. No save). Also reread the analysis list (2026-10-19. Analyses run through analyze in the terminal while the UI is open appear in the upper-left list. Previously it read only on opening and after Analyze again)
- **AI agent skills** (2026-10-12): `skills/ondina/SKILL.md` (open) and `skills/ondina-comments/SKILL.md` (add comments). `ondina update` installs them in `~/.agents/skills/` (5.5). Intended agents are Codex and OpenCode; both read this location (confirmed in both official docs). Flow: "Open the PR in ondina" → reviewer gets a broad understanding in the UI → "Comment in ondina" → reviewer reads drafts and finishes reviewing the PR. Both may be requested together as "Open it and comment." Explicit invocation is `$ondina` in Codex (official docs) and command `/ondina` in OpenCode (frontmatter `metadata.opencode/slash: "true"` makes it a TUI command. OpenCode official docs. Checked with `opencode run --command ondina`)
  - `ondina`: use only when ondina is named explicitly (analysis takes minutes and starts serve. Same as the difit skill). If `ondina list` has no row for the current head (`gh pr view --json headRefOid` for a PR; `git rev-parse` for two refs), wait for `ondina analyze` to finish with a timeout of at least 15 minutes → `ondina serve --background <key>` → `open <URL>` → provide the URL and say comments can be requested after viewing. If neither a PR number nor branch is given, open the current branch's PR (2026-10-13. `gh pr view --json number,title,state,headRefOid` without a number. In a worktree, use that worktree's branch). If the branch has no PR (`no pull requests found`) or another error occurs, ask for the number and stop; do not substitute a diff against main. Open merged or closed PRs too, mentioning their status. If comments were requested in the same request, continue to `ondina-comments`. Do not clone the repository for analysis (without the skill, Codex cloned into /tmp and analyzed there when unable to write `.git`. `.ondina/` did not remain in the target repository)
  - **worktree** (2026-10-13 / 2026-10-19): `.ondina/` sits under `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`. However, PR-number analyses (`.ondina/<番号>/`: analysis, review state, LLM saves, question conversations, base / head worktrees) are stored in `.ondina/` in the original folder (parent of `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` when it ends with `.git`), even when run from a worktree (`keyDir` in `store.ts`, `mainWorktree` in `git.ts`). One UI lists PRs analyzed in any worktree
    - On 2026-10-13 they were separate per worktree (not combined, because worktree usage could produce differences). On 2026-10-19, requesting the opening skill from another worktree reported a port conflict and started another serve. Combined only PR analyses so the running UI could be opened. PR analysis reads only gh and its own base / head worktrees, independent of the invoking worktree's working tree, so combining it introduces no differences
    - What stays in the invoking worktree's `.ondina/`: changes without a PR (`local`, because it analyzes that worktree's working tree), settings (`.ondina/ui.json`), and structure inference. Do not combine bare repositories or submodules
    - Also combine language-server files (`.ondina/lsp`) and `serve.json` in the original folder (2026-10-19. "Across worktrees" in 9.1). The language server's work does not depend on the invoking worktree (it only switches the language-server worktree to the base / head versions)
    - To share settings with the original checkout too, place a symlink to the original `.ondina` when creating a worktree (local also becomes one location). In that case, write `.ondina` in `.git/info/exclude` (a trailing `/` does not ignore the symlink. Checked with local git)
  - **`serve --background`** (2026-10-12): without the skill, Codex launched `ondina serve` within its own session, so the UI stopped when Codex finished (the provided link could not open). `nohup … &` also stopped; only a process detached into a separate process group (setsid) remained. `--background` relaunches itself as `detached` (setsid), writes output to `.ondina/serve.log`, then prints the `ondina serve: <URL>` line to stdout and returns once it appears (`packages/cli/src/background.ts`). First check `GET /api/repo`; if serve is running for this repository, return its URL without starting; if another repository's serve or something else occupies the port, start on a free port. Reuse serve in another worktree of the same repository when opening a PR number (PR analyses are in the original folder and appear in that UI. 2026-10-19). For local, do not reuse it; start on a free port and say Changes without a PR cannot be shown in that UI (「PR のない変更はその画面に表示できない」). If it exits before a URL appears or none appears within 15 seconds, exit 1 with the log as the reason
  - **`serve list` / `stop` / `restart`** (2026-10-23): AI agents started serve on various ports, making them hard to identify. A serve running since before `ondina update` also served the new UI with old code, causing theme settings not to save (`--background` reuses running serve, so rerunning leaves the old instance). Discover listening processes without a registry file (`packages/cli/src/servers.ts`). List node listeners (pid and port) with `lsof -nP -iTCP -sTCP:LISTEN -a -c node -Fpn`, send `GET /api/repo` to each port (2-second timeout), and count as serve only responses whose pid matches lsof's pid. The UI dev server (vite) proxies `/api` to serve and responds with a different pid, so it is not counted. No registry file because kill -9 would leave one behind, and it would not find serve instances launched before this mechanism (`/api/repo` has existed since 2026-10-12. Earlier serve instances are not listed). If lsof is missing, give the reason and exit 1
    - `list`: print a table of port / pid / start time (MM/DD HH:mm) / version / folder (home as `~`) to stdout. Align columns by terminal width (full-width characters count as 2). If a version differs from the currently installed clone's commit or is missing (serve predating this change), append (before update) (「（更新前）」) and suggest `ondina serve restart <そのポート>...` on stderr. If the current version is unknown (not a clone), only instances without a version count as before update
    - `stop`: send SIGTERM and wait up to 10 seconds for the port to stop responding with that pid (serve closes its listener and exits on SIGTERM). If it does not stop, suggest `kill -9 <pid>` and exit 1. If any specified port is not serve, stop none; show the list and exit 1. Also include the list when no arguments are given
    - `restart`: after stopping, launch as `--background` does in the same folder on the same port (`serveInBackground`. Launch the ondina currently being run). Serve instances in the same folder share a log (`.ondina/serve.log`), so process one at a time. If the folder is gone (worktree deleted), exit 1 without stopping (launching would recreate the folder)
    - The guidance when launching with `--background` now also says "To stop, run `ondina serve stop <ポート>`" (previously `kill <pid>`). The two skills give the same stop instructions
  - `ondina-comments`: do not analyze. Steps: (if no PR number, use the current branch's PR as `ondina` does. If none, ask for the number and stop) → `ondina list` → if no row for the requested PR, run `ondina` first if opening was also requested; otherwise say "Open it in ondina first" and stop → read the diff → pass JSON to `ondina comments add --from -` → report the count and AI drafts in the wrap-up UI. Start each body with one of `[must]` (must fix before merge) / `[imo]` (opinion) / `[ask]` (question), using Japanese and one point per comment. Do not send to GitHub, directly edit `.ondina/`, mark no issues, or add personal notes. ondina does not validate the labels (principle 8)
- Both find `.ondina/<key>/<headSha>.json` from `headSha` and append to its `ReviewState.comments` with `state: 'draft'`. `id` is the first 12 digits of `sha1(source, path, line, side, body)`, so submitting the same JSON twice does not duplicate comments
- **Carry over to a new version** (2026-10-13. 3.0): when new commits are added to a PR and it is reanalyzed, move unsent notes and AI drafts (`draft` / `edit` / `adopted`) to the new version (the option not to move them was rejected). For head-side lines, compare full text before and after (`Analysis.files`) using a line diff; if every line in the range is unchanged, shift line numbers and move it. For ranges crossing base and head (from deleted lines to added lines below, or unchanged/added lines to deleted lines below), intervening lines split across sides cannot be tracked with a line diff, so move them only when the enclosing diff is unchanged before and after (same id and `lines`). Move base-side lines as-is only when baseSha is unchanged. Leave changed lines and items without full text in the previous version; report their count in one line below the band (`carried.left`). Also move items whose destination is no longer in GitHub's diff (such as unchanged lines at the diff's edge in the previous version when that change is reverted); show Cannot send to GitHub in wrap-up. Do not move sent or dismissed items
- Sending (implemented 2026-09-17; separate handling added 2026-10-06): UI send button → `POST /api/comments/send/:key/:sha` → serve sends `{ commit_id: headSha, comments: [{path, line, side, start_line?, start_side?, body}] }` with `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/{n}/reviews --hostname <host> --method POST --input -`. Combine only drafts with `state: 'adopted'` (including personal notes) on lines in GitHub's diff into one review (no partial success)
  - **Send as an unpublished (pending) review on GitHub** (2026-10-21): omitting `event` creates the review without publishing it (`state: PENDING`). The reviewer checks it on GitHub's diff page before publishing. Previously it sent with `event: 'COMMENT'`, publishing immediately on press
  - GitHub allows only one unpublished review per person per PR. Sending a second gives `422 User can only have one pending review per pull request` (the reason appears only in gh's stdout JSON; stderr has only `HTTP 422`). Before sending, count GraphQL `pullRequest.reviews(states: PENDING)` entries with `viewerDidAuthor` true (the viewer's own); if at least one, do not send and show Publish or discard the review on GitHub, then send again (「GitHub で公開するか破棄してから、もう一度送ってください」) (`pendingReviews` in `store.ts`). Whether other people's pending reviews are visible is unverified, so filter by authorship to avoid counting them even if visible. Adding to an existing pending review (GraphQL `addPullRequestReviewThread`) was not adopted
  - Checked with a test PR on 2026-10-21: without `event`, body plus line comments, line comments only, and body only all create `PENDING` reviews and return `html_url`. Sent line, range, and outside-diff notes from ondina's UI; GitHub received one pending review (2 line comments and a body) (GraphQL returned the correct lines, 13 and 12–14. REST `reviews/{id}/comments` has null `line` and only `position` while pending). Publishing through the API without a body retains the original body. Publishing with "Submit review" in GitHub's UI replaces the body with the text field's contents. Publishing with an empty field erased the body (notes on lines outside the diff). The 2 line comments were published with correct lines (2026-10-21). This led to disabling notes on lines outside the diff ("Send only lines in the diff" below)
  - **gh api destination** (2026-10-15): `gh api` does not derive the host from the current repository; it uses gh's default host. This is `GH_HOST`, or, if absent, the sole gh login host; with two or more, github.com (confirmed in gh v2.102.0's `pkg/cmd/api/api.go` and go-gh v2.16.1's `auth.DefaultHost`). Only `{owner}/{repo}` is filled from the repository, so it queried github.com with a GitHub Enterprise Server owner/repo, and Viewed failed with `Could not resolve to a Repository`. `gh pr view` uses the repository's host (analyze had passed), so before sending, extract host and owner / repo from the PR URL (`https://<host>/<owner>/<repo>/pull/<n>`) returned by `gh pr view <n> --json url`, then send with `--hostname` and explicit owner / repo (`GhTarget` in `pr.ts`). Applies to both comment sending and GitHub Viewed (12.1). Do not cache the destination; retrieve on every call (one additional gh call). Append (destination: host/owner/repo) (「（宛先: host/owner/repo）」) to Viewed failure messages (in environments without copy/paste, users cannot paste gh output, so the notice alone must identify the destination. Do not add it for comment sending, which prints all gh arguments)
  - **Send only lines in the diff** (2026-10-22): send line comments only for lines (both start and end for a range) contained in the GitHub diff chunks (`Analysis.githubDiff`) on their respective sides. Do not attach a review body. Determine this on demand with `onGithubDiff` in web's `memo.ts` and core's `review/github-diff.ts` (copied into web because it cannot import core. Principle 5), without writing it to state. The UI disallows notes on lines outside the diff (3.3). `comments add` / `POST /api/comments` reject the entire input if any draft targets a line outside the diff (There are n comments on lines outside GitHub's diff. … (「GitHub の差分に出ない行へのコメントが n 件あります。…」), plus a line list. `POST` returns 400). Do not send adopted items written earlier or carried over outside the diff; leave them adopted. If none can be sent, show There are no comments that can be sent to GitHub. Comments on lines outside GitHub's diff cannot be sent (「GitHub に送れるコメントがありません。GitHub の差分に出ない行のコメントは送れません」). For old analyses without `githubDiff`, send everything as line comments (422 if any lines are outside the diff)
    - Previously (2026-10-06–2026-10-21), notes on lines outside the diff went into the review's overall body (under **Notes on lines outside the diff (n)** (「**差分に出ない行へのメモ（n 件）**」), each with `パス`, line n, a line link, and body. The UI had an Included in body (「本文に入る」) chip). Stopped because publishing in GitHub's UI erases the body. Adopted GitHub's rules, without adding a copy button or publishing those notes separately first
  - `analyze` records GitHub diff chunks from `@@` in `git diff -U3 --no-renames --diff-algorithm=default <baseSha> <headSha>` (`githubDiff` in `git.ts`. Golden file: `expected/github-diff.json`)
  - **Checked with an actual PR on 2026-10-06 (fixture pushed to a disposable private repository)**: all 39 GitHub PR diff chunks matched `git diff -U3`. Line comments can attach to context lines at chunk edges (third line from a change). Lines outside chunks get `422 Line could not be resolved`. Ranges pass if both endpoints appear in the diff, even across two chunks (intervening lines may be outside chunks). A start outside a chunk gets 422. Ranges from deleted lines (LEFT) to added lines (RIGHT) also pass. A link to lines in the same repository placed alone on one line in the body displays as a code excerpt box (head / base sha, single line / range). `event: COMMENT` passes with an empty body if line comments exist; body only and body plus line comments also pass. Also sent range notes and outside-diff notes from ondina's UI and confirmed arrival as line comments and body (excerpt boxes)
  - **Checked on the same PR on 2026-10-15**: reverse ranges, from head-side lines to deleted lines below (`start_side: RIGHT` → `side: LEFT`), also pass. Both unchanged line (head 3) → deleted line (base 10) and added line (head 6) → deleted line (base 8). GitHub records the direction as sent (read-back `start_side` / `side` match). Dragging from an unchanged line to deleted lines directly below in unified view gives this direction. In note headings and body line descriptions, write start and end with their respective side's line numbers (`base 6 行目〜head 11 行目` / `head 3 行目〜base 10 行目`. Body links also use each side's sha). Previously base → head was assumed, reversing line numbers for this direction. How GitHub's UI highlights this range, and which direction GitHub itself sends for this drag, have not been viewed
  - Success: set sent drafts to `state: 'sent'`, write the state file, and return `{ sent: id[], url }`. `url` is the PR diff page (remove `#…` from `html_url` and add `/files`. The place to check and publish the unpublished review). The UI shows a Sent (「送信済み」) chip, Sent. It has not been published yet. Check it on GitHub, then publish it. (「送信しました。まだ公開されていません。GitHub で確かめてから公開してください。」), and a link, without action buttons. Since `id` is derived from content, resubmitting the same JSON leaves it sent without duplication
  - Failure (gh missing, expired authentication, 422 for lines outside the diff, etc.): return 502 with gh's stderr. Drafts remain adopted. The UI shows the message below the button. Fix and press again
  - For `local` analyses (no PR number), return to AI instead of sending to GitHub ("Return to AI" below). Zero adopted items gives 400
  - The tool does not Approve; `GitHub で承認` in wrap-up (3.9) only opens the GitHub PR page in a new tab. The send button is also in wrap-up's right column (below the two boxes for personal notes and AI drafts) (moved from the diff list on 2026-10-03). There is no CLI `ondina comments send`

### 12.0 Return to AI (changes without a PR. 2026-10-15)

Show pre-PR changes created by an AI agent as a pseudo-PR and return reviewer comments to that running AI session. Display uses working-tree analysis from 5.1 (`analyze --base <ref>`).

- **Return**: for `local` analyses, wrap-up's send button becomes `AI に返す（メモ n · 下書き m）`. Pressing it makes serve (`POST /api/comments/send/local/:sha`; `returnComments` in `store.ts`) write adopted items (personal notes and adopted AI drafts) into one file in inbox `.ondina/local/returned/<返した時刻>-<headSha 先頭 7>.json`, then mark them `sent` (Sent chip. Not carried over). Response: `{ sent, waiting }`
- **Receive**: `ondina comments wait`. Checks the inbox every second, takes all unread items in return order, moves them to `returned/done/` (reads only those it moved first, so two simultaneous waiters do not both receive the same item), prints `{ comments: [{ headSha, path, line, side, startLine?, startSide?, body, source }] }` to stdout, and exits. Immediate if items have arrived; otherwise waits until arrival. Line numbers refer to that headSha version
- **Delivery to a running session**: agent-independent (works with Claude Code, Codex, or OpenCode. No vendor-specific notification mechanism). Claude Code resumes the session when a background command finishes. Codex and OpenCode wait in the foreground with a long timeout. Rerun if interrupted (returned comments remain until received)
- **Make non-delivery visible**: while waiting, `comments wait` places `.ondina/local/waiting/<pid>` and removes it on completion, Ctrl+C, or termination. When returning comments, serve checks whether there is a marker for a live pid and reports it in `waiting` (markers left after forced termination are not counted because the pid is dead). Below the button, wrap-up shows Returned to AI. The waiting AI will receive them (「AI に返しました。待っている AI が受け取ります」) or Returned to AI. No AI is waiting now. Tell the AI, "Read the comments returned through ondina" (「AI に返しました。いま待っている AI はいません。AI に「ondina で返したコメントを読んで」と伝えてください」)
- `local` analyses have no `githubDiff` (not sent to GitHub, so notes can be written on any line. AI can read comments on any line). Wrap-up's note about missing GitHub diff ranges is also omitted for `local`
- After AI fixes, rerun `analyze --base <ref>` with the same title. The UI checks the current working tree every 2 minutes; if an analysis is ready, it shows Analysis for new version … is ready (「新しいバージョン … の解析ができました」) below the band (3.0. Switching carries over notes not yet returned)
- **Skill** `skills/ondina-preview/SKILL.md`: write a title and description (same as a PR body) to a file outside the repository → `ondina analyze --base <既定のブランチ> --title … --body …` → `ondina serve --background local` → `open` → `ondina comments wait` (in the background if possible) → fix or answer each item → report what was done → rerun analyze → return to wait. Continue until the reviewer says finished. Do not commit or create a PR unless requested. The `ondina` skill routes here instead when asked to show AI-created changes without a PR

Example from Claude Code (script on the side that has review comments written):
```
claude -p "このPRをレビューし、上記JSON形式で出力" --output-format json | ondina comments add --from -
```

### 12.1 GitHub Viewed (file-level viewed marker)

2026-10-13 (decided one question at a time). Toggle the "Viewed" checkbox in GitHub PR Files changed to match ondina's No issues. GitHub's viewed marker is file-level only (no hunk-level API. Chapter 16).

- **When to mark**: when the last diff location in a file is marked No issues (through a course's No issues · Next, the diff list's No issues, or any group member). Separate from sending comments
- **Remove**: if removing No issues means the file is no longer "all," remove GitHub Viewed too (even if the user marked Viewed directly on GitHub)
- **Direction**: ondina → GitHub only. Viewed set on GitHub does not become ondina No issues; show it as a check in the diff list row (marker in 3.4). Read it when opening the analysis and returning to the tab. Do not save it (GitHub owns this information). Treat `DISMISSED` (changed since viewing; GitHub's UI also unchecks it) as not Viewed
- **Determination**: after web's `reduce`, if `ReviewState.viewed` changes within the same analysis, compare the before/after sets of "all" files (`viewedFiles` / `viewedChange` in `files.ts`). Newly all = mark; no longer all = unmark. Do not send on opening an analysis (preexisting No issues). Do not send for local, which has no PR. Combine marking/unmarking from one action into one GraphQL request (mutations with aliases `m0` / `u0` …), sending one at a time in press order
- **Sending**: serve calls `gh api graphql --hostname <host>` (`packages/cli/src/github-viewed.ts`). Like comment sending, derive the destination from the PR URL returned by `gh pr view` (chapter 12, "gh api destination"). Read the PR node id and current head (`headRefOid`), and send `markFileAsViewed` / `unmarkFileAsViewed` only when head matches the analysis headSha (3 gh calls per action: one for the destination, one to read, one mutation combining mark/unmark). Commits pushed between reading and sending cannot be detected (`markFileAsViewed` accepts only `pullRequestId` and `path`, with no head argument). If they differ, do not send; return 409 (do not mark changes from commits added after analysis as Viewed when they have not been viewed in ondina). Call gh asynchronously (`runGhAsync`) so the round trip does not block other serve requests
- **On failure** (2026-10-13. Chosen from 3 working prototypes: persistent marker in the top band / several-second notice at bottom right / only in the diff list. `components/GithubToast.tsx`): show a bottom-right notice for 6 seconds (keep it while hovered). Use polite Japanese (`docs/wording.md`): Could not mark OrderAdminView.kt as Viewed on GitHub (「OrderAdminView.kt を GitHub で Viewed にできませんでした」) (plural: "3 files …" (「3 ファイルを…」); unmarking: "Could not remove GitHub Viewed from …" (「…の GitHub の Viewed を外せませんでした」); both: "Could not change GitHub Viewed (n files)" (「GitHub の Viewed を変更できませんでした（n ファイル）」)), followed by gh's error text and Retry (「再試行」) (resend the same content. On success: "Marked … as Viewed on GitHub" (「…を GitHub で Viewed にしました」)). If head differs, show only The PR has newer commits than the analysis, so GitHub Viewed was not changed. Run analyze again (「PR に解析より新しいコミットがあるため、GitHub の Viewed は変更しませんでした。analyze を実行し直してください」), without retry. If reading fails on opening, show Could not load GitHub Viewed (「GitHub の Viewed を読み込めませんでした」) (retry rereads. Do not notify on failure to reread when returning to the tab). One notice at a time; newer replaces older. Appears in every view
- No issues is a viewed marker, not approval (principle 8). Only mark Viewed; do not submit a review or Approve
- **Checked with an actual PR on 2026-10-13 (this feature's PR analyzed with `--no-llm --no-lsp`)**: marking `docs/design.md` No issues in the diff list changed GitHub's `viewerViewedState` to `VIEWED`; removing it returned it to `UNVIEWED`. Viewed set on `docs/roadmap.md` on GitHub appeared only as a row check on reopening (ondina's No issues count remained 0). Making gh fail with `GH_TOKEN=bad` showed Could not load GitHub Viewed on opening, and Could not mark design.md as Viewed on GitHub (「design.md を GitHub で Viewed にできませんでした」) plus `gh api graphql に失敗しました: gh: Bad credentials (HTTP 401)` on press, at bottom right, disappearing after 6 seconds. Error text includes only gh's subcommand (`runGhAsync`. Printing the entire GraphQL argument made the notice unreadable)

## 13. Development plan

At the end of each milestone, actually use it to review your own PR. The condition for proceeding is "After using it, do you want to keep it?"

**Progress (done / in progress / not started), per-item checklists, and remaining tasks outside milestones are maintained in `docs/roadmap.md`** (with a Mermaid diagram. Moved from here on 2026-09-17). This chapter contains only milestone purposes and completion criteria.

### M0: Make it work as a diff viewer (diff list only)
- Scope: repository structure and `ondina.config.yaml`; base/head with `gh` + `git worktree`; semantic hunks + stable IDs with tree-sitter-kotlin; Shiki lexical highlighting; diff list (file list, side-by-side/unified diff, line numbers, persistent review state, comment draft intake and GitHub sending); `IDEA で開く`; loading `ondina.config.yaml`
- Completion criterion: usable in place of GitHub's Files changed

### M1: By purpose (How decomposition) and explanation
- Scope: follow-on detection (chapter 8. AST checks first, LLM fallback later); LLM labeling (chapter 10) and Stage 3 verification (preserve IDs with `previous`); by purpose view; explain view; progress meter
- Completion criterion: can measure "lines read closely" on a 1000-line PR, with a clear reduction

### M2: Course (formerly tour) and LSP
- Scope: kotlin-lsp launch, wait, and failure fallback; semantic token overlay (chapter 7) and finalized mapping to Catppuccin for JetBrains; callHierarchy → edges, type labels, cross-layer checks; elkjs map, course stops, and in-tool jumps
- Completion criterion: can follow one How's path without opening the IDE and understand the baseline

### M3: Operations
- Scope: install by cloning (5.5. Done); analyze and artifact caching in CI (PR open / synchronize); file-level viewed synchronization with GraphQL `markFileAsViewed`; team distribution (`npx ondina`)

### M4: Refinement
- Scope: unresolved UI in 3.8 (collapse map for long core hunks, overview across Hows, spot-check expansion of follow-on members); LLM fallback for follow-on detection and ArchUnit rule loading; verify a second language (TypeScript or Java) works by replacing only layer settings and tree-sitter grammar

---

## 14. Future branch: IntelliJ plugin

kotlin-lsp provides matching highlights and jumps, removing the main motivation for a plugin. Two benefits remain: "the team only needs to install a plugin" and "fully native editor operations."

When migrating:
- Embed the Web UI as-is with JCEF (the IDE's built-in Chromium) for entry, by purpose, and course map sections
- Replace code display with the IDE's `Editor` / `DiffManager` (highlighting and jumps come directly from the IDE)
- Keep the `Analysis.json` and `ReviewState` formats

To keep this branch open, keep the Web UI limited to "read JSON and render," without Node-specific processing in the UI.

---

## 15. Evaluation metrics

| Metric | Measurement |
|---|---|
| Lines read closely | Line count of core + deviations + unattributed. Do not count follow-on group members. Target 200–300 lines for a 1000-line PR |
| Time to Approve | From opening the PR to Approve, using GitHub timestamps |
| Explanation test | Can you explain the PR verbally to a colleague immediately after Approve without looking at it? (Self-report is acceptable) |
| Usefulness of detection | Number of unattributed items, deviations, cross-layer calls, and claims without diffs that actually led to comments |
| Reproducibility | Whether two analyze runs on the same headSha give matching Hows and assignments |

---

## 16. Risks and unresolved issues

| Item | Details | Response |
|---|---|---|
| kotlin-lsp is alpha | Partially closed source. No Kotlin Multiplatform (KMP) support. Behavior may change | Implement 9.5's fallback from the start. Pin the version |
| Gradle import cost | Minutes or more for large projects. Also uses memory | Assume CI. Provide `ondina analyze --no-lsp` locally |
| Base-side semantic highlighting | Requires 2 instances | Accept lexical-only for the first version |
| Attribute differences between Catppuccin for JetBrains and VS Code | Annotations, etc. may have different colors | Finalize 7.2's mapping from the JetBrains attribute table (once) |
| Coverage of follow-on normalization | Notational differences cause same-form items to become deviations | Exception table in 8.9. Being shown as a deviation is safe (a person reads it) |
| LLM output variation | How grouping changes between runs | Preserve with `previous`. Temperature 0. Do not reassign unchanged hunks |
| Hunk-level viewed synchronization | GitHub has no API | Keep our own. File-level only with `markFileAsViewed` (12.1. Mark when all file diffs are No issues) |
| Long core hunks | Map and code cannot coexist | 3.8. Toggle map collapse |
| Renames | Stable IDs change, breaking review state continuity | Accept. Carry over using `git diff -M` information in the future |

(2) Courses look up hunks by the caller symbol's path, so before-change lines in renamed files do not match. (3) Work inside the same class (the mockup's "inside") and DB tables (the mockup's orders table) do not come from the call hierarchy, so they are not kept. Behaviors share how flows read calls (`callsOutOf` in `behavior/build.ts`, `behavior/calls.ts`), and group titles (themes) go into the first LLM call's `previous`, so removing them is a separate PR that also changes the LLM prompts and recordings (TODO)

No data holds per-step values (`Analysis.summary.example` is only one sentence written from the test diff). An LLM guess is often wrong and cannot cover behaviors without tests, so after comparing mockups they are left out for now. To show them, first confirm where the values would come from
- Name finalized as `ondina`. npm's `ondina` is available (as of 2026-09-13); no prominent same-name project on GitHub. Use `ondina` consistently for CLI command, config file, and cache directory
- README's first line is "A review support tool for reading large PRs." (The tagline was dropped before publication). Do not use logos or character images from the work (the name is a common word, so there is no trademark issue)

## 18. Controls, motion, and shell strategy (toward a desktop app)

Goal: a flowing UI/UX. With both mouse and keyboard, no waiting, comfortable motion, and no friction.

### 18.1 Shell strategy: one UI, three containers
- Keep the UI "a pure function reading `Analysis.json` and `ReviewState` and rendering." Confine Node-specific processing (Shiki, tree-sitter, LSP client, git) to `packages/core`; do not call it directly from the UI
- Three containers, each with the same UI bundle
  1. Browser: `ondina serve` (M0)
  2. Desktop: **Tauri 2** is the first choice. Small binary, OS WebView, worktree / LSP / git handled in Rust. URL scheme `ondina://pr/4821`, menu bar, notifications (CI analysis completion), multiple windows, global shortcuts
  3. IntelliJ: JCEF (chapter 14)
- Tauri caveat: WebView differs by OS (macOS WebKit / Windows WebView2 / Linux WebKitGTK). Since modern CSS such as `color-mix()`, View Transitions, and `:has()` is used, retain an Electron fallback for Linux
- Calling `core` on desktop: bundle Node as a sidecar or port to Rust. Use a sidecar for the first version

### 18.2 Interaction model: everything is a command
- Every operation has a command ID (`view.home` / `view.hows` / `view.diff` / `view.course` (2026-09-30. Previously `view.a`–`view.d`), `home.tab`, `home.select <コース>`, `settings.open [タブ]` / `settings.tab <タブ>` / `settings.close`, `ui.keymap <変更>` / `ui.keymap.reset` (shortcut keys. 2026-10-20), `ui.pane <列> <px | null>` (column widths. Drag and double-click dividers. 2026-10-20), `hunk.next` / `hunk.prev`, `hunk.markViewed`, `how.select <n>`, `how.clear`, `group.toggle`, `group.reveal`, `course.enter <コース> [停留所]`, `course.go`, `course.ok`, `course.forward` / `course.prev`, `course.jump <停留所>`, `course.complete` (wrap-up if the last course), `course.again`, `course.leave`, `course.map`, `file.full` (2026-10-13. Previously `course.full`), `fold.open <パス> <行の key>` (band for lines far from changed lines. 3.4), `find.open` / `find.query <文字>` / `find.next` / `find.prev` / `find.close` (search within diffs. 3.4. 2026-10-27), `view.closing`, `closing.approve`, `diff.open <hunk>`, `diff.viewport <一番上のファイル> <見えている差分> <合図の番号>` (diff list scroll notification. No key binding. 3.4. 2026-10-20), `comment.open <下書き> [コース]`, `enter` (dispatched by view), `map.zoom`, `map.peek <シンボル>` / `map.unpeek`, `rel.open <箱> [向き] [層] [相手]` / `rel.toggle` / `rel.big` / `rel.close` / `rel.select <相手>` (drawer for unchanged counterparts. 3.3. 2026-10-11), `jump.callee`, `jump.ide`, `diff.mode.sbs` / `diff.mode.unified`, `comment.adopt` / `comment.edit` / `comment.text` / `comment.discard` (`comment.text` is text being edited. 2026-10-13), `memo.pick <パス> <side> <行> [extend]` / `memo.draft` / `memo.cancel` / `memo.save` / `memo.edit` / `memo.update` / `memo.delete` (3.3. 2026-10-06), `ask.toggle` / `ask.close` / `ask.draft` / `ask.send` / `ask.retry` / `ask.stop` / `ask.cite <行の場所>` (3.10. 2026-10-08. Answers, failures, and history arrive through `ask.done` / `ask.failed` / `ask.loaded`), `github.retry` / `github.toast.close <番号>` (GitHub Viewed notices. 12.1. 2026-10-13. Read/write results arrive through `github.loaded` / `github.loadFailed` / `github.set` / `github.setFailed`)). Mouse clicks and keys invoke the same commands
- Command palette (equivalent to IDEA's Search Everywhere. `⌘K` / `Ctrl+K`). Search across Hows, symbols, and files and jump to them (implemented 2026-09-21. `packages/web/src/palette.ts` and `components/Palette.tsx`)
  - Search only these three kinds of "places," without operations. Individual follow-on group member symbols can also be found (collapsing a group into one row serves the by purpose view)
  - Fuzzy matching (typed characters may be nonconsecutive if their order matches). Try different starting positions and choose the match with the fewest runs. Underline matched characters, showing on the row why it appeared (to compensate for scores being invisible)
  - Sort lexicographically by (number of runs, first matched position, type How → symbol → file, original order). Do not combine scores. List up to 20 results and show the overflow count
  - On selection: How filters to that How in the by purpose view / file opens in the diff list / symbol selects its hunk (from entry or course views that do not show the hunk, move to the diff list; expand a collapsed follow-on group if needed; clear How filtering)
  - Do not open alongside the keymap display (`?`) (they share `.overlay`; opening either closes the other). Do not open before the analysis is loaded. Always route `Esc` to the `escape` command, keeping closing-order decisions in one place in the reducer
- Input fields (palette, notes, questions, settings) leave composition keys for Japanese and other IMEs to the IME rather than treating them as actions (`Enter` to commit composition, `Esc` to cancel, arrows to select candidates) (2026-10-20. Previously the palette selected its first candidate on composition-committing `Enter`, while composition-canceling `Esc` closed the palette, note editor, or drawer). Detection uses `composingKey` in `keymap.ts` (`isComposing` or keyCode 229. Safari emits keydown after composition ends with `isComposing` false, so keyCode is also checked)
- Keys can be changed in settings' (3.0) Shortcut keys tab (2026-10-20. Chose "add to settings" from two prototypes. Previously replacement through `keymap.json` remained a plan; three hard-coded tables in `keymap.ts` were used, with the `?` list and in-view key labels manually written separately)
  - The action table `KEY_ACTIONS` (`packages/web/src/commands/keymap.ts`) is the single source of truth. Each action has a command, default key, settings row name, `?` list description, and heading (Everywhere (「どの画面でも」) / Courses / Entry / Fixed keys (「変えられないキー」)). Key resolution (`resolveKey`), the `?` list, settings tab, and in-view key labels (`<Key>` in `components/Key.tsx`; `D` and `⌘K` in the top band, `B` `M` `O` `R` `⇧M` `←` in courses, `F` in diffs, etc.) all derive from this table and changed keys. Hide in-view labels for actions with removed keys (the `⌘K` button becomes Palette (「パレット」))
  - Save only changed actions in `keymap` (3.8) in `.ondina/ui.json` (action name → key array; empty array means no key). Later default changes apply to unchanged actions. Action names are command IDs (only `home.tab` with arguments uses `home.tab.map` / `home.tab.text`). Key syntax is `KeyboardEvent.key` (uppercase `M` for Shift letters), `Mod+k` for ⌘ / Ctrl, space-separated `g i` for sequences, and `Space` for space. Ignore undefined action names, changes to `Enter` / `Esc`, and malformed values (when edited by hand), retaining defaults
  - Settings tab (`components/KeysSettings.tsx`): two columns (Everywhere on the left; Courses, Entry, Fixed keys on the right). Press a key field to show Press a key… (「キーを押す…」), then replace it with the key pressed. The field captures keys during recording without forwarding them to view actions. `Esc` during recording only cancels recording. `Tab` moves focus instead of being recorded; leaving the field stops recording. Add with ＋ (「＋」), remove with Remove (「外す」) during recording. Changed rows have a dot and Reset to default (「既定に戻す」); if any rows changed, show Reset all to defaults (「すべて既定に戻す」) below. If a key is assigned elsewhere (including overlap with the first key of a sequence), notify below the row and offer Swap (「入れ替える」) (only when changing an existing field), Move here (「こちらに移す」), or Cancel. `Enter` / `Esc` are fixed; pressing them shows Fixed keys. Only Open in IDEA (whose default is a sequence) is set by pressing two keys in sequence. Recording fields and notices are tab-local state; only finalized changes flow through `ui.keymap`
  - The `?` list is read-only (avoid accidental changes while looking up keys). Heading: Shortcut keys; the same two columns as the settings tab. Below: Change in settings (「設定で変える」) (opens settings' shortcut-key tab)
  - While an overlay (settings, `?` list, palette) is open, enable only closing and switching to another overlay (`Esc`, `?`, `⌘K`, including changed keys); do not forward other keys to the view behind it. `Enter` acts on buttons inside the overlay (2026-10-20. Previously `D` changed the background to the diff list, and `Enter` on a button also affected the background view)
- Default keymap. M0's diff list added `1`–`4` (since 2026-09-30, `1` / `2` select entry tabs; `3` / `4` do nothing), `j`/`k`/`↓`/`↑`, `v`, `[`/`]`, `s`/`u`, `gi`, `Esc`, `?` (`packages/web/src/commands/keymap.ts`). `⌘K` is M1 (2026-09-21. Until 2026-10-20, modifier keys were separate in `keymap.ts`'s `MOD` table). `o` (jump to callee) is M2 (2026-09-25). `→` / `←` / `m` / `f` and `Enter` dispatch are for courses (2026-09-30). `Shift+M` (enlarge map), plus enabling `m` / `f` / `s` / `u` on the guidance view, were added 2026-10-11. `b` (collapse left guidance) was added 2026-10-12. `r` / `Shift+R` (unchanged-counterpart drawer) were also added 2026-10-11. `d` (to diff list) was in the same PR as wrap-up (2026-10-03). `q` (Ask the guide) was added 2026-10-08. `⌘F` / `Ctrl+F` (search within diffs; only views with a diff column) were added 2026-10-27

| Key | Action |
|---|---|
| `1` / `2` | Entry tabs: Map / Explain (only in entry) |
| `j` / `k`, `↓` / `↑` | Next / previous hunk (next / previous stop in courses, without marking No issues). Next / previous counterpart while the unchanged-counterpart drawer is open |
| `v` | Viewed (toggle) |
| `[` / `]` | Previous / next file (diff list) |
| `Enter` | Entry: enter the selected course (next course if none selected; wrap-up if all complete). By purpose: toggle follow-on group. Course: proceed (No issues · Next at a stop; complete at the recap). Wrap-up: Approve on GitHub (opens PR page) |
| `d` | Go to diff list (show `D` on the top-band button) |
| `→` / `←` | Course: proceed / back. On the home map tab: next / previous step of the flow (2026-11-05; with a course selected, only that course's steps) |
| `m` | Course stop: open / close map drawer |
| `Shift+M` | Course stop: enlarge map / restore size (open enlarged if closed) |
| `r` | Course stop: open / close the current stop's unchanged counterparts from the right (press the No diff box on the map) |
| `Shift+R` | Enlarge unchanged-counterpart drawer / restore size (open enlarged if closed in a course) |
| `f` | Course stop: full file / context only. Diff list and by purpose preview: show all / near changes only (2026-10-13. Previously courses only) |
| `b` | Course stop: collapse / show the right guidance column (2026-10-12. Left guidance column until 2026-10-19) |
| `Esc` | Close overlays (including the Ask the guide drawer), then filters, finally return to entry (3.0). Close the unchanged-counterpart drawer first in every view. In courses: map (drawer/enlarged) → peek → entry. In entry, deselect the course |
| `o` | Jump to callee (course stops. 3.6). Do not use vim's `gd` (2026-09-25) |
| `q` | Ask the guide (focus the right-column input at course stops; toggle the drawer in other views. 3.10) |
| `gi` | Open in IDEA |
| `s` / `u` | Side-by-side / unified |
| `⌘K` | Command palette |
| `?` | Shortcut-key list (read-only; change in settings) |

- Do not separate selection and focus. Focus is always in one place, visible through an accent-colored ring
- Repeated actions (repeated `j` presses to skim hunks) must work naturally. This is what "flowing" means

### 18.3 Motion principles
- Use animation only to "explain a state change." No decoration
  - Select a How → unrelated hunks fade (120ms)
  - Recognize the same hunk across views → the selected hunk moves smoothly with the View Transitions API (FLIP where unavailable). Add `view-transition-name: hunk-<id>` to hunk elements
  - Viewed → green dot appears and meter counts up (160ms)
  - Next in a course → ring around the current map node moves (180ms ease-out). Code switches immediately (no wait)
  - **Switch Hows on the map → one 1.0-second timeline (ease-in-out)** (approved 2026-09-24. Only this item is outside the 100–200ms range below). Remaining boxes, edges (each point), and band lines slide from their positions; disappearing elements fade in place within the first 40%; appearing elements fade in during the last 40%. Diagram size (viewBox and width/height) moves on the same timeline; keep zoom fixed (camera movement obscures relative motion). When shrinking, wait until disappearing elements finish fading. If another How is pressed mid-animation, continue from current visible positions. Implemented in `packages/web/src/map/tween.ts` and `draw.ts` (write attributes every frame outside React)
  - Expand follow-on group → height transition (150ms)
  - **Next step of the flow on the home map → a ball runs along the current step's line for 0.76 s (ease-out); after it arrives the box gets a frame and the label appears** (2026-11-05. flow5 mockup. Outside the range like the 1.0-second item above). Returns run from the callee back to the caller. The speed slider divides the time (0.38 s at 2×). Playback waits 1.86 s divided by the speed before the next step. With reduced motion the ball does not run and sits at the end of the line (playback keeps the same wait)
- Duration 100–200ms, ease-out. Repeated presses skip ongoing animation to the final state
- Respect `prefers-reduced-motion`
- Synchronize diff list file-list and diff scrolling (as in IDEA)

### 18.4 Performance requirements to remove friction
- Startup: show cached Analysis within 200ms. Analyze in the background and replace on completion (show previous results first instead of a skeleton). 2026-10-13: when reanalyzing from the UI, keep the previous version visible; on completion, replace through Switch in the single line below the band (no automatic replacement. Decided by comparing prototypes. 3.0)
- Virtualize long lists (assume 1,000 hunks and 10,000-line diffs)
- Complete tokenization and side-by-side alignment in Stage 1; UI only renders
- Split rendering for operations that cannot meet 16ms from input to display (such as switching huge diffs to side-by-side)
- Save state immediately on every action. Review state survives crashes

### 18.5 Impact on M0
- Add the command layer and keymap in M0. Retrofitting leads to a mouse-centric UI
- Use `hunk-<id>` for hunk element identifiers from the start, assuming shared elements in View Transitions
- Add the virtual list in M0's diff list (replacing it later changes behavior)
- Enforce the rule against calling Node APIs from the UI with lint (prohibit direct imports of `node:*` and `packages/core` from `packages/web`). Use `import type` from `packages/model` for types
- 2026-09-16: the four points above are implemented in the diff list. Virtual list uses `@tanstack/react-virtual` (virtualize all hunks of the selected file as one sequence of "header rows + diff rows"). Side-by-side alignment (`alignRows`) runs in the UI (18.4's "complete in Stage 1" was judged unnecessary while hunks are small. Move to core if it becomes costly)
- 2026-10-15: the virtual list rounds measured row heights to integers for positioning (`@tanstack/react-virtual`'s default measurement). With code lines at 13px × 1.6 = 20.8px, placing them every 21px left 0.2px gaps and a 1px dark line below wrapped rows (diff list and by purpose preview. Course view uses one table and was unaffected). Set line-number and code-cell line-height to integer 21px across all three views (empty rows also 21px)
- 2026-10-27: the virtual list omits offscreen rows from the DOM, making them unfindable with browser find. Kept the virtual list and added our own search in the diff column ("Search within diffs" in 3.4)
